“Poor old tree!” said Shocky, pointing to a crooked and gnarled elm standing by itself in the middle of a field. For when the elm, naturally the most graceful of trees, once gets a “bad set,” it can grow to be the most deformed. This solitary tree had not a single straight limb.

“Why do you say ‘poor old tree’?” asked Ralph.

===Chinese===
「可怜的老树！」肖基指着田中央一棵孤零零、虬曲多节的榆树说。榆树本是树中最婀娜多姿的一种，可一旦「落了坏地」，也能长成最歪扭的模样。这棵孤零零的树，没有一根枝条是直的。

「你怎么说它『可怜的老树』？」拉尔夫问。

===Original===
“ ’Cause it’s lonesome. All its old friends is dead and chopped down, and there’s their stumps a-standin’ jes like gravestones. It *must* be lonesome. Some folks says it don’t feel, but I think it does. Everything seems to think and feel. See it nodding its head to them other trees in the woods? and a-wantin’ to shake hands! But it can’t move. I think that tree must a growed in the night.”

“Why, Shocky?”

“ ’Cause it’s so crooked,” and Shocky laughed at his own conceit; “must a growed when they was no light so as it could see how to grow.”

===Chinese===
「因为它孤零零的呀。它的老朋友们都死了、都砍倒了，只剩那些树桩立在那儿，跟墓碑一个样。它*准是*孤单的。有人说树没有感觉，我看它有。样样东西好像都会想事儿、都有感觉。你瞧它朝林子那边的树点头呢！还想跟人家握手！可它动不了。我看呀，这棵树准是在黑夜里长出来的。」

「怎么见得，肖基？」

「它长得多歪呀，」肖基为自己的妙想笑了，「长的时候准是没有光，看不见该怎么长。」

===Original===
And then they walked on in silence a minute. Presently Shocky began looking up into Ralph’s eyes to get a smile. “I guess that tree feels just like me. Don’t you?”

“Why, how do you feel?”

===Chinese===
随后两人默默走了一阵。不一会儿，肖基仰起眼睛看拉尔夫的脸色，想讨一个笑。「我猜那棵树心里的滋味跟我一模一样。你说是不是？」

「哦？你是什么滋味？」

===Original===
“Kind o’ bad and lonesome, and like as if I wanted to die, you know. Felt that way ever sence they put my father into the graveyard, and sent my mother to the poorhouse and Hanner to ole Miss Means’s. What kind of a place is a poorhouse? Is it a poorer place than Means’s? I wish I was dead and one of them clouds was a-carryin’ me and Hanner and mother up to where father’s gone, you know! I wonder if God forgets all about poor folks when their father dies and their mother gits into the poorhouse? Do you think He does? Seems so to me. Maybe God lost track of my father when he come away from England and crossed over the sea. Don’t nobody on Flat Creek keer fer God, and I guess God don’t keer fer Flat Creek. But I would, though, ef he’d git my mother out of the poorhouse and git Hanner away from Means’s, and let me kiss my mother every night, you know, and sleep on my Hanner’s arm, jes like I used to afore father died, you see.”

===Chinese===
「就是有点难受、有点孤单，就像想死掉算了，你知道的。打从他们把我爹埋进墓地、把我娘送进贫民院、把汉娜送到老米恩斯太太家那天起，我就一直是这个滋味。贫民院是个什么样的地方？比米恩斯家还穷的地方吗？我恨不得自己死了，有一朵云把我、汉娜和娘一起接到爹去了的地方，你知道的！我不知道，穷人家的爹死了、娘进了贫民院，上帝是不是就把这家人忘得一干二净？你说他会不会？我瞧着像。兴许我爹当年离开英国、漂洋过海过来的时候，上帝就跟丢了他。弗拉特溪上没有一个人把上帝放在心上，我猜上帝也不把弗拉特溪放在心上。可我还是愿意把上帝放在心上，只要他把娘从贫民院里弄出来，把汉娜从米恩斯家弄出来，让我每天夜里亲亲娘，睡在我汉娜的胳膊上，就像爹没死的时候那样，你瞧。」

===Original===
Ralph wanted to speak, but he couldn’t. And so Shocky, with his eyes looking straight ahead, and as if forgetting Ralph’s presence, told over the thoughts that he had often talked over to the fence-rails and the trees. “It was real good in Mr. Pearson to take me, wasn’t it? Else I’d a been bound out tell I was twenty-one, maybe, to some mean man like Ole Means. And I a’n’t but seven. And it would take me fourteen years to git twenty-one, and I never could live with my mother again after Hanner gets done her time. ’Cause, you see, Hanner’ll be through in three more year, and I’ll be ten and able to work, and we’ll git a little place about as big as Granny Sanders’s, and﻿—”

Ralph did not hear another word of what Shocky said that afternoon. For there, right before them, was Granny Sanders’s log-cabin, with its row of lofty sunflower stalks, now dead and dry, in front, with its rainwater barrel by the side of the low door, and its ash-barrel by the fence. In this cabin lived alone the old and shriveled hag whose hideousness gave her a reputation for almost supernatural knowledge. She was at once doctress and newspaper. She collected and disseminated medicinal herbs and personal gossip. She was in every regard indispensable to the intellectual life of the neighborhood. In the matter of her medical skill we cannot express an opinion, for her “yarbs” are not to be found in the pharmacopoeia of science.

What took Ralph’s breath was to find Dr. Small’s fine, faultless horse standing at the door. What did Henry Small want to visit this old quack for?

===Chinese===
拉尔夫想说话，却说不出来。于是肖基眼睛望着正前方，像是忘了拉尔夫就在身边，把那些他常常讲给篱笆桩子和树木听的念头，一一数了出来。「皮尔逊先生肯收留我，真是天大的好心，是不是？不然我兴许要被卖身帮工到二十一岁，给老米恩斯那样刻薄的人干活。我今年才七岁。到二十一岁还得熬十四年；那样的话，等汉娜满期之后，我就再也不能跟娘一块儿过日子了。因为，你瞧，汉娜再有三年就满期，那时我十岁，能干活了，我们就能置一个小地方，跟桑德斯婆婆（Granny Sanders）家差不多大，还有——」

那天下午肖基后来还说了些什么，拉尔夫一个字也没听进去。因为就在他们眼前，立着桑德斯婆婆的圆木小屋：屋前立着一排高高的向日葵秆，如今枯死了；矮门边放着一口接雨水的大桶，篱笆旁是一炉灰桶。屋里独住着那个干瘪的老妖婆，她那副丑相，倒让她得了个近乎通晓神鬼的名声。她一身兼作医生和报纸：采集并散布草药，也采集并散布家长里短。无论从哪方面说，她都是这一带精神生活里少不了的人物。至于她的医道，我们不便置评，因为她那些「草头药」（yarbs），在科学的药典里是查不到的。

叫拉尔夫倒吸一口凉气的是，斯莫尔医生那匹漂亮无瑕的马正拴在门前。亨利·斯莫尔（Henry Small）来看这个老巫婆做什么？

## XXXIV “How It Came Out” / 「结局如何」

===Original===
We are all children in reading stories. We want more than all else to know how it all came out at the end, and, if our taste is not perverted, we like it to come out well. For my part, ever since I began to write this story, I have been anxious to know how it was going to come out.

===Chinese===
读故事的我们，个个都是孩子。我们最想知道的，莫过于结尾一切如何收场；只要趣味没有长歪，我们还巴不得它收个好场。就我而言，打从动笔写这个故事起，我就一直急着想晓得它究竟会怎样收场。

===Original===
Well, there were very few invited. It took place at ten in the morning. The “preacher-in-charge” came, of course. Miss Nancy Sawyer was there. But Ralph’s uncle was away, and Aunt Matilda had a sore throat and couldn’t come. Perhaps the memory of the fact that she had refused Mrs. Thomson, the pauper, a bed for two nights, affected her throat. But Miss Nancy and her sister were there, and the preacher. And that was all, besides the family, and Bud and Martha. Of course Bud and Martha came. And driving Martha to a wedding in a “jumper” was the one opportunity Bud needed. His hands were busy, his big boots were out of sight, and it was so easy to slip from Ralph’s love affair to his own, that Bud somehow, in pulling Martha Hawkins’ shawl about her, stammered out half a proposal, which Martha, generous soul, took for the whole ceremony, and accepted. And Bud was so happy that Ralph guessed from his face and voice that the agony was over, and Bud was betrothed at last to the “gal as was a gal.”

===Chinese===
好在受邀的人极少。婚礼上午十点举行。「主管牧师」当然到了场。南希·索耶小姐（Miss Nancy Sawyer）也在。可拉尔夫的叔叔出了门，玛蒂尔达婶母嗓子疼，来不了。也许是因为记起自己曾两夜不肯给穷人汤姆森太太（Mrs. Thomson）一张床，才闹的嗓子疼。但南希小姐和她妹妹来了，还有牧师。除了家里人，到场的就只有巴德和玛莎，一共这些。巴德和玛莎当然要来。赶着「蹦蹦车」（jumper，一种单马小雪橇）送玛莎去参加婚礼，正是巴德千载难逢的机会：两只手有活可干，一双大靴子藏在车下看不见，从拉尔夫的亲事溜达进自己的亲事又毫不费事。于是不知怎么的，巴德在替玛莎·霍金斯掖披肩的当口，结结巴巴地求了半回婚——玛莎心肠宽厚，把这半回当作了一整场仪式，应允了他。巴德那份欢喜，让拉尔夫一看他的脸、一听他的嗓音就明白：煎熬过去了，巴德总算和他那位「姑娘中的姑娘」订了婚。

===Original===
And after Ralph and Hannah were married﻿—there was no trip, Ralph only changed his boarding-place and became head of the house at Mrs. Thomson’s thereafter﻿—after it was all over, Bud came to Mr. Hartsook, and, snickering just a little, said as how as him and Martha had fixed it all up, and now they wanted to ax his advice; and Martha proud but blushing, came up and nodded assent. Bud said as how as he hadn’t got no book-larnin’ nor nothin’, and as how as he wanted to be somethin’, and put in his best licks fer Him, you know. And that Marthy, she was of the same way of thinkin’, and that was a blessin’. And the Squire was a-goin’ to marry agin’, and Marthy would ruther vacate. And his mother and Mirandy was sech as he wouldn’t take no wife to. And he thought as how Mr. Hartsook might think of some way or some place where he and Marthy mout make a livin’ fer the present, and put in their best licks fer Him, you know.

===Chinese===
拉尔夫和汉娜完婚之后——没有外出旅行，拉尔夫只是换了个搭伙的地方，从此做了汤姆森太太家的当家人——一切落定之后，巴德来到哈茨克先生跟前，嘿嘿憨笑了两声，说他和玛莎已经把事儿全都定妥了，如今想求他指点指点；玛莎又骄傲又脸红，走上前来点了点头。巴德说，他没啥书本学问，啥也不是，可他想做个有出息的人，想为他老人家卖尽力气，你懂的。玛莎呢，她想的是同一条道儿，这是天大的福气。乡绅又要续弦了，玛莎情愿腾屋子。他娘和米兰迪那种人，他可不能把媳妇往那家里领。他想，哈茨克先生兴许能想出个什么法子、寻个什么地方，让他和玛莎眼下能谋个生计，好为他老人家卖尽力气，你懂的。

===Original===
Ralph thought a moment. He was about to make an allusion to Hercules and the Augean stables, but he remembered that Bud would not understand it, though it might remind Martha of something she had seen at the East, the time she was to Bosting.

“Bud, my dear friend,” said Ralph, “it looks a little hard to ask you to take a new wife”﻿—here Bud looked admiringly at Martha﻿—“to the poorhouse. But I don’t know anywhere where you can do so much good for Christ as by taking charge of that place, and I can get the appointment for you. The new commissioners want just such a man.”

“What d’ye say, Marthy?” said Bud.

“Why, somebody ought to do for the poor, and I should like to do it.”

And so Hercules cleaned the Augean stables.

===Chinese===
拉尔夫想了一会儿。他正想引用赫拉克勒斯（Hercules）清扫奥吉亚斯牛圈（Augean stables）的典故，转念想起巴德听不懂，虽然这话没准儿能让玛莎想起她在东部、她去波士顿（Bosting）那阵子见过的什么场面。

「巴德，我的好朋友，」拉尔夫说，「要你带着一位新媳妇——」说到这里，巴德爱慕地望了望玛莎——「去贫民院，这话是有点难出口。可我不知道还有哪个地方，能像主管那个院子一样，让你为基督做这么多好事。而且这份差事我能替你谋来。新上任的委员们正要找这样一个人。」

「你说咋样，玛莎？」巴德说。

「咋说呢，穷人总得有人照应，我倒愿意干这个。」

于是，赫拉克勒斯清扫了奥吉亚斯牛圈。

===Original===
And so my humble, homely Hoosier story of twenty years ago28 draws to a close, and not without regret I take leave of Ralph and Hannah; and Shocky, and Bud, and Martha, and Miss Nancy, and of my readers.

---

P.S.﻿—A copy of the Lewisburg *Jeffersonian* came into my hands today, and I see by its columns that Ralph Hartsook is principal of the Lewisburg Academy. It took me some time, however, to make out that the sheriff of the county, Mr. Israel W. Means, was none other than my old friend Bud, of the Church of the Best Licks. I was almost as much puzzled over his name as I was when I saw an article in a city paper, by Prof. W. J. Thomson, on Poorhouses. I should not have recognized the writer as Shocky, had I not known that Shocky has given his spare time to making outcasts feel that God has not forgot.

===Chinese===
于是，我这个写二十年前印第安纳乡野的质朴无华的小故事28 便要收尾了。向拉尔夫和汉娜道别，向肖基、巴德、玛莎、南希小姐道别，也向我的读者们道别，心中不无眷恋。

---

又及——今天一份刘易斯堡《杰斐逊报》（*Jeffersonian*）到了我手里，我从报上得知，拉尔夫·哈茨克如今是刘易斯堡学堂的校长。不过，我倒是花了好一阵子才认出来，本县治安官伊斯拉尔·W·米恩斯（Israel W. Means）先生，不是别人，正是我的老朋友巴德——「最尽力教会」（the Church of the Best Licks）的巴德。看到他的名字时我那份纳闷，几乎赶上看城里报纸那篇 W·J·汤姆森（W. J. Thomson）教授写的《论贫民院》时一样。若不是知道肖基素来把闲暇功夫都用来让弃儿们感到上帝没有忘掉，我是认不出执笔人来就是肖基的。

## XXXIII Into the Light / 走进光亮里

===Original===
For two weeks longer Ralph taught at the Flat Creek schoolhouse. He was everybody’s hero. And he was Bud’s idol. He did what he could to get Bud and Martha together, and though Bud always “saw her safe home” after this, and called on her every Sunday evening, yet, to save his life, he could not forget his big fists and his big feet long enough to say what he most wanted to say, and what Martha most wanted him to say.

At the end of two weeks Ralph found himself exceedingly weary of Flat Creek, and exceedingly glad to hear from Mr. Means that the school-money had “gin out.” It gave him a good excuse to return to Lewisburg, where his heart and his treasure were. A certain sense of delicacy had kept him from writing to Hannah just yet.

When he got to Lewisburg he had good news. His uncle, ashamed of his previous neglect, and perhaps with an eye to his nephew’s growing popularity, had got him the charge of the grammar department in the new graded school in the village. So he quietly arranged to board at a boardinghouse. His aunt could not have him about, of which fact he was very glad. She could not but feel, she said, that he might have taken better care of Walter than he did, when they were only four miles apart.

===Chinese===
此后拉尔夫又在弗拉特溪校舍教了两个星期。他是人人眼中的英雄，更是巴德心里的偶像。他尽力撮合巴德和玛莎；打这以后，巴德每次都要「把她平安送到家」，每个礼拜天晚上都去她家坐坐，可他死活没法把那双大拳头和两只大脚忘记得足够久，久到能说出那句他最想说、玛莎最想听的话。

两个星期一到头，拉尔夫发现自己对弗拉特溪厌烦透了，而听米恩斯先生说办学款子已经「使见了底」，又欢喜透了。这给了他一个回刘易斯堡的好借口——他的心和心尖上的人都在那儿。出于某种细腻的顾虑，他一时还没有给汉娜写信。

他一到刘易斯堡就得了好消息。他叔叔先前亏待过他，如今有些惭愧——多半也惦记着侄子日渐响亮的名声——已经替他在村里新办的分级学校里谋得了文法科主管的位子。于是他悄悄安排在一家包伙的寄宿公寓搭伙。婶母容不下他在跟前，这一点他求之不得。婶母说，她总不免觉得，当初两人相距不过四英里，他本可以把沃尔特（Walter）照应得周到些的。

===Original===
He did not hasten to call on Hannah. Why should he? He sent her a message, of no consequence in itself, by Nancy Sawyer. Then he took possession of his school; and then, on the evening of the first day of school, he went, as he had appointed to himself, to see Hannah Thomson.

And she, with some sweet presentiment, had got things ready by fixing up the scantily-furnished room as well as she could. And Miss Nancy Sawyer, who had seen Ralph that afternoon, had guessed that he was going to see Hannah. It’s wonderful how much enjoyment a generous heart can get out of the happiness of others. Is not that what He meant when he said of such as Miss Sawyer that they should have a hundredfold in this life for all their sacrifices? Did not Miss Nancy enjoy a hundred weddings and have the love of five hundred children? And so Miss Nancy just happened over at Mrs. Thomson’s humble home, and, just in the most matter-of-course way, asked that lady and Shocky to come over to her house. Shocky wanted Hannah to come too. But Hannah blushed a little, and said that she would rather not.

===Chinese===
他没有急着去见汉娜。何必急呢？他托南希·索耶（Nancy Sawyer）给她捎了个口信，口信本身无关紧要。随后他到校上任；然后，在开学头一天的晚上，他按照给自己定下的约，去看望汉娜·汤姆森（Hannah Thomson）。

而汉娜，怀着某种甜丝丝的预感，早已把那间陈设寒酸的屋子尽力拾掇停当。南希·索耶小姐那天下午见过拉尔夫，猜到他要去看汉娜。一颗宽厚的心能从别人的幸福里得到多少快乐，真是不可思议。主说到索耶小姐这样的人，为他们所作的牺牲，今世必得百倍报偿——他指的岂不正是这个意思吗？南希小姐不是享受过一百场婚礼、得过五百个孩子的爱吗？于是南希小姐「恰好」踱到汤姆森太太那寒素的家，用一种再自然不过的口气，请太太和肖基上她家去坐坐。肖基想拉汉娜一块儿去。可汉娜微微红了脸，说她还是不去的好。

===Original===
And when she was left alone, Hannah fixed her hair two or three times, and swept the hearth, and moved the chairs first one way and then another, and did a good many other needless things. Needless: for a lover, if he be a lover, does not see furniture or dress.

And then she sat down by the fire, and tried to sew, and tried to look unconcerned, and tried to feel unconcerned, and tried not to expect anybody, and tried to make her heart keep still. And tried in vain. For a gentle rap at the door sent her pulse up twenty beats a minute and made her face burn. And Hartsook was for the first time, abashed in the presence of Hannah. For the oppressed girl had, in two weeks, blossomed out into the full-blown woman.

And Ralph sat down by the fire, and talked of his school and her school, and everything else but what he wanted to talk about. And then the conversation drifted back to Flat Creek, and to the walk through the pasture, and to the box-elder tree, and to the painful talk in the lane. And Hannah begged to be forgiven, and Ralph laughed at the idea that she had done anything wrong. And she praised his goodness to Shocky, and he drew her little note out of﻿—But I agreed not tell you where he kept it. And then she blushed, and he told how the note had sustained him, and how her white face kept up his courage in his flight down the bed of Clifty Creek. And he sat a little nearer, to show her the note that he had carried in his bosom﻿—I have told it! And﻿—but I must not proceed. A love-scene, ever so beautiful in itself, will not bear telling. And so I shall leave a little gap just here, which you may fill up as you please.﻿ ﻿… Somehow, they never knew how, they got to talking about the future instead of the past, after that, and to planning their two lives as one life. And﻿ ﻿… And when Miss Nancy and Mrs. Thomson returned later in the evening, Ralph was standing by the mantelpiece, but Shocky noticed that his chair was close to Hannah’s. And good Miss Nancy Sawyer looked in Hannah’s face and was happy.

===Chinese===
屋里只剩她一个人的时候，汉娜把头发理了两三遍，扫了扫炉台，把椅子先挪到这边、又挪到那边，还做了好些个多余的事。多余——因为一个真正堕入情网的人，是看不见家具也看不见衣裳的。

随后她在炉火边坐下，想做点针线，想装出若无其事的样子，想真的若无其事，想不去盼任何人来，想让心安分下来。全都是徒劳。门上轻轻一叩，就让她的脉搏一分钟多跳了二十下，脸上烧了起来。而哈茨克在汉娜面前，头一回发窘了。因为这个受尽压榨的姑娘，两星期之间，已绽放成一个丰盈成熟的女人。

拉尔夫在炉火边坐下，谈起他的学校、她的学校，什么都谈，唯独不谈他最想谈的。随后话头又漂回弗拉特溪，漂到穿过牧场的那一路，漂到那棵羽叶槭树下，漂到小巷里那番叫人难受的谈话。汉娜求他原谅，拉尔夫笑道，她哪里有什么错处。她夸他对肖基的好，他便把她的那张小字条从——不行，我答应过不告诉你们他把字条收在哪儿的。她红了脸；他讲那张字条怎样支撑了他，讲他顺着克利夫蒂溪（Clifty Creek）的河床奔逃时，她那张苍白的脸怎样给他壮着胆。他坐得挨近了些，好让她看看他贴胸揣着的字条——坏了，我说出来了！还有——可我不能再往下讲了。一场情爱场面，本身再美，也是经不起转述的。所以我就在这儿留一小段空白，随你们自己去填补。……不知怎么的——他们自己也始终不明白是怎么会的——从那以后，两人谈的不再是过去，而是将来，是把两条命规划成一条命。还有……等南希小姐和汤姆森太太晚上稍晚些回来时，拉尔夫正站在壁炉台旁，可肖基注意到，他的椅子紧挨着汉娜的。好心的南希·索耶小姐朝汉娜脸上望了望，心里欢喜。

## III Mirandy, Hank, and Shocky / 米兰迪、汉克和肖基

===Original===
Mirandy had nothing but contempt for the new master until he developed the bulldog in his character. Mirandy fell in love with the bulldog. Like many other girls of her class, she was greatly enamored with the “subjection of women,” and she stood ready to fall in love with any man strong enough to be her master. Much has been said of the strong-minded woman. I offer this psychological remark as a contribution to the natural history of the weak-minded woman.

It was at the close of that very second day on which Ralph had achieved his first victory over the school, and in which Mirandy had been seized with her desperate passion for him, that she told him about it. Not in words. We do not allow *that* in the most civilized countries, and still less would it be tolerated in Hoopole County. But Mirandy told the master the fact that she was in love with him, though no word passed her lips. She walked by him from school. She cast at him what are commonly called sheep’s-eyes. Ralph thought them more like calf’s eyes. She changed the whole tone of her voice. She whined ordinarily. Now she whimpered. And so by ogling him, by blushing at him, by tittering at him, by giggling at him, by snickering at him, by simpering at him, by making herself tenfold more a fool even than nature had made her, she managed to convey to the dismayed soul of the young teacher the frightful intelligence that he was loved by the richest, the ugliest, the silliest, the coarsest, and the most entirely contemptible girl in Flat Creek district.

===Chinese===
在新老师露出性格里那条斗牛犬之前，米兰迪对他只有轻蔑。老师一露出斗牛犬，米兰迪就爱上了这条斗牛犬。和她那一类的许多姑娘一样，她一心迷恋「女人的臣服」，随便哪个男人只要强悍得能做她的主，她随时准备爱上他。关于心智强悍的女人，人们已经谈得够多。我谨献上这条心理学的观察，算是对心智柔弱的女人之自然史的一点小小贡献。

正是拉尔夫初胜全校的那第二天傍晚——米兰迪那场不要命的热恋也正是这天发作的——她把这事告诉了他。不是用话说的。在最文明的国家里我们也不兴*那个*，在胡普尔县（Hoopole County）就更不能容许了。可米兰迪确实让老师知道了她爱他，虽然嘴唇上没吐一个字。放学她挨着他走。朝他抛那种俗话叫「媚眼」的眼风。拉尔夫觉得那更像牛犊的眼。她把整个嗓音的调门都换了。平常她是嗲声嗲气，如今是呜咽缠绵。就这样，又是媚眼，又是红脸，又是吃吃笑，又是咯咯笑，又是嘿然窃笑，又是傻笑不迭，把自己弄得比天生造就的还蠢上十倍，总算把这吓人的消息传进了年轻教师惊惶的灵魂：爱上他的，是弗拉特溪学区最有钱、最丑、最蠢、最粗、也最叫人瞧不上眼的姑娘。

===Original===
Ralph sat by the fire the next morning trying to read a few minutes before school-time, while the boys were doing the chores and the bound girl was milking the cows, with no one in the room but the old woman. She was generally as silent as Bud, but now she seemed for some unaccountable reason disposed to talk. She had sat down on the broad hearth to have her usual morning smoke; the poplar table, adorned by no cloth, stood in the middle of the floor; the unwashed blue teacups sat in the unwashed blue saucers; the unwashed blue plates kept company with the begrimed blue pitcher. The dirty skillets by the fire were kept in countenance by the dirtier pots, and the ashes were drifted and strewn over the hearthstones in a most picturesque way.

“You see,” said the old woman, knocking the residuum from her cob pipe, and chafing some dry leaf between her withered hands preparatory to filling it again, “you see, Mr. Hartsook, my ole man’s purty well along in the world. He’s got a right smart lot of this world’s plunder,10 one way and another.” And while she stuffed the tobacco into her pipe Ralph wondered why she should mention it to him. “You see, we moved in here nigh upon twenty-five years ago. ’Twas when my Jack, him as died afore Bud was born, was a baby. Bud’ll be twenty-one the fif’ of next June.”

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，拉尔夫坐在炉边，想在上课前读几分钟书。男孩子们在外面做杂活，帮工姑娘在挤牛奶，屋里只有那老太太一个人。她平日和巴德一样闷声不响，此刻却不知为什么来了谈话的兴致。她已在宽宽的炉台上坐定，抽她每天早晨的那一袋烟；白木桌子上不铺桌布，立在屋子正中；没洗的蓝茶杯坐在没洗的蓝茶碟里；没洗的蓝盘子陪着油腻发黑的蓝水壶。炉边脏兮兮的平底锅，靠更脏的汤锅撑着场面；炉灰在炉板上堆积飘洒，别有一种生动的景致。

「你瞧，」老太太说着，把玉米芯烟斗里的残灰磕出来，又用两只干瘪的手搓碎几片干烟叶，预备再装一斗，「你瞧，哈茨克先生，俺们老头子在世上混得挺不赖。七凑八凑，他攒下好一大票世上的浮财。10」她往斗里塞烟叶的工夫，拉尔夫纳闷她为什么要对自己说这个。「你瞧，俺们搬进这儿来，都快二十五年了。那时俺的杰克——就是巴德出生前死去的那个——还是个吃奶的娃娃。到下个月六月十五，巴德就满二十一了。」

===Original===
Here Mrs. Means stopped to rake a live coal out of the fire with her skinny finger, and then to carry it in her skinny palm to the bowl﻿—or to the *hole*﻿—of her cob pipe. When she got the smoke a-going, she proceeded:

“You see, this yere bottom land was all Congress land11 in them there days, and it sold for a dollar and a quarter, and I says to my ole man, ‘Jack,’ says I, ‘Jack, do you git a plenty while you’re a-gittin’. Git a plenty while you’re a-gittin’,’ says I, ‘fer ’twon’t never be no cheaper’n ’tis now,’ and it ha’n’t been; I knowed ’twouldn’t,” and Mrs. Means took the pipe from her mouth to indulge in a good chuckle at the thought of her financial shrewdness. “ ‘Git a plenty while you’re a-gittin’ says I. I could see, you know, they was a powerful sight of money in Congress land. That’s what made me say, ‘Git a plenty while you’re a-gittin’.’ And Jack, he’s wuth lots and gobs of money, all made out of Congress land. Jack didn’t git rich by hard work. Bless you, no! Not him. That a’n’t his way. Hard work a’n’t, you know. ’Twas that air six hundred dollars he got along of me, all salted down into Flat Crick bottoms at a dollar and a quarter a’ acre, and ’twas my sayin’ ‘Git a plenty while you’re a-gittin’ ’ as done it.” And here the old ogre laughed, or grinned horribly, at Ralph, showing her few straggling, discolored teeth.

===Chinese===
说到这儿，米恩斯太太停下来，用皮包骨的手指从火里扒出一块红炭，再摊在皮包骨的手心里，端到烟斗的斗——或者不如说那个*窟窿*——跟前。等烟抽得顺了，她接着说：

「你瞧，这片河滩地那阵子全是国会公地，11 一亩卖一块两毛五。俺就跟俺们老头子说：『杰克，』俺说，『杰克，趁能捞的时候可劲儿捞。趁能捞的时候可劲儿捞，』俺说，『往后再也赶不上现在这么便宜了。』往后也真没便宜过；俺就知道便宜不了。」想到自己理财的那份精明，米恩斯太太把烟斗从嘴上拿下来，好一阵得意地咯咯笑。「『趁能捞的时候可劲儿捞，』俺说。俺看得明白，你知道，这国会公地里头藏着老鼻子钱了。俺才说：『趁能捞的时候可劲儿捞。』如今杰克有的是钱，大把大把的，全是从国会公地上挣的。杰克可不是靠卖苦力发的家。老天爷，可不是！他才不呢。那不是他的路数。卖苦力可不行，你懂的。还不是靠他跟着俺弄来的那六百块，一亩一块两毛五，全撒进弗拉特溪的河滩地里，再加上俺那句『趁能捞的时候可劲儿捞』，才成的这事。」说到这儿，老妖婆冲着拉尔夫笑了，或者说龇牙咧嘴地狞笑了一通，露出那几颗稀稀拉拉的黄牙。

===Original===
Then she got up and knocked the ashes out of her pipe, and laid the pipe away and walked round in front of Ralph. After adjusting the chunks12 so that the fire would burn, she turned her yellow face toward Ralph, and scanning him closely came out with the climax of her speech in the remark: “You see as how, Mr. Hartsook, the man what gits my Mirandy’ll do well. Flat Crick land’s wuth nigh upon a hundred a’ acre.”

This gentle hint came near knocking Ralph down. Had Flat Creek land been worth a hundred times a hundred dollars an acre, and had he owned five hundred times Means’s five hundred acres, he would have given it all just at that moment to have annihilated the whole tribe of Meanses. Except Bud. Bud was a giant, but a good-natured one. He thought he would except Bud from the general destruction. As for the rest, he mentally pictured to himself the pleasure of attending their funerals. There was one thought, however, between him and despair. He felt confident that the cordiality, the intensity, and the persistency of his dislike of Sis Means were such that he should never inherit a foot of the Flat Creek bottoms.

===Chinese===
随后她站起来，磕掉烟斗里的灰，把烟斗搁到一边，绕到拉尔夫跟前。她把柴火棍拨弄好12，让火能烧起来，然后转向拉尔夫，把那张黄脸凑近了，上上下下地打量他，终于抖出了这番话的压轴一句：「你瞧是这么回事，哈茨克先生，娶了俺米兰迪的人，日子差不了。弗拉特溪的地，一亩快值一百块了。」

这一声温柔的提点，差一点把拉尔夫放倒在地。哪怕弗拉特溪的地一亩值一百个一百块，哪怕他坐拥米恩斯家五百亩的五百倍，那一刻他也情愿全部拿出去，只求把米恩斯这一族连根抹掉。巴德除外。巴德是个巨人，却是个好脾气的巨人。他想，这满门抄斩里得把巴德饶过去。至于其余的，他在心里描画着参加他们葬礼的快意。不过，横在他和绝望之间还有一念：他确信，自己对米家大小姐（Sis Means，即米兰迪）的厌恶是那样由衷、那样强烈、那样持久，他这辈子休想继承到弗拉特溪河滩的一寸土。

===Original===
But what about Bud? What if he joined the conspiracy to marry him to this weak-eyed, weak-headed wood-nymph, or backwoods nymph?

If Ralph felt it a misfortune to be loved by Mirandy Means, he found himself almost equally unfortunate in having incurred the hatred of the meanest boy in school. “Hank” Banta, low-browed, smirky, and crafty, was the first sufferer by Ralph’s determination to use corporal punishment, and so Henry Banta, who was a compound of deceit and resentment, never lost an opportunity to annoy the young schoolmaster, who was obliged to live perpetually on his guard against his tricks.

===Chinese===
可巴德呢？万一他也掺和进这门亲事，要把他拴给这个眼皮子软、脑瓜子也软的林中仙子——或者说荒林仙子——呢？

如果说被米兰迪·米恩斯爱上是一场不幸，那么惹上全校最卑劣的男孩的仇恨，也几乎是同样地不幸。「汉克」·班塔（“Hank” Banta），低额头，一脸假笑，一肚子奸猾，是拉尔夫决意动用体罚后的第一个受害者。于是这位由狡诈和怨恨合成的亨利·班塔，从不放过任何一个作弄年轻教师的机会，害得教师不得不时时刻刻提防着他的诡计。

===Original===
One morning, as Ralph walked toward the schoolhouse, he met little Shocky. What the boy’s first name or last name was the teacher did not know. He had given his name as Shocky, and all the teacher knew was that he was commonly called Shocky, that he was an orphan, that he lived with a family named Pearson over in Rocky Hollow, and that he was the most faithful and affectionate child in the school. On this morning that I speak of, Ralph had walked toward the school early to avoid the company of Mirandy. But not caring to sustain his dignity longer than was necessary, he loitered along the road, admiring the trunks of the maples, and picking up a beechnut now and then. Just as he was about to go on toward the school, he caught sight of little Shocky running swiftly toward him, but looking from side to side, as if afraid of being seen.

“Well, Shocky, what is it?” and Ralph put his hand kindly on the great bushy head of white hair from which came Shocky’s nickname. Shocky had to pant a minute.

===Chinese===
一天早晨，拉尔夫正朝校舍走去，迎面碰见了小肖基。这孩子姓什么、叫什么，老师并不知道。报名的时候他说自己叫肖基，老师所知道的只是：大家平常都管他叫肖基；他是个孤儿；住在乱石谷那边一户姓皮尔逊的人家里；而且是全校最实心眼、最重情的孩子。在我所说的这个早晨，拉尔夫早早动身去学校，为的是躲开米兰迪。但架子也不必端得比需要的更久，他便在路上磨磨蹭蹭，看看枫树的树干，不时弯腰拾一颗山毛榉的果子。他正要继续朝学校走，一眼瞥见小肖基飞快地朝他跑来，一边跑一边左右张望，像是怕被人看见。

「怎么啦，肖基？」拉尔夫把手亲切地放在那头蓬蓬的白发上——「肖基」（Shocky，「乱发蓬松」的意思）这个诨名正是打这头发上来的。肖基喘了一阵才缓过气来。

===Original===
“Why, Mr. Hartsook,” he gasped, scratching his head, “they’s a pond down under the schoolhouse,” and here Shocky’s breath gave out entirely for a minute.

“Yes, Shocky, I know that. What about it? The trustees haven’t come to fill it up, have they?”

“Oh! no, sir; but Hank Banta, you know﻿—” and Shocky took another breathing spell, standing as close to Ralph as he could, for poor Shocky got all his sunshine from the master’s presence.

“Has Henry fallen in and got a ducking, Shocky?”

“Oh! no, sir; he wants to git you in, you see.”

“Well, I won’t go in, though, Shocky.”

“But, you see, he’s been and gone and pulled back the board that you have to step on to git ahind your desk; he’s been and gone and pulled back the board so as you can’t help a-tippin’ it up, and a-sowsin’ right in ef you step there.”

===Chinese===
「是这么回事，哈茨克先生，」他挠着头，喘着气说，「校舍底下有个水池，」说到这儿肖基的气又彻底接不上了。

「我知道，肖基。那怎么了？校董们还没来把它填上吧？」

「噢！不是，先生；是汉克·班塔，你知道——」肖基又歇了一口气，使劲往拉尔夫身边靠——可怜的肖基，他所有的阳光都是从老师身上得来的。

「是亨利掉进去洗了个澡吗，肖基？」

「噢！不是，先生；是他想让您掉进去，您明白吧。」

「哦，那我偏不进去，肖基。」

「可是您瞧，他把您踩着绕到讲台后头去的那块板子，偷偷挪得翘起来了；他把板子挪好了，您一踩上去，板子准得一翘，把您整个儿栽进水里。」

===Original===
“And so you came to tell me.” There was a huskiness in Ralph’s voice. He had, then, one friend in Flat Creek district﻿—poor little Shocky. He put his arm around Shocky just a moment, and then told him to hasten across to the other road, so as to come back to the schoolhouse in a direction at right angles to the master’s approach. But the caution was not needed. Shocky had taken care to leave in that way, and was altogether too cunning to be seen coming down the road with Mr. Hartsook. But after he got over the fence to go through the “sugar camp” (or sugar *orchard*, as they say at the East), he stopped and turned back once or twice, just to catch one more smile from Ralph. And then he hied away through the tall trees, a very happy boy, kicking and ploughing the brown leaves before him in his perfect delight, saying over and over again: “How he looked at me! how he did look!” And when Ralph came up to the schoolhouse door, there was Shocky sauntering along from the other direction, throwing bits of limestone at fence rails, and smiling still clear down to his shoes at thought of the master’s kind words.

===Chinese===
「所以你就跑来告诉我了。」拉尔夫的嗓音有些发哑。这么说，他在弗拉特溪学区还有一个朋友——可怜的小肖基。他用胳膊搂了肖基一下，随即叫他赶紧抄到另一条路上去，好在老师走近时从与老师来路成直角的方向回到校舍。可这叮嘱是多余的。肖基早想到要从那边离开，也精得很，才不肯让人瞧见他和哈茨克先生一路走来。翻过篱笆走进「熬糖林」（sugar camp，槭树林；用东部人的说法，叫「糖园」〔sugar orchard〕）之后，他还停住脚回了一两次头，只为再多接一个拉尔夫的笑。随后他匆匆跑进高高的树林，一个快活透顶的孩子，一路把满地褐色的落叶踢得四处翻飞，嘴里一遍又一遍地念叨：「他那么看我！他真那么看我！」等拉尔夫走到校舍门口，肖基正从另一个方向溜溜达达地过来，拿石灰岩碎块投篱笆桩子玩，一想起老师的好话，笑得连鞋帮子里都是笑意。

===Original===
“What a quare boy Shocky is!” remarked Betsey Short, with a giggle. “He just likes to wander round alone. I see him a-comin’ out of the sugar camp just now. He’s been in there half an hour.” And Betsey giggled again; for Betsey Short could giggle on slighter provocation than any other girl on Flat Creek.

When Ralph Hartsook, with the quiet, dogged tread that he was cultivating, walked into the schoolroom, he took great care not to seem to see the trap set for him; but he carelessly stepped over the board that had been so nicely adjusted. The boys who were Hank’s confidants in the plot were very busy over their slates, and took pains not to show their disappointment.

The morning session wore on without incident. Ralph several times caught two people looking at him. One was Mirandy. Her weak and watery eyes stole loving glances over the top of her spelling-book, which she would not study. Her looks made Ralph’s spirits sink to forty below zero, and congeal.

But on one of the backless little benches that sat in the middle of the schoolroom was little Shocky, who also cast many love glances at the young master; glances as grateful to his heart as Mirandy’s ogling﻿—he was tempted to call it ogring﻿—was hateful.

===Chinese===
「肖基这孩子多古怪呀！」贝琪·肖特（Betsey Short）吃吃笑着搭话。「他就是爱一个人瞎转悠。我刚才瞅见他从熬糖林里出来。他在里头待了半个钟头咧。」贝琪又吃吃笑起来；因为在弗拉特溪，再没有哪个姑娘比贝琪·肖特更容易被逗笑了。

拉尔夫·哈茨克迈着他正刻意修炼的那种沉稳执拗的步子走进教室时，分外小心地装作没看见那为他设下的圈套；他若无其事地跨过了那块调配得恰到好处的木板。参与汉克密谋的那几个男孩都埋头死磕石板，还留神着不露出失望的神色。

上午的课平平安安地挨过去。拉尔夫好几次逮见有两双眼睛在看他。一双是米兰迪的。她那水汪汪的软眼睛从拼字课本顶上偷递过来脉脉的情眼——课本她是不肯念的。她这些眼色，让拉尔夫的心气沉到零下四十度，还冻上了。

可是在教室正中那些没有靠背的小长凳上，坐着小肖基，他也朝年轻老师投去许多爱慕的目光；这些目光让他心里多么受用，米兰迪的媚眼——他很想管它叫「妖眼」——就有多么可憎。

===Original===
“Look at Shocky,” giggled Betsey Short, behind her slate. “He looks as if he was a-goin’ to eat the master up, body and soul.”

And so the forenoon wore on as usual, and those who laid the trap had forgotten it, themselves. The morning session was drawing to a close. The fire in the great old fireplace had burnt low. The flames, which seemed to Shocky to be angels, had disappeared, and now the bright coals, which had played the part of men and women and houses in Shocky’s fancy, had taken on a white and downy covering of ashes, and the great half-burnt backlog lay there smouldering like a giant asleep in a snowdrift. Shocky longed to wake him up.

===Chinese===
「瞧肖基，」贝琪·肖特躲在石板后面吃吃笑。「他那样子，像要把老师连皮带骨、连魂儿一块儿吞了。」

上午就这么照常挨过去，设陷的人自己倒把陷阱忘了。上午的课快收尾了。老式大壁炉里的火已经烧得很低。在肖基眼里本是天使的那些火苗都不见了，先前在他幻想里扮演男人、女人和房屋的那些亮煤，如今盖上了一层白绒绒的灰；那半截没烧透的大压火柴卧在那儿闷燃着，活像睡在雪堆里的巨人。肖基一心想把他叫醒。

===Original===
As for Henry Banta, he was too much bothered to get the answer to a “sum” he was doing, to remember anything about his trap. In fact, he had quite forgotten that half an hour ago in the all-absorbing employment of drawing ugly pictures on his slate and coaxing Betsey Short to giggle by showing them slyly across the schoolroom. Once or twice Ralph had been attracted to Betsey’s extraordinary fits of giggling, and had come so near to catching Hank that the boy thought it best not to run any further risk of the beech switches, four or five feet long, laid up behind the master in sight of the school as a prophylactic. Hence his application just now to his “sum” in long division, and hence his puzzled look, for, idler that he was, his “sums” did not solve themselves easily. As usual in such cases, he came up in front of the master’s desk to have the difficulty explained. He had to wait a minute until Ralph got through with showing Betsey Short, who had been seized with a studying fit, and who could hardly give any attention to the teacher’s explanations, she did want to giggle so much! Not at anything in particular, but just at things in general.

While Ralph was “doing” Betsey’s “sum” for her, he was solving a much more difficult question. A plan had flashed upon him, but the punishment seemed a severe one. He gave it up once or twice, but he remembered how turbulent the Flat Creek elements were; and had he not inly resolved to be as unrelenting as a bulldog? He fortified himself by recalling again the oft-remembered remark of Bud, “Ef Bull wunst takes a holt, heaven and yarth can’t make him let go.” And so he resolved to give Hank and the whole school one good lesson.

===Chinese===
至于亨利·班塔，他正为一道「算术题」算不出得数而心烦意乱，哪还想得起什么陷阱。其实，早在半小时前他就忘了个干净——那时他正专心致志地在石板上画丑八怪，偷偷隔着教室递给贝琪·肖特看，逗她吃吃地笑。有一两回，贝琪那阵莫名其妙的傻笑引得拉尔夫侧目，差一点就逮住汉克，吓得这孩子觉得，最好别再冒什么风险去招惹那几根四五英尺长的山毛榉条子——它们就码在老师身后、全校学生看得见的地方，是防患于未然的。于是他这会儿埋头攻他那道长除法「算术题」，于是也就有了那副愁眉苦脸——因为像他这样的懒汉，「算术题」从来不肯自己解出自己。碰到这种情形，照例，他就走到讲台跟前，请老师讲解难点。他得先等一分钟，等拉尔夫给贝琪·肖特讲完题——贝琪刚犯了一阵用功的毛病，老师讲什么她几乎一个字也听不进去，她实在太想笑了！并不是笑什么特别的，就是见什么笑什么。

拉尔夫替贝琪「做」「算术题」的当口，心里正解着一道难得多的题。他脑子里闪过一个方案，可这惩罚似乎太狠了些。他一两回想作罢，但想起弗拉特溪这帮学生是何等桀骜难驯；再说，他不是已在心里立誓，要像斗牛犬一样绝不松口吗？他又默诵了一遍巴德那句常记在心的话，给自己壮胆：「布尔只要咬上了，天上的神地下的鬼也掰不开它的嘴。」于是他打定主意，给汉克、也给全校好好上一课。

===Original===
“Just step round behind me, Henry, and you can see how I do this,” said Ralph.

Hank was entirely off his guard, and, with his eyes fixed upon the slate on the teacher’s desk, he sidled round upon the broad loose board misplaced by his own hand, and in an instant the other end of the board rose up in the middle of the schoolroom, almost striking Shocky in the face, while Henry Banta went down into the ice-cold water beneath the schoolhouse.

“Why, Henry!” cried Ralph, jumping to his feet with well-feigned surprise. “How *did* this happen?” and he helped the dripping fellow out and seated him by the fire.

Betsey Short giggled.

Shocky was so tickled that he could hardly keep his seat.

The boys who were in the plot looked very serious indeed.

===Chinese===
「绕到我后头来，亨利，好看清楚我是怎么算的。」拉尔夫说。

汉克毫无戒备，眼睛盯着讲台上的石板，侧着身子踩上了那块被他亲手挪了位的宽宽的活木板。一眨眼，木板的另一头在教室中央翘了起来，差点打中肖基的脸，亨利·班塔本人则栽进了校舍底下冰凉的水里。

「哎呀，亨利！」拉尔夫跳起身，装出恰到好处的惊愕，喊道。「这是*怎么*搞的？」说着把那落汤鸡捞上来，安置到炉火边坐了。

贝琪·肖特吃吃地笑了。

肖基乐得差点在凳子上坐不住。

参与密谋的那几个男孩，脸上可就严肃得很了。

===Original===
Ralph made some remarks by way of improving the occasion. He spoke strongly of the utter meanness of the one who could play so heartless a trick on a schoolmate. He said that it was as much thieving to get your fun at the expense of another as to steal his money. And while he talked, all eyes were turned on Hank﻿—all except the eyes of Mirandy Means. They looked simperingly at Ralph. All the rest looked at Hank. The fire had made his face very red. Shocky noticed that. Betsey Short noticed it, and giggled. The master wound up with an appropriate quotation from Scripture. He said that the person who displaced that board had better not be encouraged by the success﻿—he said *success* with a curious emphasis﻿—of the present experiment to attempt another trick of the kind. For it was set down in the Bible that if a man dug a pit for the feet of another he would be very likely to fall in it himself. Which made all the pupils look solemn, except Betsey Short, who giggled. And Shocky wanted to. And Mirandy cast an expiring look at Ralph. And if the teacher was not lovesick, he certainly was sick of Mirandy’s love.

When school was “let out,” Ralph gave Hank every caution that he could about taking cold, and even lent him his overcoat, very much against Hank’s will. For Hank had obstinately refused to go home before the school was dismissed.

Then the master walked out in a quiet and subdued way to spend the noon recess in the woods, while Shocky watched his retreating footsteps with loving admiration. And the pupils not in the secret canvassed the question of who moved the board. Bill Means said he’d bet Hank did it, which set Betsey Short off in an uncontrollable giggle. And Shocky listened innocently.

But that night Bud said slyly: “Thunder and lightning! what a manager you *air*, Mr. Hartsook!” To which Ralph returned no reply except a friendly smile. Muscle paid tribute to brains that time.

But Ralph had no time for exultation; for just here came the spelling-school.

===Chinese===
拉尔夫趁机发表了几句训导。他言辞恳切地痛斥了那种能对同学下此毒手的人是何等下作。他说，拿别人的苦头换自己的乐子，跟偷人家的钱一样，都是偷。他讲话的当口，所有的眼睛都转向了汉克——只有米兰迪·米恩斯的眼睛除外。那双眼睛脉脉含笑地望着拉尔夫。其余人都看着汉克。炉火把他的脸烤得通红。肖基注意到了。贝琪·肖特也注意到了，还吃吃笑了。老师最后引了一句《圣经》里贴切的话收尾。他说，挪动那块板子的人，最好别因为眼下这次实验的*成功*——他说「成功」两个字时带着一种古怪的加重——就受了鼓舞，再去玩这一类的把戏。因为《圣经》上写着：人若给别人脚前挖坑，自己多半要掉进去。这话让全体学生都郑重起来，只有贝琪·肖特例外，她吃吃笑了。肖基也很想笑。米兰迪朝拉尔夫投去一记回光返照式的眼波。要说老师害没害相思病不好讲，米兰迪的这份爱，他确确实实是腻出病来了。

「散学」之后，拉尔夫把能想到的防着凉的话都叮嘱了汉克一遍，还把自己的大衣借给了他——汉克一百个不情愿。因为散学之前，汉克死活不肯先回家。

随后老师悄没声儿、收敛地踱出教室，到树林里去消磨午间的休息，肖基用爱慕的目光目送他远去的背影。不知内情的学生们则讨论起是谁挪了板子。比尔·米恩斯（Bill Means）说敢打赌是汉克干的，惹得贝琪·肖特一阵收不住的傻笑。肖基在一旁没心事地听着。

可是当天晚上，巴德狡黠地说：「我的雷公电母！你可真是个*当家的*，哈茨克先生！」拉尔夫只回了他一个友善的笑，别的什么也没说。那一回，是力气向脑子纳了贡。

可拉尔夫顾不上得意；因为恰恰在这当口，拼字赛来了。

## XI Miss Martha Hawkins / 玛莎·霍金斯小姐

===Original===
“It’s very good for the health to dig in the elements. I was quite emaciated last year at the East, and the doctor told me to dig in the elements. I got me a florial hoe and dug, and it’s been most excellent for me.”18 Time, the Saturday following the Friday on which Ralph kept Shocky company as far as the “forks” near Granny Sanders’s house. Scene, the Squire’s garden. Ralph helping that worthy magistrate perform sundry little jobs such as a warm winter day suggests to the farmer. Miss Martha Hawkins, the Squire’s niece, and his housekeeper in his present bereaved condition, leaning over the palings﻿—pickets she called them﻿—of the garden fence, talking to the master. Miss Hawkins was recently from Massachusetts. How many people there are in the most cultivated communities whose education is partial!

“It’s very common for schoolmaster to dig in the elements at the East,” proceeded Miss Martha. Like many other people born in the celestial empires (of which there are three﻿—China, Virginia, Massachusetts), Miss Martha was not averse to reminding outside barbarians of her good fortune in this regard. It did her good to speak of the East.

===Chinese===
「在元素里刨土，对身体大有好处。我去年在东部的时候瘦得不成样子，医生就嘱咐我在元素里刨土。我置了一把花锄，刨啊刨的，对我真是再好没有了。」18 时间：礼拜五拉尔夫陪肖基走到桑德斯婆婆家附近那个「岔路口」之后的礼拜六。地点：乡绅家的园子。拉尔夫帮那位可敬的法官做些零活——暖和的冬日会让庄稼人想起来的那些零活。玛莎·霍金斯小姐，乡绅的侄女，如今乡绅丧妻，便由她主持家务；她正倚在菜园栅栏——她管栅栏木条叫「尖桩」——上，跟老师说话。霍金斯小姐新近才从马萨诸塞来。最有教养的社群里，教育残缺不全的人何其多啊！

「在东部，老师们在元素里刨土，是顶平常的事。」玛莎小姐接着说。生在天朝上国的人（天朝上国世上共有三个——中国、弗吉尼亚、马萨诸塞）多半有个通病，就是爱在外邦蛮子跟前念叨自己这方面的好福气，玛莎小姐也不例外。说说东部，对她身心有益。

===Original===
Now Ralph was amused with Miss Martha. She really had a good deal of intelligence despite her affectation, and conversation with her was both interesting and diverting. It helped him to forget Hannah, and Bud, and the robbery, and all the rest, and she was so delighted to find somebody to make an impression on that she had come out to talk while Ralph was at work. But just at this moment the schoolmaster was not so much interested in her interesting remarks, nor so much amused by her amusing remarks, as he should have been. He saw a man coming down the road riding one horse and leading another, and he recognized the horses at a distance. It must be Bud who was riding Means’s bay mare and leading Bud’s roan colt. Bud had been to mill, and as the man who owned the horse-mill kept but one old blind horse himself, it was necessary that Bud should take two. It required three horses to run the mill; the old blind one could have ground the grist, but the two others had to overcome the friction of the clumsy machine. But it was not about the horse-mill that Ralph was thinking nor about the two horses. Since that Wednesday evening on which he escorted Hannah home from the spelling-school he had not seen Bud Means. If he had any lingering doubts of the truth of what Mirandy had said, they had been dissipated by the absence of Bud from school.

“When I was to Bosting﻿—” Miss Martha was *to* Boston only once in her life, but as her visit to that sacred city was the most important occurrence of her life, she did not hesitate to air her reminiscences of it frequently. “When I was to Bosting,” she was just saying, when, following the indication of Ralph’s eyes, she saw Bud coming up the hill near Squire Hawkins’s house. Bud looked red and sulky, and to Ralph’s and Miss Martha Hawkins’s polite recognitions he returned only a surly nod. They both saw that he was angry. Ralph was able to guess the meaning of his wrath.

===Chinese===
跟玛莎小姐在一起，拉尔夫觉得挺有意思。她虽然做作，人却实在聪明，同她说话既有趣又解闷。这能让他暂时忘掉汉娜、巴德、劫案和其余的一切；而她好不容易逮着一个能被打动的人，欢喜得趁拉尔夫干活也跑出来聊天。可偏偏在这个当口，老师对她那些有趣的高论不那么着迷，对那些逗笑的妙语也不那么开怀了。他望见一个人顺着大路过来，骑着一匹马，还牵着一匹，离老远就认出了那两匹马。来的准是巴德，骑着米恩斯家的栗色母马，牵着自己的沙毛马驹。巴德是去磨坊了；磨坊主自己只养了一匹瞎眼老马，所以巴德必须带两匹去。转动那座磨坊得用三匹马：瞎眼老马只够磨谷子的，另外两匹是用来克服那台笨机器的摩擦力的。可拉尔夫心里想的既不是马力磨坊，也不是那两匹马。打从礼拜三晚上送汉娜离开拼字赛回家之后，他就没见过巴德·米恩斯。倘若他对米兰迪那番话还有半点疑心，巴德不来上学这件事也早把疑心扫光了。

「我在波士顿那阵——」玛莎小姐一辈子只*去过*一次波士顿，不过既然那趟圣地之行是她生平头等大事，她也就毫不客气，动不动就要翻出来讲讲。「我在波士顿那阵，」她正说到这儿，顺着拉尔夫的眼光一望，看见巴德正走上霍金斯乡绅家旁边那道坡。巴德涨红着脸，一脸愠色；拉尔夫和玛莎·霍金斯小姐客客气气地跟他打招呼，他只闷闷地点了一下头。两人都看得出他在生气。拉尔夫猜得到他这火气的来路。

===Original===
Toward evening Ralph strolled through the Squire’s cornfield toward the woods. The memory of the walk with Hannah was heavy upon the heart of the young master, and there was comfort in the very miserableness of the cornstalks with their disheveled blades hanging like tattered banners and rattling discordantly in the rising wind. Wandering without purpose, Ralph followed the rows of stalks first one way and then the other in a zigzag line, turning a right angle every minute or two. At last he came out in a woods mostly of beech, and he pleased his melancholy fancy by kicking the dry and silky leaves before him in billows, while the soughing of the wind through the long, vibrant boughs and slender twigs of the beech forest seemed to put the world into the wailing minor key of his own despair.

What a fascination there is in a path come upon suddenly without a knowledge of its termination! Here was one running in easy, irregular curves through the wood, now turning gently to the right in order to avoid a stump, now swaying suddenly to the left to gain an easier descent at a steep place, and now turning wantonly to the one side or the other, as if from very caprice in the man who by idle steps unconsciously marked the line of the footpath at first. Ralph could not resist the impulse﻿—who could?﻿—to follow the path and find out its destination, and following it he came presently into a lonesome hollow, where a brook gurgled among the heaps of bare limestone rocks that filled its bed. Following the path still, he came upon a queer little cabin built of round logs, in the midst of a small garden-patch enclosed by a brush fence. The stick chimney, daubed with clay and topped with a barrel open at both ends, made this a typical cabin.

===Chinese===
傍晚时分，拉尔夫信步穿过乡绅家的玉米地，朝树林走去。与汉娜同路的那段记忆沉沉压在年轻老师的心上；玉米秆那副可怜相反倒给人安慰——披散的叶子像扯烂的旗子挂着，在渐起的风里飒啦啦地响得不成调子。拉尔夫漫无目的地走着，顺着一行行玉米秆先走这边、又走那边，走出一条之字线，每一两分钟拐一个直角。最后他走进一片以山毛榉为主的树林。他把面前干燥柔滑的落叶踢成一浪一浪，让忧郁的幻想得了些快意；风穿过山毛榉林那长长颤动的枝干和纤细的枝梢，发出呜呜的声响，仿佛把整个世界都调成了他自己绝望的那支哀婉小调。

一条小路突兀地出现在眼前，又不知通向哪里——这里头自有一种叫人着迷的劲儿！眼前这一条，就在林子里画着舒展而不规则的曲线：一会儿为绕开一个树桩轻轻右转，一会儿为在陡处寻个好下脚又猛地左摆，一会儿又任性地歪向这一边或那一边，仿佛当初那个闲步踏出这条路来的人，走的每一步都由着性子。拉尔夫忍不住想顺着这条路走到头，看看它通到哪里——谁能忍得住呢？——走着走着，他来到一处冷清的洼地，一条小溪在塞满河床的成堆裸露石灰岩间汩汩作响。再顺着路走，他撞见一座古怪的小圆木屋，坐落在灌木篱笆围起来的一小片园地当中。枝条搭的烟囱抹着泥，顶上安一只两头打通的木桶——这就成了一座再典型不过的小木屋。

===Original===
It flashed upon Ralph that this place must be Rocky Hollow, and that this was the house of old John Pearson, the one-legged basket-maker, and his rheumatic wife﻿—the house that hospitably sheltered Shocky. Following his impulse, he knocked and was admitted, and was not a little surprised to find Miss Martha Hawkins there before him.

“You here, Miss Hawkins?” he said when he had returned Shocky’s greeting and shaken hands with the old couple.

“Bless you, yes,” said the old lady. “That blessed gyirl”﻿—the old lady called her a girl by a sort of figure of speech perhaps﻿—“that blessed gyirl’s the kindest creetur you ever saw﻿—comes here every day, most, to cheer a body up with somethin’ or nuther.”

===Chinese===
拉尔夫灵光一闪：这地方准是乱石谷，这就是独腿编筐匠老约翰·皮尔逊（John Pearson）和他患风湿的老伴的家——也就是好客地收留肖基的那户人家。他凭着一股冲动敲了门，被让了进去，发现玛莎·霍金斯小姐竟已先他而至，不禁相当吃惊。

「你怎么在这儿，霍金斯小姐？」他应过肖基的招呼、同老两口握过手，说道。

「老天爷，可不嘛，」老太太说。「那位好心的姑娘」——老太太管她叫姑娘，兴许用的是一种修辞格——「那位好心的姑娘可是你从没见过的好心人儿——差不多天天来，变着法儿给俺们解闷儿。」

===Original===
Miss Martha blushed, and said “she came because Rocky Hollow looked so much like a place she used to know at the East. Mr. and Mrs. Pearson were the kindest people. They reminded her of people she knew at the East. When she was to Bosting﻿—”

Here the old basket-maker lifted his head from his work, and said: “Pshaw! that talk about kyindness” (he was a Kentuckian and said *kyindness*) “is all humbug. I wonder so smart a woman as you don’t know better. You come nearder to bein kyind than anybody I know; but, laws a me! we’re all selfish akordin’ to my tell.”

“You wasn’t selfish when you set up with my father most every night for two weeks,” said Shocky as he handed the old man a splint.

===Chinese===
玛莎小姐红了脸，说「她来，是因为乱石谷太像她从前在东部熟悉的一个地方了。皮尔逊先生和皮尔逊太太是天底下最厚道的人。他们让她想起她在东部认识的那些人。她在波士顿那阵——」

老编筐匠这时从活计上抬起头来，说：「呸！什么厚道不厚道」（他是肯塔基人，「厚道」两个字总拖着长腔，说成『厚道——』）「全是瞎话。恁聪明的女人竟不明白这个，俺倒奇怪了。要说厚道，你比俺认得的人都厚道；可是，老天爷！照俺的说法，咱们人人自私。」

「我爹最后那两个礼拜，你差不多夜夜陪着他守夜，那时候你可不自私。」肖基一边递给老人一根篾条一边说。

===Original===
“Yes, I was, too!” This in a tone that made Ralph tremble. “Your father was a miserable Britisher. I’d fit redcoats, in the war of eighteen-twelve, and lost my leg by one of ’em stickin’ his dog-on’d bagonet right through it, that night at Lundy’s Lane; but my messmate killed him though which is a satisfaction to think on. And I didn’t like your father ’cause he was a Britisher. But ef he’d a died right here in this free country, ’though nobody to give him a drink of water, blamed ef I wouldn’t a been ashamed to set on the platform at a Fourth of July barbecue, and to hold up my wooden leg fer to make the boys cheer! That was the selfishest thing I ever done. We’re all selfish akordin’ to my tell.”

“You wasn’t selfish when you took me that night, you know,” and Shocky’s face beamed with gratitude.

“Yes, I war, too, you little sass-box! What did I take you fer? Hey? Bekase I didn’t like Pete Jones nor Bill Jones. They’re thieves, dog-on ’em!”

Ralph shivered a little. The horse with the white forefoot and white nose galloped before his eyes again.

===Chinese===
「自私！俺可不自私！」这嗓门让拉尔夫都打了个哆嗦。「你爹是个倒霉的英国佬。一八一二年打仗那阵，俺跟红袄子兵交过手，就是那天夜里在伦迪巷（Lundy’s Lane），叫一个该死的红袄子一刺刀正正戳穿了这条腿，废了；可俺的战友结果了他，想起这事心里总算舒坦。俺不喜欢你爹，就因为他是英国佬。可他要真死在咱这块自由的土地上，身边连个给他递口水的人都没有，那俺往后坐在七月四日烧烤会的主席台上、举着这条木腿好让小伙子们喝彩的时候，脸往哪儿搁？俺这辈子干过的顶自私的事，就是这一件。照俺的说法，咱们人人自私。」

「那天夜里你收留我的时候，你可不自私，你知道的。」肖基的脸上放光，满是感激。

「自私！俺咋不自私，你这张小贫嘴！俺图啥收留你？啊？就因为俺看皮特·琼斯和比尔·琼斯不顺眼。他们是贼，叫他们见鬼去！」

拉尔夫微微一颤。那匹白前蹄、白鼻子的马，又在他眼前奔了起来。

===Original===
“They’re a set of thieves. That’s what they air.”

“Please, Mr. Pearson, be careful. You’ll get into trouble, you know, by talking that way,” said Miss Hawkins. “You’re just like a man that I knew at the East.”

“Why, do you think an old soldier like me, hobbling on a wooden leg, is afraid of them thieves? Didn’t I face the Britishers? Didn’t I come home late last Wednesday night? I rather guess I must a took a little too much at Welch’s grocery, and laid down in the middle of the street to rest. The boys thought ’twas funny to crate19 me. I woke up kind o’ cold, ’bout one in the mornin’. ’Bout two o’clock I come up Means’s hill, and didn’t I see Pete Jones, and them others that robbed the Dutchman, and somebody, I dunno who, a-crossin’ the bluegrass paster *towards* Jones’s?” (Ralph shivered.) “Don’t shake your finger at me, old woman. Tongue is all I’ve got to fight with now; but I’ll fight them thieves tell the sea goes dry, I will. Shocky, gim me a splint.”

===Chinese===
「他们是一窝贼。就是这么回事。」

「皮尔逊先生，说话可要当心。你这么讲，是要惹祸上身的，你知道吧。」霍金斯小姐说。「你真像我在东部认识的一个男人。」

「嗐，你当俺这么个拄着木腿一瘸一拐的老兵，会怕他们那伙贼？俺连英国佬都没怕过！俺上礼拜三夜里是不是很晚才到家？俺寻思俺准是在韦尔奇（Welch）杂货铺多灌了几口，就躺在街当中歇了歇。那帮小子觉得拿篓子把俺扣上19怪好玩。俺醒来有点冷，约莫凌晨一点。两点来钟，俺爬上米恩斯家那道坡，俺是不是瞧见了皮特·琼斯，还有抢那荷兰佬的那伙人里的几个，还有一个——俺不认得是谁——正穿过蓝草牧场，*朝*琼斯家去？」（拉尔夫打了个哆嗦。）「老婆子，别冲俺摇手指头。俺如今就剩一条舌头能打仗了；可俺跟这帮贼打到底，打到海水干掉，俺说打就打。肖基，递根篾条给俺。」

===Original===
“But you wasn’t selfish when you tuck me,” Shocky stuck to his point most positively.

“Yes, I was, you little towheaded fool! I didn’t take you kase I was good, not a bit of it. I hated Bill Jones what keeps the poorhouse, and I knowed him and Pete would get you bound to some of their click, and I didn’t want no more thieves raised; so when your mother hobbled, with you a-leadin’ her, poor blind thing! all the way over here on that winter night, and said, ‘Mr. Pearson, you’re all the friend I’ve got, and I want you to save my boy,’ why, you see I was selfish as ever I could be in takin’ of you. Your mother’s cryin’ sot me a-cryin’ too. We’re all selfish in everything, akordin’ to my tell. Blamed ef we ha’n’t, Miss Hawkins, only sometimes I’d think you was real benev’lent ef I didn’t know we war all selfish.”

===Chinese===
「可你收留我的时候就是不自私。」肖基咬定这一点，寸步不让。

「自私，俺就是自私，你这小白毛傻小子！俺收留你可不是因为俺心善，一点也不是。俺恨透了管贫民院的比尔·琼斯，俺也知道他跟皮特早晚会把你卖身给他们那一伙里的什么人，俺不想这个世上再多养出几个贼来；所以那天夜里，你娘——可怜的瞎子！——由你牵着，一路摸到俺这儿来，说：『皮尔逊先生，你是我仅有的朋友了，求你救救我的孩子。』你瞧，俺收留你的时候，自私得不能再自私了。你娘哭，把俺也哭得掉泪。照俺的说法，咱们事事都自私。咱们可不就是这么回事嘛，霍金斯小姐；只是有时候俺几乎要当你真是个大善人——要不是俺知道咱们人人自私的话。」

## XXI Miss Nancy Sawyer / 南希·索耶小姐

===Original===
In a little old cottage in Lewisburg, on one of the streets which was never traveled except by a solitary cow seeking pasture or a countryman bringing wood to some one of the half-dozen families living in it, and which in summer was decked with a profusion of the yellow and white blossoms of the dog-fennel﻿—in this unfrequented street, so generously and unnecessarily broad, lived Miss Nancy Sawyer and her younger sister Semantha. Miss Nancy was a providence, one of those old maids that are benedictions to the whole town; one of those in whom the mother-love, wanting the natural objects on which to spend itself, overflows all bounds and lavishes itself on every needy thing, and grows richer and more abundant with the spending, a fountain of inexhaustible blessing. There is no nobler life possible to anyone than to an unmarried woman. The more shame that some choose a selfish one, and thus turn to gall all the affection with which they are endowed. Miss Nancy Sawyer had been Ralph’s Sunday-school teacher, and it was precious little, so far as information went, that he learned from her; for she never could conceive of Jerusalem as a place in any essential regard very different from Lewisburg, where she had spent her life. But Ralph learned from her what most Sunday-school teachers fail to teach, the great lesson of Christianity, by the side of which all antiquities and geographies and chronologies and exegetics and other niceties are as nothing.

===Chinese===
在刘易斯堡一座小小的旧村舍里——那街平日绝无人迹，只有一头寻草吃的孤零零的母牛，或是个给街上六七户人家中的某家送柴的乡下人会走一走；一到夏天，满街开遍狗茴香（dog-fennel）黄黄白白的花——就在这条僻静得出奇、宽绰得没道理的街上，住着南希·索耶小姐和她妹妹瑟曼莎（Semantha）。南希小姐是一位「天意」，是那种给全镇带来福分的老姑娘之一；她们身上的母爱，因为缺少天然的倾注对象，便漫过一切堤界，倾洒在每一件穷苦可怜的事物上，而且越洒越丰盈、越洒越充裕，成了一眼永不枯竭的福泉。再没有比未婚女子更高贵的生涯了。可惜偏偏有人选了自私的一种，把天赋的柔情全酿成了苦胆。南希·索耶小姐做过拉尔夫的主日学老师；论传授知识，他从她那儿学到的少得可怜，因为她一辈子住在刘易斯堡，无论如何也想像不出耶路撒冷在哪个要紧的地方和刘易斯堡有什么两样。可拉尔夫从她那儿学到了大多数主日学老师教不出来的东西——基督教的那门大功课；在它面前，一切古物学、地理学、年代学、解经学以及种种精微，都算不得什么。

===Original===
And now he turned the head of the roan toward the cottage of Miss Nancy Sawyer as naturally as the roan would have gone to his own stall in the stable at home. The snow had gradually ceased to fall, and was eddying round the house, when Ralph dismounted from his foaming horse, and, carrying the still form of Shocky as reverently as though it had been something heavenly, knocked at Miss Nancy Sawyer’s door.

With natural feminine instinct that lady started back when she saw Hartsook, for she had just built a fire in the stove, and she now stood at the door with unwashed face and uncombed hair.

“Why, Ralph Hartsook, where did you drop down from﻿—and what have you got?”

===Chinese===
此刻他把沙毛马的头拨向南希·索耶小姐的小屋，就像沙毛马回了自家棚里的那个槽头一样自然。雪已渐渐停了，只在屋子四周打着旋。拉尔夫跳下浑身冒着白气的马，把肖基那纹丝不动的小身子捧在怀里，恭恭敬敬，仿佛捧的是一件天上的东西，然后敲响了南希·索耶小姐的门。

那位小姐凭女人的天性，一见哈茨克就往后缩了一缩，因为她刚在炉子里生了火，此刻站在门口，脸没洗，头发也没梳。

「哎呀，拉尔夫·哈茨克，你从哪儿落下来的——你抱着的是什么呀？」

===Original===
“I came from Flat Creek this morning, and I brought you a little angel who has got out of heaven, and needs some of your motherly care.”

Shocky was brought in. The chill shook him now by fits only, for a fever had spotted his cheeks already.

“Who are you?” said Miss Nancy, as she unwrapped him.

“I’m Shocky, a little boy as God forgot, and then thought of again.”

===Chinese===
「我今早从弗拉特溪来，给你带来了一位溜出天堂的小天使，正缺你的一份慈母照看。」

肖基被抱了进去。寒战如今只是一阵一阵地攫他，因为热病已在他两颊烧出了斑斑红晕。

「你是谁呀？」南希小姐一面给他解开裹着的披巾，一面问。

「我是肖基，一个小男孩，上帝先把我忘了，后来又想起我来了。」

## XVIII Odds and Ends / 零零碎碎

===Original===
The Spring-in-rock, or, as it was sometimes, by a curious perversion, called, the “rock-in-spring,” was a spring running out of a cave-like fissure in a high limestone cliff. Here the old man sheltered himself on that dreary Christmas evening, until Bud brought his roan colt to the top of the cliff above, and he and Ralph helped the old man up the cliff and into the saddle. Ralph went back to bed, but Bud, who was only too eager to put in his best licks, walked by the side of old John Pearson the six miles over to Buckeye Run, and at last, after eleven o’clock, he deposited him in a hollow sycamore by the road, there to wait the coming of the mail-wagon that would carry him into Jackson County.

“Goodbye,” said the basket-maker, as Bud mounted the colt to return. “Ef I’m wanted jest send me word, and I’ll make a forrard movement any time. I don’t like this ’ere thing of running off in the nighttime. But I reckon General Winfield Scott would a ordered a retreat ef he’d a been in my shoes. I’m lots obleeged to you. Akordin’ to my tell, we’re all of us selfish in everything; but I’ll be dog-on’d ef I don’t believe you and one or two more is exceptions.”

===Chinese===
「石中泉」——有时也被古怪地倒了过来，叫成「泉中石」——是一眼泉水，从高高的石灰岩崖壁上一道洞穴般的裂缝里流出来。那个凄凉的圣诞夜，老人就躲在这里，直到巴德把他的沙毛马驹牵到崖顶上，他和拉尔夫两人把老人扶上崖、扶上鞍。拉尔夫回床接着躺，巴德呢，正巴不得卖尽力气，便步行六英里，一路陪着老约翰·皮尔逊到七叶树溪（Buckeye Run），十一点过后，才把他安顿在路边一棵空心梧桐树里，在那儿等送他去杰克逊县（Jackson County）的邮车。

「再会了，」巴德翻身上马要回去时，编筐匠说。「要是用得着俺，捎个话来，俺随时来个向前推进。俺不稀罕这种夜里开溜的勾当。不过俺寻思，温菲尔德·斯科特将军（General Winfield Scott）要是换了俺这双鞋，也得下令撤退。多谢你啦。照俺的说法，咱们事事都自私；可要是俺不信你和另外一两位是例外，俺就不是人。」

===Original===
Whether it was that the fact that Pete Jones had got consid’able shuck up demoralized his followers, or whether it was that the old man’s flight was suspected, the mob did not turn out in very great force, and the tarring was postponed indefinitely, for by the time they came together it became known somehow that the man with a wooden leg had outrun them all. But the escape of one devoted victim did not mollify the feelings of the people toward the next one.

By the time Bud returned his arm was very painful, and the next day he went under Dr. Small’s treatment to reduce the fracture. Whatever suspicions Bud might have of Pete Jones, he was not afflicted with Ralph’s dread of the silent young doctor. And if there was anything Small admired it was physical strength and courage. Small wanted Bud on his side, and least of all did he want him to be Ralph’s champion. So that the silent, cool, and skillful doctor went to work to make an impression on Bud Means.

===Chinese===
不知是因为皮特·琼斯挨的那一下够呛、弄得他的党羽泄了气，还是因为老头儿的出逃走漏了风声，那伙暴民没凑起多大的阵仗，涂柏油的事无限期推了下去——因为他们聚到一块儿的时候，不知怎么都听说了：装木腿的那位把他们全都甩在了后头。可一位忠心的受害者脱了身，并没有让人们对下一位心软半分。

巴德回来时胳膊已疼得厉害，第二天便去找斯莫尔医生诊治复位。不管巴德对皮特·琼斯有几分疑心，他却没染上拉尔夫对这位沉默的年轻医生的那种畏惧。而斯莫尔要是还钦佩什么，那就是体力和胆气。斯莫尔想把巴德拉到自己一边，最不愿见到的就是他当拉尔夫的卫士。于是这位沉默、冷静、手艺高明的医生，动起手来给巴德·米恩斯留个好印象。

===Original===
Other influences were at work upon him also. Mrs. Means volleyed and thundered in her usual style about his “takin’ up with a one-legged thief, and runnin’ arter that master that was a mighty suspicious kind of a customer, akordin’ to her tell. She’d allers said so. Ef she’d a been consulted he wouldn’t a been hired. He warn’t fit company fer nobody.”

And old Jack Means ’lowed Bud must want to have *their* barns burnt like some other folkses had been. Fer his part, he had sense enough to know they was some people as it wouldn’t do to set a body’s self agin. And as fer him, he didn’t butt his brains out agin a buckeye-tree. Not when he was sober. And so they managed, during Bud’s confinement to the house, to keep him well supplied with all the ordinary discomforts of life.

But one visit from Martha Hawkins, ten words of kindly inquiry from her, and the remark that his broken arm reminded her of something she had seen at the East and something somebody said the time she was to Bosting, were enough to repay the champion a thousand fold for all that he suffered. Indeed, that visit, and the recollection of Ralph’s saying that Jesus Christ was a sort of a Flat Creeker himself, were manna in the wilderness to Bud.

===Chinese===
别的力量也在他身上使劲。米恩斯太太拿出她惯常的那套连珠炮，轰骂他「跟一个一条腿的贼混在一块儿，还追着那个老师跑——照俺的说法，那老师就是个顶顶可疑的主儿。俺从来就这么说。当初要是问了俺，压根儿就不会雇他。他跟谁都不配做伴。」

老杰克·米恩斯则一口咬定，巴德准是想让*他们自家*的谷仓也像别人家那样烧掉。就他自己而言，他还算有点脑子，知道世上有些人，是万万不能跟他对着干的。至于他嘛，他可不会拿自己的脑壳往七叶树上撞。脑子清醒的时候不会。就这样，趁巴德困在屋里养伤的工夫，一家人把他人生在世的种种寻常苦楚，供应得足足的。

可玛莎·霍金斯来探望了一回，问了十来句关切的话，又说他的断臂让她想起她在东部见过的什么，还有她去波士顿那阵子听谁说过的一句什么——光是这些，就够报答这位卫士千倍，抵过他受的一切苦。真的，那一趟探望，再加上想起拉尔夫说过的那句「耶稣基督自己也算是个弗拉特溪人」，对巴德简直就是荒野里的吗哪。

===Original===
Poor Shocky was sick. The excitement had been too much for him, and though his fever was very slight it was enough to produce just a little delirium. Either Ralph or Miss Martha was generally at the cabin.

“They’re coming,” said Shocky to Ralph, “they’re coming. Pete Jones is a-going to bind me out for a hundred years. I wish Hanner would hold me so’s he couldn’t. God’s forgot all about us here in Flat Creek, and there’s nobody to help it.”

And he shivered at every sudden sound. He was never free from this delirious fright except when the master held him tight in his arms. He staggered around the floor, the very shadow of Shocky, and was so terrified by the approach of darkness that Ralph stayed in the cabin on Wednesday night and Miss Hawkins stayed on Thursday night. On Friday, Bud sent a note to Ralph, askin him to come and see him.

===Chinese===
可怜的肖基病倒了。这一场惊吓对他来说太过火了；虽说烧得不重，也够引起一点轻微的谵语。小屋里通常总有拉尔夫或玛莎小姐守着。

「他们来了，」肖基对拉尔夫说，「他们来了。皮特·琼斯要把我卖身帮工一百年。我恨不得汉娜抱住我，他就带不成了。上帝把咱们弗拉特溪的人全忘光了，没人帮得了这个忙。」

每一声突然的响动都叫他一哆嗦。只有老师把他紧紧搂在怀里时，他才从这谵语的惊惶里解脱出来。他在地板上摇摇晃晃地走来走去，活脱脱成了肖基的影子；他那样害怕天黑下来，礼拜三夜里拉尔夫便留在小屋，礼拜四夜里霍金斯小姐留下。礼拜五，巴德给拉尔夫捎了张条子，请他来见一面。

===Original===
“You see, Mr. Hartsook, I ha’n’t forgot what was said about puttin’ in our best licks for Jesus Christ. I’ve been a-trying to read some about him while I set here. And I read where he said somethin about doing fer the least of his brethren being as the same like as if it was done fer Jesus Christ his-self. Now there’s Shocky. I reckon, p’r’aps, as anybody is a little brother of Jesus Christ, it is that Shocky. Pete Jones and his brother Bill is determined to have him back there to-morry. Bekase you see, Pete’s one of the County Commissioners and to-morry’s the day that they bind out. He wants to bind out that boy jes’ to spite ole Pearson and you and me. You see, the ole woman’s been helped by the neighbors, and he’ll claim Shocky to be a pauper, and they a’n’t no human soul here as dares to do a thing con*tra*ry to Pete. Couldn’t you git him over to Lewisburg? I’ll lend you my roan colt.”

Ralph thought a minute. He dared not take Shocky to the uncle’s where he found his only home. But there was Miss Nancy Sawyer, the old maid who was everybody’s blessing. He could ask her to keep him. And, at any rate, he would save Shocky somehow.

As he went out in the dusk, he met Hannah in the lane.

===Chinese===
「你瞧，哈茨克先生，俺没忘了咱们说过的，要为耶稣基督卖尽力气。俺坐在这儿，一直想法子读点写他的书。俺读到他说过的话：为他的弟兄中最小的一个做的，就跟做在耶稣基督本人身上一个样。如今就有个肖基。俺寻思，要说谁够得上是耶稣基督的小兄弟，那就是肖基了。皮特·琼斯和他兄弟比尔铁了心，明儿个要把他弄回去。因为你知道，皮特是县委专员之一，明儿个正是他们办卖身帮工的日子。他把那孩子抓去卖身，就是要气死老皮尔逊，气死你俺。你瞧，那老婆子一直靠邻居接济过活，他就会一口咬定肖基是贫民；这一带没有一个人敢做一件违*背*皮特的事。你能不能把他送到刘易斯堡去？俺把俺的沙毛马借给你。」

拉尔夫想了一分钟。他不敢把肖基带到叔叔家去——那是他唯一的栖身之处。可还有南希·索耶小姐，那位人人沾她福气的姑娘。他可以求她收留。无论如何，他总要把肖基救下来。

他趁暮色出门时，在小巷里遇见了汉娜。

## VII Ominous Remarks of Mr. Jones / 琼斯先生的凶话

===Original===
The schoolmaster’s mind was like ancient Gaul﻿—divided into three parts. With one part he mechanically performed his school duties. With another he asked himself, What shall I do about the robbery? And with the third he debated about Bud and Hannah. For Bud was not present, and it was clear that he was angry, and there was a storm brewing. In fact, it seemed to Ralph that there was a storm brewing all round the sky. For Pete Jones was evidently angry at the thought of having been watched, and it was fair to suppose that Dr. Small was not in any better humor than usual. And so, between Bud’s jealousy and revenge and the suspicion and resentment of the men engaged in the robbery at “the Dutchman’s” (as the only German in the whole region was called), Ralph’s excited nerves had cause for tremor. At one moment he would resolve to have Hannah at all costs. In the next his conscience would question the rightfulness of the conclusion. Then he would make up his mind to tell all he knew about the robbery. But if he told his suspicions about Small, nobody would believe him. And if he told about Pete Jones, he really could tell only enough to bring vengeance upon himself. And how could he explain his own walk through the pasture and down the road? What business had he being out of bed at two o’clock in the morning? The circumstantial evidence was quite as strong against him as against the man on the horse with the white left forefoot and the white nose. Suspicion might fasten on himself. And then what would be the effect on his prospects? On the people at Lewisburg? On Hannah? It is astonishing how much instruction and comfort there is in a bulldog. This slender schoolmaster, who had been all his life repressing the animal and developing the finer nature, now found a need of just what the bulldog had. And so, with the thought of how his friend the dog would fight in a desperate strait, he determined to take hold of his difficulties as Bull took hold of the raccoon. Moral questions he postponed for careful decision. But for the present he set his teeth together in a desperate, bulldog fashion, and he set his feet down slowly, positively, bulldoggedly. After a wretched supper at Pete Jones’s he found himself at the spelling-school, which, owing to the absence of Hannah, and the excitement about the burglary, was a dull affair. Half the evening was spent in talking in little knots. Pete Jones had taken the afflicted “Dutchman” under his own particular supervision.

===Chinese===
这位老师的心思活像古高卢——分作三块。一块机械地应付着教学的本职。另一块问自己：劫案这件事，我该怎么办？第三块则拿来斟酌巴德和汉娜的事。因为巴德没来上学，明摆着在生气，一场风暴正在酝酿。其实，在拉尔夫看来，满天四下里都在酝酿风暴。皮特·琼斯一想到被人盯了梢，显然动了火；而斯莫尔医生的心情，料想也不会比平常好到哪里去。于是，一边是巴德的醋意和报复心，一边是参与抢劫「荷兰佬」家（这一带唯一的德国人就得了这么个称呼）的那伙人的疑心和怨毒，拉尔夫绷紧的神经有的是发抖的理由。这一刻他下定决心，不惜一切也要得到汉娜；下一刻良心又来质问这结论正当不正当。然后他拿定主意，要把劫案里自己知道的全说出来。可他要是说出对斯莫尔的怀疑，没有人会信；要是说皮特·琼斯，他真正能说的那点东西，只够把仇引到自己头上。再说，他自己穿过牧场、顺着大路走的那一段，又怎么解释？凌晨两点不躺在床上，他有什么正经事？旁证指向他的分量，跟指向那个骑马的人——骑一匹左前脚和鼻子都是白色的马的人——的分量一样沉。嫌疑没准儿就落到他自己头上。到那时，他的前程会怎样？刘易斯堡那些人会怎样？汉娜会怎样？一条斗牛犬身上竟有这么多教益和安慰，实在叫人吃惊。这位瘦削的老师，一辈子都在压抑身上的兽性、涵养精细的天性，如今却发现，自己恰恰缺一条斗牛犬有的东西。于是，想到他的朋友那条狗在绝境里会怎样厮打，他决意像布尔咬住浣熊那样，一口咬住自己的难题不放。道德问题，他暂且搁起，留待从长计议。但眼下，他绝望地、斗牛犬式地把牙关咬紧，把脚步放慢、放实、放到斗牛犬那份固执。在皮特·琼斯家吃过一顿难以下咽的晚饭，他来到了拼字赛——汉娜没来，人人又为失窃的事激动，拼字赛开得兴味索然。半个晚上都耗在一小团一小团的窃窃私语里。皮特·琼斯把那位受害的「荷兰佬」置于自己特别的照管之下。

===Original===
“I s’pose,” said Pete, “that them air fellers what robbed your house must a come down from Jinkins Run. They’re the blamedest set up there I ever see.”

“Ya‑as,” said Schroeder, “put how did Yinkins vellers know dat I sell te medder to te Shquire, hey? How tid Yinkins know anyting ’bout the Shquire’s bayin’ me dree huntert in te hard gash﻿—hey?”

“Some scoundrels down in these ’ere parts is a-layin’ in with Jinkins Run, I’ll bet a hoss,” said Pete. Ralph wondered whether he’d bet the one with the white left forefoot and the white nose. “Now,” said Pete, “ef I could find the feller that’s a-helpin’ them scoundrels rob us folks, I’d help stretch him to the neardest tree.”

“So vood I,” said Schroeder. “I’d shtretch him dill he baid me my dree huntert tollars pack, so I vood.”

And Betsey Short, who had found the whole affair very funny, was transported with a fit of tittering at poor Schroeder’s English. Ralph, fearing that his silence would excite suspicion, tried to talk. But he could not tell what he knew, and all that he said sounded so hollow and hypocritical that it made him feel guilty. And so he shut his mouth, and meditated profitably on the subject of bull dogs. And when later he overheard the garrulous Jones declare that he’d bet a hoss he could p’int out somebody as know’d a blamed sight more’n they keerd to tell, he made up his mind that if it came to p’inting out he should try to be even with Jones.

===Chinese===
「俺琢磨，」皮特说，「抢你家的那帮家伙，准是从金金斯溪（Jinkins Run）那边下来的。那一带的货色，是俺这辈子见过最操蛋的一窝。」

「是——呀，」施罗德（Schroeder）说，「可是金金斯的伙计怎么地晓得我要把草场卖给乡绅，嘿？他们怎么地晓得乡绅要拿现款付我三百块——嘿？」

「咱们这地界里有些恶棍，跟金金斯溪是一伙的，俺敢拿一匹马打赌。」皮特说。拉尔夫暗想，不知他敢不敢拿那匹左前脚和鼻子都是白色的马来赌。「这么着，」皮特说，「要是叫俺逮着那个帮这帮恶棍抢咱们乡亲的家伙，俺乐意搭把手，把他吊上最近的一棵树。」

「我也一样，」施罗德说。「我要吊着他，吊到他赔我那三百块钱为止，我一定这么干。」

贝琪·肖特本就觉得整件事好笑得很，这会儿听可怜的施罗德讲英语，更是乐得一阵吃吃笑个不住。拉尔夫生怕自己一言不发惹人疑心，也试着搭话。可他知道的内情说不得，而说出口的每一句又都那么空洞虚伪，倒叫他自己都觉得心虚。于是他索性闭上嘴，就「斗牛犬」这个题目默想了一阵，颇有所得。后来，他又听见那张碎嘴子琼斯宣称：他敢拿一匹马打赌，有一个人知道的鬼名堂，比他们肯说出来的多出一大截——听到这儿，拉尔夫打定了主意：真到了指认的那一天，他要设法跟琼斯算个平手。

## XXII Pancakes / 煎饼

===Original===
Half an hour later, Ralph, having seen Miss Nancy Sawyer’s machinery of warm baths and simple remedies safely in operation, and having seen the roan colt comfortably stabled, and rewarded for his faithfulness by a bountiful supply of the best hay and the promise of oats when he was cool﻿—half an hour later Ralph was doing the most ample, satisfactory, and amazing justice to his Aunt Matilda’s hot buckwheat-cakes and warm coffee. And after his life in Flat Creek, Aunt Matilda’s house did look like paradise. How white the tablecloth, how bright the coffeepot, how clean the woodwork, how glistening the brass doorknobs, how spotless everything that came under the sovereign sway of Mrs. Matilda White! For in every Indiana village as large as Lewisburg, there are generally a half-dozen women who are admitted to be the best housekeepers. All others are only imitators. And the strife is between these for the preeminence. It is at least safe to say that no other in Lewisburg stood so high as an enemy to dirt, and as a “rat, roach, and mouse exterminator,” as did Mrs. Matilda White, the wife of Ralph’s maternal uncle, Robert White, Esq., a lawyer in successful practice. Of course no member of Mrs. White’s family ever stayed at home longer than was necessary. Her husband found his office﻿—which he kept in as bad a state as possible in order to maintain an equilibrium in his life﻿—much more comfortable than the stiffly clean house at home. From the time that Ralph had come to live as a chore-boy at his uncle’s, he had ever crossed the threshold of Aunt Matilda’s temple of cleanliness with a horrible sense of awe. And Walter Johnson, her son by a former marriage, had﻿—poor, weak-willed fellow!﻿—been driven into bad company and bad habits by the wretchedness of extreme civilization. And yet he showed the hereditary trait, for all the genius which Mrs. White consecrated to the glorious work of making her house too neat to be habitable, her son Walter gave to tying exquisite knots in his colored cravats and combing his oiled locks so as to look like a dandy barber. And she had no other children. The kind Providence that watches over the destiny of children takes care that very few of them are lodged in these terribly clean houses.

===Chinese===
半小时后，拉尔夫眼看南希·索耶小姐那套温水浴和简单药剂的全套机器都已稳妥运转，又眼看沙毛马驹舒舒服服进了棚，忠心得了犒赏——上好的干草管够，等汗凉了还有燕麦——半小时后，拉尔夫正对玛蒂尔达舅母的热荞麦煎饼和热咖啡，进行着最充分的、最叫人满意的、也最惊人的公正对待。在弗拉特溪过了那些日子之后，玛蒂尔达舅母的家看上去真的像天堂。桌布多白，咖啡壶多亮，木器多干净，黄铜门把手多锃光，一切落在玛蒂尔达·怀特太太（Mrs. Matilda White）治下的东西都一尘不染！因为在刘易斯堡这样规模的印第安纳村镇里，公认的最佳持家好手通常总有那么五六位，其余的都只是模仿者，争雄也在她们几个之间。至少可以保险地说：作为污垢的仇敌、作为「大老鼠小耗子蟑螂歼灭者」，刘易斯堡没有谁的地位高得过玛蒂尔达·怀特太太——拉尔夫的舅舅罗伯特·怀特先生（Robert White, Esq.）的太太，一位开业兴隆的律师。当然，怀特太太家里没有一个人在家多待得超过必要的时分。她丈夫觉得自己的事务所舒服得多——他把那儿故意弄得尽可能的乱，好让自己的人生维持一点平衡。打从拉尔夫到他舅舅家当杂工住下起，每次跨进玛蒂尔达舅母这座洁净的殿堂，他心里都怀着一种可怕的敬畏。而沃尔特·约翰逊（Walter Johnson），她前次婚姻带来的儿子——可怜的意志薄弱的人！——已经被这种极端文明的悲惨逼进了坏朋友和坏习惯里。不过他也露了家风：怀特太太把全部天才奉献给「把家收拾得干净得没法住人」这项光荣事业，她儿子沃尔特则把他的天才用来在花领巾上打精致的结，把抹了油的头发梳出理发店花花公子的模样。她没有别的孩子。看顾儿童命运的仁慈天意，总是留意着，极少让哪个孩子被安置在这类干净得可怕的宅子里。

===Original===
But Walter was not at the table, and Ralph had so much anxiety lest his absence should be significant of evil, that he did not venture to inquire after him as he sat there between Mr. and Mrs. White disposing of Aunt Matilda’s cakes with an appetite only justified by his long morning’s ride and the excellence of the brown cakes, the golden honey, and the coffee, enriched, as Aunt Matilda’s always was, with the most generous cream. Aunt Matilda was so absorbed in telling of the doings of the Dorcas Society that she entirely forgot to be surprised at the early hour of Ralph’s arrival. When she had described the number of the garments finished to be sent to the Five Points Mission, or the Home for the Friendless, or the South Sea Islands, I forget which, Ralph thought he saw his chance, while Aunt Matilda was in a benevolent mood, to broach a plan he had been revolving for some time. But when he looked at Aunt Matilda’s immaculate﻿—horribly immaculate﻿—housekeeping, his heart failed him, and he would have said nothing had she not inadvertently opened the door herself.

===Chinese===
可沃尔特不在席上。拉尔夫生怕这缺席是不祥之兆，焦虑得很，坐在怀特先生和怀特太太中间，把玛蒂尔达舅母的煎饼一扫而光，也不敢问起他——这副好胃口，全亏清晨那一路长骑，也全亏那焦黄的煎饼、金黄的蜂蜜和咖啡实在出色，而且玛蒂尔达舅母的咖啡向来慷慨地兑足了奶油。玛蒂尔达舅母正专心致志地讲多加会（Dorcas Society，妇女慈善缝纫会）的事迹，把拉尔夫到得这么早这件事忘了个干净，压根没想到该惊讶一下。等她数说完缝好待寄的衣裳——是要寄给五点区布道会（Five Points Mission），还是「无亲者之家」，还是南太平洋诸岛，我忘了是哪一处——拉尔夫觉得机会来了，趁舅母心情慈善，好提出那个盘算已久的主意。可是一看玛蒂尔达舅母那纤尘不染——干净得吓人——的家政，他又胆怯了；若不是她自己不留神把门打开，他本来一个字也不会提的。

===Original===
“How did you get here so early, Ralph?” and Aunt Matilda’s face was shadowed with a coming rebuke.

“By early rising,” said Ralph. But, seeing the gathering frown on his aunt’s brow, he hastened to tell the story of Shocky as well as he could. Mrs. White did not give way to any impulse toward sympathy until she learned that Shocky was safely housed with Miss Nancy Sawyer.

===Chinese===
「你怎么到得这么早，拉尔夫？」玛蒂尔达舅母的脸上罩起了一层即将发作的责备。

「起得早呗。」拉尔夫说。但一见舅母眉间聚拢的阴云，他赶紧把肖基的事尽可能讲了一遍。直到听说肖基已在南希·索耶小姐家安然住下，怀特太太才让同情心动了那么一动。

===Original===
“Yes, Sister Sawyer has no family cares,” she said by way of smoothing her slightly ruffled complacency, “she has no family cares, and she can do those things. Sometimes I think she lets people impose on her and keep her away from the means of grace, and I spoke to our new preacher about it the last time he was here, and asked him to speak to Sister Sawyer about staying away from the ordinances to wait on everybody, but he is a queer man, and he only said that he supposed Sister Sawyer neglected the inferior ordinances that she might attend to higher ones. But I don’t see any sense in a minister of the gospel calling prayer-meeting a lower ordinance than feeding catnip-tea to Mrs. Brown’s last baby. But hasn’t this little boy﻿—Shocking, or what do you call him?﻿—got any mother?”

“Yes,” said Ralph, “and that was just what I was going to say.” And he proceeded to tell how anxious Shocky was to see his half-blind mother, and actually ventured to wind up his remarks by suggesting that Shocky’s mother be invited to stay over Sunday in Aunt Matilda’s house.

===Chinese===
「是啊，索耶姐妹没有家累，」她说这话，好把自己那点被搅乱的得意抚平，「她没有家累，才做得来那些事。有时候我倒觉得，她是由着人家欺负，把蒙恩的法子都耽搁了；上回我们那位新牧师来，我还跟他说起这事，请他去劝劝索耶姐妹，别为了伺候这个伺候那个，把圣礼都撇下了。可他是个怪人，只说了一句：他料想索耶姐妹疏忽了较低的圣礼，正是为了照管更高的。可我不明白，一个传福音的牧师，把祷告会说成比给布朗太太新生的娃娃喂猫薄荷茶还低一等的圣礼，这有什么道理。不过——这个小男孩——叫肖克，还是怎么称呼来着？——他没有母亲吗？」

「有的，」拉尔夫说，「我正要说的就是这个。」于是他讲肖基多么急着要见他那半瞎的母亲，讲到最后，竟斗胆建议：可否请肖基的母亲到玛蒂尔达舅母家过了礼拜天。

===Original===
“Bless my stars!” said that astounded saint, “fetch a pauper here? What crazy notions you have got! Fetch her here out of the poorhouse? Why, she wouldn’t be fit to sleep in my﻿—” here Aunt Matilda choked. The bare thought of having a pauper in her billowy beds, whose snowy whiteness was frightful to any ordinary mortal, the bare thought of the contagion of the poorhouse taking possession of one of her beds, smothered her. “And then you know sore eyes are very catching.”

Ralph boiled a little. “Aunt Matilda, do you think Dorcas was afraid of sore eyes?”

It was a center shot, and the lawyer-uncle, lawyerlike, enjoyed a good hit. And he enjoyed a hit at his wife best of all, for he never ventured on one himself. But Aunt Matilda felt that a direct reply was impossible. She was not a lawyer but a woman, and so dodged the question by making a countercharge.

===Chinese===
「我的老天爷！」这位惊呆了的圣徒叫起来，「把一个穷人弄到这儿来？你都起了些什么疯念头！把她从贫民院里接出来弄到这儿？哎呀，她怎么配睡在我的——」玛蒂尔达舅母说到这儿噎住了。一想到她那波涛般层叠的被褥里要躺一个贫民——那雪一样的白，寻常凡人看了都心惊——一想到贫民院的「传染」要占据她的一张床，她就透不过气来。「再说你知道的，眼疾是顶容易过人的。」

拉尔夫有点冒火。「玛蒂尔达舅母，您当多加还怕眼疾不成？」

这一箭正中靶心。当律师的舅舅一副律师做派，很欣赏这一记好球。而打在太太身上的好球他看得最过瘾，因为他自己从来不敢来这么一下。可玛蒂尔达舅母觉得正面回答是不可能的了。她不是律师，是女人，于是闪开问题，反控一手。

===Original===
“It seems to me, Ralph, that you have picked up some very low associates. And you go around at night, I am told. You get over here by daylight, and I hear that you have made common cause with a lame soldier who acts as a spy for thieves, and that your running about of night is likely to get you into trouble.”

Ralph was hit this time. “I suppose,” he said, “that you’ve been listening to some of Henry Small’s lies.”

“Why, Ralph, how you talk! The worst sign of all is that you abuse such a young man as Dr. Small, the most exemplary Christian young man in the county. And he is a great friend of yours, for when he was here last week he did not say a word against you, but looked so sorry when your being in trouble was mentioned. Didn’t he, Mr. White?”

===Chinese===
「依我看，拉尔夫，你交上了一些很低贱的伙伴。听说你还夜里到处走。天一亮你就赶到了这儿；我又听说，你跟一个给贼当眼线的瘸腿老兵结成了同伙，还听说你这夜里乱跑，早晚会惹祸上身。」

这回轮到拉尔夫挨了一下。「我看，」他说，「您这是听了亨利·斯莫尔的什么谎话了吧。」

「哎呀，拉尔夫，看你说的！最糟糕的征兆，就是你竟毁谤斯莫尔医生这样的人——全县最模范的基督徒青年。他还是你的至交呢：上礼拜他来这儿，一句你的坏话没说，提起你身陷麻烦，还一脸的难过。是不是，怀特先生？」

===Original===
Mr. White, as in duty bound, said yes, but he said yes in a cool, lawyerlike way, which showed that he did not take quite so much stock in Dr. Small as his wife did. This was a comfort to Ralph, who sat picturing to himself the silent flattery which Dr. Small’s eyes paid to his Aunt Matilda, and the quiet expression of pain that would flit across his face when Ralph’s name was mentioned. And never until that moment had Hartsook understood how masterful Small’s artifices were. He had managed to elevate himself in Mrs. White’s estimation and to destroy Ralph at the same time, and had managed to do both by a contraction of the eyebrows!

But the silence was growing painful and Ralph thought to break it and turn the current of talk from himself by asking after Mrs. White’s son.

“Where is Walter?”

===Chinese===
怀特先生照着本分说了声是，不过那声「是」说得又冷静又像律师，听得出他对斯莫尔医生的信心并不像太太那么足。这让拉尔夫得了点安慰。他坐在那儿，心里描画着斯莫尔医生的眼睛怎样向玛蒂尔达舅母默默献媚，描画着一提到拉尔夫的名字、他脸上便掠过的那一丝沉痛。直到那一刻，哈茨克才明白斯莫尔的手法何等老辣：他设法在怀特太太的估价里抬高自己，同时又毁掉拉尔夫——而且两件事一并办成，全凭眉毛一皱！

可沉默越来越叫人难受，拉尔夫想打破它，把话头从自己身上引开，便问起居特太太的儿子。

「沃尔特呢？」

===Original===
“Oh! Walter’s doing well. He went down to Clifty three weeks ago to study medicine with Henry Small. He seems so fond of the doctor, and the doctor is such an excellent man, you know, and I have strong hopes that Wallie will be led to see the error of his ways by his association with Henry. I suppose he would have gone to see you but for the unfavorable reports that he heard. I hope, Ralph, you too will make the friendship of Dr. Small. And for the sake of your poor, dead mother”﻿—here Aunt Matilda endeavored to show some emotion﻿—“for the sake of your poor dead mother﻿—”

But Ralph heard no more. The buckwheat-cakes had lost their flavor. He remembered that the colt had not yet had his oats, and so, in the very midst of Aunt Matilda’s affecting allusion to his mother, like a stiff-necked reprobate that he was, Ralph Hartsook rose abruptly from the table, put on his hat, and went out toward the stable.

“I declare,” said Mrs. White, descending suddenly from her high moral standpoint, “I declare that boy has stepped right on the threshold of the back door,” and she stuffed her white handkerchief into her pocket, and took down the floor-cloth to wipe off the imperceptible blemish left by Ralph’s boot-heels. And Mr. White followed his nephew to the stable to request that he would be a little careful what he did about anybody in the poorhouse, as any trouble with the Joneses might defeat Mr. White’s nomination to the judgeship of the Court of Common Pleas.

===Chinese===
「哦！沃尔特挺好。他三个礼拜前下克利夫蒂去了，跟亨利·斯莫尔学医。他好像跟医生处得很好，那医生又是那么优秀的人，你知道的；我满心指望沃利跟亨利结交之后，能被引着看清自己路子上的错处。我想他要不是听了那些不利的传闻，本来会去看你的。我希望，拉尔夫，你也跟斯莫尔医生交个朋友。看在你可怜的、去世的母亲份上」——说到这里，玛蒂尔达舅母努力做出一点感情——「看在你可怜的去世的母亲份上——」

可拉尔夫一个字也没再听进去。荞麦煎饼已经没了滋味。他想起马驹还没吃上燕麦。于是，正当玛蒂尔达舅母动情地提到他母亲、而他自己又偏偏是个死不回头的堕落者的当口，拉尔夫·哈茨克猛地从桌边站起，戴上帽子，出门朝马棚去了。

「我说，」怀特太太说，忽然从她那崇高的道德高地跌落下来，「我说，那孩子踩在后门的门槛上了。」说着把白手绢塞回口袋，取下抹地布，去擦拉尔夫靴跟留下的那一点肉眼看不见的污迹。怀特先生则跟着外甥来到马棚，请他凡事关涉贫民院里的人时稍微留点神——因为跟琼斯家闹出任何纠纷，都可能坏了怀特先生获提名当普通诉讼法院法官的前程。

## Preface / 序言

===Original===
I may as well confess, what it would be affectation to conceal, that I am more than pleased with the generous reception accorded to this story as a serial in the columns of *Hearth and Home*. It has been in my mind since I was a Hoosier boy to do something toward describing life in the back-country districts of the Western States. It used to be a matter of no little jealousy with us, I remember, that the manners, customs, thoughts, and feelings of New England country people filled so large a place in books, while our life, not less interesting, not less romantic, and certainly not less filled with humorous and grotesque material, had no place in literature. It was as though we were shut out of good society. And, with the single exception of Alice Cary, perhaps, our Western writers did not dare speak of the West otherwise than as the unreal world to which Cooper’s lively imagination had given birth.

I had some anxiety lest Western readers should take offence at my selecting what must always seem an exceptional phase of life to those who have grown up in the more refined regions of the West. But nowhere has the Schoolmaster been received more kindly than in his own country and among his own people.

===Chinese===
我不妨索性招认——藏着掖着反倒矫情——这部小说在《炉边与家》（Hearth and Home）杂志上连载时所得的慷慨接待，叫我心满意足，何止心满意足。打从我还是个胡希尔乡下孩子的时候，我心里就存着一桩事：把西部各州穷乡僻壤学区里的生活描写一番。我记得，我们从前总有些眼热：书里装满了新英格兰乡下人的规矩、风俗、心思和情感，而我们的生活——不见得没趣，不见得不浪漫，诙谐滑稽的材料也决计不见得少——在文学里却没有一席之地。这就好比我们叫上流社会拒之门外。而且，除爱丽丝·卡里（Alice Cary）这一个例外，或许吧，我们西部的作家们写起西部来，也只敢把它当作库珀那活跃的想象力生出来的那个虚构世界来写。

我也曾有些提心吊胆，唯恐西部读者见怪：我挑来写的这种生活，在西部较文雅地方长大的人眼里，总归要算个例外。可是不管在哪里，「校长」受到的款待，都不及在它自己的乡土上、自己人中间那样亲厚。

===Original===
Some of those who have spoken generous words of the Schoolmaster and his friends have suggested that the story is an autobiography. But it is not, save in the sense in which every work of art is an autobiography: in that it is the result of the experience and observation of the writer. Readers will therefore bear in mind that not Ralph nor Bud nor Brother Sodom nor Dr. Small represents the writer, nor do I appear, as Talleyrand said of Madame de Staël, “disguised as a woman,” in the person of Hannah or Mirandy. Some of the incidents have been drawn from life; none of them, I believe, from my own. I should like to be considered a member of the Church of the Best Licks, however.

It has been in my mind to append some remarks, philological and otherwise, upon the dialect, but Professor Lowell’s admirable and erudite preface to the *Biglow Papers* must be the despair of everyone who aspires to write on Americanisms. To Mr. Lowell belongs the distinction of being the only one of our most eminent authors and the only one of our most eminent scholars who has given careful attention to American dialects.
