His family was represented by Shocky, who had come, doubtless, to get a glimpse of Hannah, not to hear Mr. Bosaw preach. In fact, few were thinking of the religious service. They went to church as a common resort to hear the news, and to find out what was the current sensation.

On this particular morning there seemed to be some unusual excitement. Ralph perceived it as he rode up. An excited crowd, even though it be at a church-door on Sunday morning, can not conceal its agitation. Ralph deposited Miss Hawkins on the stile, and then got down himself, and paid her the closest attention to the door. This attention was for Bud’s benefit. But Bud only stood with his hands in his pockets, scowling worse than ever. Ralph did not go in at the door. It was not the Flat Creek custom. The men gossiped outside, while the women chatted within. Whatever may have been the cause of the excitement, Ralph could not get at it. When he entered a little knot of people they became embarrassed, the group dissolved, and its component parts joined other companies. What had the current of conversation to do with him? He overheard Pete Jones saying that the blamed old wooden leg was in it anyhow. He’d been seen goin’ home at two in the mornin’. And he could name somebody else ef he choosed. But it was best to clean out one at a time. And just then there was a murmur: “Meetin’s taken up.” And the masculine element filled the empty half of the “hewed-log” church.

===Chinese===
让拉尔夫吃惊的是，弗拉特溪竟倾巢出动上礼拜堂来了。人人都在——米恩斯家、琼斯家、班塔家，其余各家各户。弗拉特溪的人像是全到齐了，只缺那个装木腿的老编篮匠。他家由肖基代表出席——这孩子来，八成是为着瞧一眼汉娜，不是为听博索先生讲道。说实在的，也没几个人把心思放在礼拜上。他们上教堂，就当上个公共热闹去处，听听新闻，瞧瞧眼下的新鲜事儿是什么。

单说这个早晨，似乎有一阵不寻常的骚动。拉尔夫骑马过来时就觉出来了。一群激动的人，哪怕聚在礼拜天的教堂门口，也藏不住他们的不安。拉尔夫把霍金斯小姐安顿在上马的踏凳上，自己再翻身下来，一路殷勤周到地把她护送到门口。这份殷勤是做给巴德看的。可巴德只是双手插在兜里站在那儿，眉头拧得比平时更凶。拉尔夫没有从正门进去。这不是弗拉特溪的规矩。男人们在外头扯闲篇，女人们在里头唠嗑。不管这阵骚动为的是什么，拉尔夫探不着底。他一凑近一小堆人，那堆人就发窘，圈子散了，成员们各自归入别堆。眼下的话头跟他有什么相干？他听见皮特·琼斯在那儿说，反正那个该死的木头腿也脱不了干系。有人瞧见他半夜两点往家走来着。他乐意的话，还能点出另一个人来。不过还是一个一个收拾的好。正说着，人群里起了一阵嗡嗡：「礼拜开始喽。」男人们便灌满了这座砍削原木教堂里空着的那半边。

===Original===
When Ralph saw Hannah looking utterly dejected, his heart smote him, and the great struggle set in again. Had it not been for the thought of the other battle, and the comforting presence of the Helper, I fear Bud’s interests would have fared badly. But Ralph, with the spirit of a martyr, resolved to wait until he knew what the result of Bud’s suit should be, and whether, indeed, the young Goliath had prior claims, as he evidently thought he had. He turned hopefully to the sermon, determined to pick up any crumbs of comfort that might fall from Mr. Bosaw’s meager table.

In reporting a single specimen passage of Mr. Bosaw’s sermon, I shall not take the liberty which Thucydides and other ancient historians did, of making the sermon and putting it into the hero’s mouth, but shall give that which can be vouched for.

===Chinese===
拉尔夫看见汉娜一脸全然的沮丧，心里一阵自责，那场大挣扎又开始了。要不是心里惦着另一场争战，又有那位帮助者令人安慰的同在，我怕巴德的利益就要遭殃了。可拉尔夫怀着殉道者的精神，打定主意等到水落石出：巴德的追求究竟成与不成；这位年轻的大力士，是不是真像他显然自以为的那样，握有优先的权利。他满怀指望地转向讲道，下定决心：博索先生那点寒酸筵席上但凡掉下什么安慰的碎渣，他都要捡起来。

在转述博索先生讲道的一个标本段落时，我可不敢学修昔底德和别的古代史家那样放肆——自己编出一篇讲道来塞到主人公嘴里——我只给有据可查的。

===Original===
“You see, my respective hearers,” he began—but alas! I can never picture to you the rich red nose, the see sawing gestures, the nasal resonance, the sniffle, the melancholy minor key, and all that. “My respective hearers-ah, you see-ah as how-ah as my tex’-ah says that the ox-ah knoweth his owner-ah, and-ah the ass-ah his master’s crib-ah. A‑h‑h! Now, my respective hearers-ah, they’re a mighty sight of resemblance-ah atwext men-ah and oxen-ah” [Ralph could not help reflecting that there was a mighty sight of resemblance between some men and asses. But the preacher did not see this analogy. It lay too close to him], “bekase-ah, you see, men-ah is mighty like oxen-ah. Fer they’s a tremengious defference-ah atwixt defferent oxen-ah, jest as thar is atwext defferent men-ah; fer the ox knoweth-ah his owner-ah, and the ass-ah, his master’s crib-ah. Now, my respective hearers-ah” [the preacher’s voice here grew mellow, and the succeeding sentences were in the most pathetic and lugubrious tones], “you all know-ah that your humble speaker-ah has got-ah jest the best yoke of steers-ah in this township-ah.” [Here Betsey Short shook the floor with a suppressed titter.] “They a’n’t no sech steers as them air two of mine-ah in this whole kedentry-ah. Them crack oxen over at Clifty-ah ha’n’t a patchin’ to mine-ah. Fer the ox knoweth his owner-ah and the ass-ah his master’s crib-ah.

===Chinese===
「你们瞧，俺各位可敬的听众啊，」他开了头——可惜呀！那红喷喷的酒糟鼻子，那一俯一仰的姿势，那鼻腔的共鸣，那抽鼻子的声音，那忧伤的小调，还有那一整套，我是万万没法给你们描画出来的。「俺各位可敬的听众啊，你们瞧啊，正像啊，俺的经文啊说的，牛啊，认识它的主人啊，驴啊，认识主人的槽啊。啊——！瞧啊，俺各位可敬的听众啊，人啊跟牛啊，可是像得没边儿了啊」［拉尔夫不由得暗想，有些人和驴也真是像得没边儿。可惜传道人瞧不见这个比方，它离他太近了］，「因为啊，你们瞧，人啊就是跟牛像得不得了啊。因为不同的牛啊跟不同的牛啊，有天大的分别啊，就跟不同的人啊彼此有分别一样啊；因为牛认识它的主人啊，驴啊，认识主人的槽啊。瞧啊，俺各位可敬的听众啊」［说到这儿，传道人的嗓音圆润起来，以下各句用的是最凄婉、最悲苦的调子］，「你们都知道啊，你们这位不起眼的讲话人啊，养着啊，本乡里顶好的一对犍牛啊。」［这时贝琪·肖特憋着的一声嗤笑震得地板直颤。］「这么好的犍牛啊，这整个的国啊，再没有第二对比得上俺那两头啊。克利夫蒂那边那些顶尖的牛啊，给俺的提鞋都不配啊。因为牛认识它的主人啊，驴啊认识主人的槽啊。

===Original===
“Now, my respective hearers-ah, they’s a right smart sight of defference-ah atwext them air two oxen-ah, jest like they is atwext defferent men-ah. Fer-ah” [here the speaker grew earnest, and sawed the air, from this to the close, in a most frightful way], “fer-ah, you see-ah, when I go out-ah in the mornin’-ah to yoke-ah up-ah them air steers-ah, and I says-ah, ‘Wo, Berry-ah! *Wo, Berry-ah!* **Wo, Berry-ah**,’ why Berry-ah jest stands stock still-ah and don’t hardly breathe-ah while I put on the yoke-ah, and put in the bow-ah, and put in the key-ah, fer, my brethering-ah and sistering-ah, the ox knoweth his owner-ah, and the ass-ah his master’s crib-ah. Hal-le-lu-ger-ah!

“But-ah, my hearers-ah, but-ah when I stand at t’other eend of the yoke-ah, and say, ‘Come, Buck-ah! *Come, Buck-ah!* **Come, Buck-ah!** **Come, Buck-ah!**’ why what do you think-ah? Buck-ah, that ornery ole Buck-ah, ’stid of comin’ right along-ah and puttin’ his neck under-ah, acts jest like some men-ah what is fools-ah. Buck-ah jest kinder sorter stands off-ah, and kinder sorter puts his head down-ah this ’ere way-ah, and kinder looks mad-ah, and says, Boo-*oo*-**oo**-**oo**-*ah*!”

===Chinese===
「瞧啊，俺各位可敬的听众啊，那两头牛啊彼此之间，也有老大一截子分别啊，就跟不同的人啊彼此有分别一样。因为啊」［说到这儿，讲道人激动起来，从这时起到收场，一路把空气锯得吓死人］，「因为啊，你们瞧啊，清早晨啊，俺出去啊，套啊，那对犍牛啊，俺就喊啊：『吁，贝利啊！*吁，贝利啊！***吁——贝利啊——』，嘿，贝利啊就钉在那儿一动不动啊，气儿都不敢喘啊，由着俺把轭套上啊，把轭弓安上啊，把键子插上啊，因为啊，俺的弟兄们啊，姊妹们啊，牛认识它的主人啊，驴啊认识主人的槽啊。哈——路——利——嗐——啊！

「可是啊，俺的听众们啊，可是啊，等俺站到轭的另一头啊，喊：『来，巴克啊！*来，巴克啊！***来，巴克啊！***来，巴克啊！**』嘿，你们猜怎么着啊？巴克啊，那头赖皮的老公牛巴克啊，不痛痛快快地走过来啊，把脖子往轭底下一伸啊，倒学起有些蠢货男人的做派来。巴克啊就是那么蔫不唧地躲着啊，那么蔫不唧地把脑袋低下去啊，照这么个样儿啊，还蔫不唧地绷着脸啊，喊一声：哞——哞——哞——啊！」

===Original===
Alas! Hartsook found no spiritual edification there, and he was in no mood to be amused. And so, while the sermon drew on through two dreary hours, he forgot the preacher in noticing a bright green lizard which, having taken up its winter quarters behind the tin candlestick that hung just back of the preacher’s head, had been deceived by the genial warmth coming from the great box-stove, and now ran out two or three feet from his shelter, looking down upon the red-nosed preacher in a most confidential and amusing manner. Sometimes he would retreat behind the candlestick, which was not twelve inches from the preacher’s head, and then rush out again. At each reappearance Betsey Short would stuff her handkerchief into her mouth and shake in a most distressing way. Shocky wondered what the lizard was winking at the preacher about. And Miss Martha thought that it reminded her of a lizard that she see at the East, the time she was to Bosting, in a jar of alcohol in the Natural History Rooms. The Squire was not disappointed in his anticipation that Mr. Bosaw would attack his denomination with some fury. In fact, the old preacher outdid himself in his violent indignation at “these people that follow Campbell-ah, that thinks-ah that obejience-ah will save ’em-ah and that belongs-ah to temp’rince societies-ah and Sunday-schools-ah, and them air things-ah, that’s not ortherized in the Bible-ah, but comes of the devil-ah, and takes folks as belongs to ’em to hell-ah.”

===Chinese===
唉！哈特索克在那儿没寻着半点灵性的造就，他也没兴致发笑。于是，讲道在两个凄闷的钟头里往前磨，他把传道人忘在了脑后，只顾端详一只翠绿的小蜥蜴。这蜥蜴把过冬的窝安在传道人脑袋正后上方挂着的白铁烛台后头，让大铁炉子透出来的暖意骗了，如今从藏身处跑出两三英尺，居高临下地望着那个红鼻子传道人，那副推心置腹的滑稽相就别提了。它时而缩回烛台后头——那儿离传道人的脑袋不到一英尺——时而又冲出来。它每冒一回头，贝琪·肖特就把手绢往嘴里一塞，抖得死去活来。肖基纳闷，蜥蜴朝传道人眨巴眼是为个啥。玛莎小姐则想起来了，这叫她想起她在东部见过的一只蜥蜴——就是她上波士顿去的那回，在自然史陈列馆一瓶酒精里泡着的那只。治安官没白指望：博索先生果然要朝着他的教派轰一通。果然，老传道人抡圆了怒火，痛骂「那些跟从坎伯尔啊的人，那些以为啊顺从啊能救他们啊、加入了啊戒酒会啊和主日学啊还有这些个名堂啊的人，这些名堂啊圣经里没有啊，不是圣经啊授权的啊，是从魔鬼那儿来的啊，把属于它们的人统统带进地狱啊」，这一回真是超水平发挥。

===Original===
As they came out the door Ralph rallied enough to remark: “He did attack your people, Squire.”

“Oh, yes,” said the Squire. “Didn’t you see the Sarpent inspirin’ him?”

But the long, long hours were ended and Ralph got on the clay-bank mare and rode up alongside the stile whence Miss Martha mounted. And as he went away with a heavy heart, he overheard Pete Jones call out to somebody:

“We’ll tend to his case a Christmas.” Christmas was two days off.

And Miss Martha remarked with much trepidation that poor Pearson would have to leave. She’d always been afraid that would be the end of it. It reminded her of something she heard at the East, the time she was down to Bosting.

===Chinese===
出门的时候，拉尔夫强打起精神搭了句话：「他可真轰了你们的人，治安官。」

「哦，可不，」治安官说。「你没瞧见那条古蛇在他身上附体么？」

漫长漫长的几个钟头总算熬到了头，拉尔夫跨上黄沙母马，骑到踏凳旁边，玛莎小姐从那儿上了马。他怀着沉重的心走开时，听见皮特·琼斯朝什么人嚷了一句：

「到圣诞节俺们就料理他的案子。」离圣诞节还有两天。

玛莎小姐则战战兢兢地议论道，可怜的皮尔逊怕是待不成了。她一直就担心，早晚是这个下场。这叫她想起在东部听见过的一件事——就是她下波士顿去的那回。

## VIII The Struggle in the Dark / 八 黑暗中的挣扎

===Original===
It was a long, lonesome, fearful night that the schoolmaster passed, lying with nerves on edge and eyes wide open in that comfortless bed in the “furdest corner” of the loft of Pete Jones’s house, shivering with cold, while the light snow that was falling sifted in upon the ragged patchwork quilt that covered him. Nerves broken by sleeplessness imagine many things, and for the first hour Ralph felt sure that Pete would cut his throat before morning.

And you, friend Callow, who have blunted your palate by swallowing the Cayenne pepper of the penny-dreadfuls, you wish me to make this night exciting by a hand-to-hand contest between Ralph and a robber. You would like it better if there were a trapdoor. There’s nothing so convenient as a trapdoor, unless it be a subterranean passage. And you’d like something of that sort just here. It’s so pleasant to have one’s hair stand on end, you know, when one is safe from danger to one’s self. But if you want each individual hair to bristle with such a “Struggle in the Dark,” you can buy trapdoors and subterranean passages dirt cheap at the next newsstand. But it was, indeed, a real and terrible “Struggle in the Dark” that Ralph fought out at Pete Jones’s.

===Chinese===
校长在皮特·琼斯家阁楼「顶里头那个角」那张不舒服的床上，熬过了漫长、凄清、提心吊胆的一夜。他神经紧绷，眼睛睁得老大，冷得直打哆嗦，飘落的细雪从缝里筛进来，落在盖着他的那床破烂百家布被上。失眠弄坏的神经会想出许多事来，头一个钟头里，拉尔夫一直认定，天亮以前皮特准会来割他的喉咙。

还有你，卡洛朋友，你让一便士惊险小说那股辣椒粉把舌头烧钝了，你巴不得我安排拉尔夫跟一个强盗赤手空拳干上一场，好叫这一夜热闹热闹。要是再有一道暗门，你更称心。天底下再没有比暗门更便当的东西了，除非是地下暗道。你正巴不得在这儿来上这么一手。自己的脑袋万无一失时，叫汗毛根根倒竖，你知道，那有多惬意。不过你要是想叫每一根头发都为这样一场「黑暗中的挣扎」竖起来，隔壁书报摊上的暗门和地下暗道便宜得很，尽管去买。可是拉尔夫在皮特·琼斯家打完的，确是一场实实在在、骇人的「黑暗中的挣扎」。

===Original===
When he had vanquished his fears of personal violence by reminding himself that it would be folly for Jones to commit murder in his own house, the question of Bud and Hannah took the uppermost place in his thoughts. And as the image of Hannah spelling against the master came up to him, as the memory of the walk, the talk, the box-elder tree, and all the rest took possession of him, it seemed to Ralph that his very life depended upon his securing her love. He would shut his teeth like the jaws of a bulldog, and all Bud’s muscles should not prevail over his resolution and his stratagems.

It was easy to persuade himself that this was right. Hannah ought not to throw herself away on Bud Means. Men of some culture always play their conceit off against their consciences. To a man of literary habits it usually seems to be a great boon that he confers on a woman when he gives her his love. Reasoning thus, Ralph had fixed his resolution, and if the night had been shorter, or sleep possible, the color of his life might have been changed.

===Chinese===
他提醒自己，琼斯若在自己家里行凶杀人，那真是蠢到家了——靠这个，他把对人身伤害的恐惧压了下去；于是巴德和汉娜的问题便又占了上风。当汉娜跟先生拼字的那副形象浮上来，当那回散步、那场谈话、那棵梣叶槭树和其余种种攫住了他，拉尔夫便觉得，自己的性命就系在能不能赢得她的爱上。他要像斗牛犬似的咬紧牙关，巴德浑身是腱子肉，也压不垮他的决心和计谋。

说服自己这是对的，并不费事。汉娜不该把自己糟蹋给巴德·米恩斯。有几分教养的男人，总能拿自满去糊弄良心。对惯于舞文弄墨的人来说，把自己的爱情给一个女人，通常就像是赐了她天大的恩典。拉尔夫这么一推理，决心便定了。倘若那一夜短些，或者睡得着，他一生的颜色兴许就换了一种。

===Original===
But some time along in the tedious hours came the memory of his childhood, the words of his mother, the old Bible stories, the aspiration after nobility of spirit, the solemn resolutions to be true to his conscience. These angels of the memory came flocking back before the animal, the bull-doggedness, had “set,” as workers in plaster say. He remembered the story of David and Nathan, and it seemed to him that he, with all his abilities and ambitions and prospects, was about to rob Bud of the one ewe-lamb, the only thing he had to rejoice in in his life. In getting Hannah, he would make himself unworthy of Hannah. And then there came to him a vision of the supreme value of a true character; how it was better than success, better than to be loved, better than heaven. And how near he had been to missing it! And how certain he was, when these thoughts should fade, to miss it! He was as one fighting for a great prize who feels his strength failing and is sure of defeat.

===Chinese===
可是在这熬人的时辰里的某一刻，童年的记忆来了：母亲的话，古老的圣经故事，对高尚灵魂的向往，对得起良心的庄严誓愿。这些记忆的天使成群飞了回来——赶在那兽性、那股牛劲儿，照抹石膏的匠人的说法，「凝住」之前。他想起大卫和拿单的故事，觉得自己这个满腹才华、野心和前程的人，正要抢走巴德唯一的羊羔——他这辈子唯一可以欢喜的东西。得了汉娜，他就要变得不配得汉娜。接着，他又看见真正品格的无上价值：它胜过成功，胜过被人爱，胜过天堂。而这一样，他方才差一点儿就错过了！而且他清楚，等这些念头一淡，他准会错过！他就像一个争夺大奖的人，眼看力气使尽，败局已定。

===Original===
This was the real, awful “Struggle in the Dark.” A human soul fighting with heaven in sight, but certain of slipping inevitably into hell! It was the same old battle. The Image of God fought with the Image of the Devil. It was the same fight that Paul described so dramatically when he represented the Spirit as contending with the Flesh. Paul also called this dreadful something the Old Adam, and I suppose Darwin would call it the remains of the Wild Beast. But call it what you will, it is the battle that every well-endowed soul must fight at some point. And to Ralph it seemed that the final victory of the Evil, the Old Adam, the Flesh, the Wild Beast, the Devil, was certain. For, was not the pure, unconscious face of Hannah on the Devil’s side? And so the battle had just as well be given up at once, for it must be lost in the end.

But to Ralph, lying there in the still darkness, with his conscience as wide awake as if it were the Day of Doom, there seemed something so terrible in this overflow of the better nature which he knew to be inevitable as soon as the voice of conscience became blunted, that he looked about for help. He did not at first think of God; but there came into his thoughts the memory of a travel-worn Galilean peasant, hungry, sleepy, weary, tempted, tried, like other men, but having a strange, divine Victory in him by which everything evil was vanquished at his coming. He remembered how He had reached out a Hand to every helpless one, how He was the Helper of every weak one. And out of the depths of his soul he cried to the Helper, and found comfort. Not victory, but, what is better, strength. And so, without a thought of the niceties of theological distinctions, without dreaming that it was the beginning of a religious experience, he found what he needed, help. And the Helper gave His beloved sleep.

===Chinese===
这才是那场真实而可怕的「黑暗中的挣扎」。一个灵魂，望得见天国，却在无可避免地滑向地狱！这是那场古老的争战。上帝的形象与魔鬼的形象相搏。这就是保罗用那般戏剧性的笔法描画的争战——他说圣灵与情欲相争。保罗还把这可怕的东西叫作老亚当；我想达尔文会管它叫野兽的残余。可随你怎么叫，这是每个天资丰厚的灵魂都免不了要在某处打的一仗。而在拉尔夫看来，恶——老亚当、情欲、野兽、魔鬼——的最后得胜，已成定局。因为，汉娜那张纯洁无猜的脸，不正是站在魔鬼一边么？那么这一仗不妨趁早撂下，反正终归要输。

可是对躺在死寂黑暗里的拉尔夫来说——他的良心大睁着眼，仿佛就是末日审判——他明白，等良心的声音一钝，这更好的天性便必然这样溃堤，这里头有一种说不出的可怕，于是他四下里寻求帮助。他起初没有想到上帝；可是他想起了一个风尘仆仆的加利利庄稼人：饥饿、困倦、疲乏、受试探、遭熬炼，跟旁人一样，身子里却存着一种奇异的、属天的得胜，凡邪恶的，他一到，便都被克伏。他想起祂怎样向每一个无助者伸出手来，怎样做一切软弱者的帮助者。于是他从灵魂的深处向那位帮助者呼求，并且得了安慰。不是得胜——是比得胜更好的东西：力量。就这样，没有半点细抠神学名目的念头，也做梦想不到这是一场宗教经历的起头，他找到了他所需要的：帮助。而那位帮助者，叫祂所亲爱的安然睡了。

## XXXI The Trial Concluded / 三十一 审判收场

===Original===
I do not know how much interest the “gentle reader” may feel in Bud. But I venture to hope that there are some Buddhists among my readers who will wish the contradictoriness of his actions explained. The first dash of disappointment had well-nigh upset him. And when a man concludes to throw overboard his good resolutions, he always seeks to avoid the witness of those resolutions. Hence Bud, after that distressful Tuesday evening on which Miss Martha had given him “the sack,” wished to see Ralph less than anyone else. And yet when he came to suspect Small’s villainy, his whole nature revolted at it. But having broken with Ralph, he thought it best to maintain an attitude of apparent hostility, that he might act as a detective, and, perhaps, save his friend from the mischief that threatened him. As soon as he heard of Ralph’s arrest he determined to make Walter Johnson tell his own secret in court, because he knew that it would be best for Ralph that Walter should tell it. Bud’s telling at second hand would not be conclusive. And he sincerely desired to save Walter from prison. For Walter Johnson was the victim of Dr. Small, or of Dr. Small and such novels as *The Pirate’s Bride*, *Claude Duval*, *The Wild Rover of the West Indies*, and the cheap biographies of such men as Murrell. Small found him with his imagination inflamed by the history of such heroes, and opened to him the path to glory for which he longed.

===Chinese===
我不知道「温和的读者」对巴德怀有多少兴致。可我冒昧指望，读者里总也有几位「巴德派」（Buddhists，巴德 Bud 的信众），巴不得我把他的行止前后那点矛盾解释个明白。头一记失望的猛浪，差点儿把他掀翻。一个人既决定把自个儿的好决心扔下船，总要想法躲开那些决心的见证人。所以，自打那个伤心的星期二晚上——玛莎小姐把他「打发」了的那个晚上——起，巴德最不想见的就是拉尔夫。可等到他疑心到斯莫尔的奸恶，他整个的天性都造了反。只是既已同拉尔夫掰了，他觉得还是摆出一副表面上作对的架势为上，好充当一名暗探，兴许还能把朋友从临头的祸事里救出来。他一听说拉尔夫被押，就打定主意要叫沃尔特·约翰逊到法庭上自吐隐情，因为他知道，这事由沃尔特亲口讲出来，对拉尔夫顶好。巴德转二手讲一遍，算不得铁证。何况他还真心实意，要救沃尔特不进监牢。因为沃尔特·约翰逊是斯莫尔大夫的牺牲品——或者说是斯莫尔大夫，外加《海盗的新娘》（The Pirate’s Bride）、《克劳德·杜瓦尔》（Claude Duval）、《西印度群岛的流浪汉》（The Wild Rover of the West Indies）那一类小说，外加默雷尔（Murrell）之流的便宜传记，合伙造出来的牺牲品。斯莫尔寻着他的时候，他正叫这类英雄的行传烧得满脑子烈火，斯莫尔便替他打开了那条他渴慕已久的通往荣光的路。

===Original===
The whole morning after Ralph’s arrest Bud was working on Walter’s conscience and his fears. The poor fellow, unable to act for himself, was torn asunder between the old ascendency of Small and the new ascendency of Bud Means. Bud finally frightened him, by the fear of the penitentiary, into going to the place of trial. But once inside the door, and once in sight of Small, who was more to him than God, or, rather, more to him than the devil—for the devil was Walter’s God, or, perhaps, I should say, Walter’s God was a devil—once in sight of Small, he refused to move an inch farther. And Bud, after all his perseverance, was about to give up in sheer despair.

Fortunately, just at that moment Small’s desire to relieve himself from the taint of suspicion and to crush Ralph as completely as possible, made him overshoot the mark by asking that Walter be called to the stand, as we have before recounted. He knew that he had no tool so supple as the cowardly Walter. In the very language of the request, he had given Walter an intimation of what he wanted him to swear to. Walter listened to Small’s words as to his doom. He felt that he should die of indecision. The perdition of a man of his stamp is to have to make up his mind. Such men generally fall back on someone more positive, and take all their resolutions ready-made. But here Walter must decide for himself. For the constable was already calling his name; the court, the spectators, and, most of all, Dr. Small, were waiting for him. He moved forward mechanically through the dense crowd, Bud following part of the way to whisper, “Tell the truth or go to penitentiary.” Walter shook and shivered at this. The witness with difficulty held up his hand long enough to be sworn.

===Chinese===
拉尔夫被押之后的那整个早晨，巴德都在沃尔特的良心和恐惧上下工夫。这可怜的家伙自个儿做不得主，叫斯莫尔的旧威势和巴德·米恩斯的新威势撕成了两半。巴德末了拿感化院吓住了他，把他吓到了开庭的地方。可一进门，一望见斯莫尔——斯莫尔在他心里比上帝还大，或者不如说，比魔鬼还大，因为魔鬼正是沃尔特的上帝，也许我该说，沃尔特的上帝是个魔鬼——一望见斯莫尔，他一寸也不肯再挪了。巴德百般死缠，末了简直要撒手绝望了。

幸而恰在那时，斯莫尔一心要洗脱自个儿的嫌疑、要把拉尔夫往死里踩，竟叫他把箭射过了头，主动请求传沃尔特上证人席——这一节我们方才已经讲过。他知道，再没有比怯懦的沃尔特更顺手的工具了。就在那请求的措辞里，他已经把要他赌什么咒的底，递给了沃尔特。沃尔特听着斯莫尔的话，如同听着自己的末日判决。他觉得自己要死在拿不定主意上了。他这一号人的沉沦，就沉沦在非自个儿拿主意不可。这种人平常总是靠到一个更硬气的人身上，主意一律取现成的。可这回沃尔特非自个儿裁断不可。因为差役已经在点他的名；法庭、看客，顶要紧的是斯莫尔大夫，都在等他。他木然地穿过密不透风的人群往前挪，巴德跟了半截路，凑到耳边说：「讲真话，不然进感化院。」沃尔特听得又抖又颤。证人好容易把一只手举到能宣完誓的工夫。

===Original===
“Please tell the court,” said Bronson, “whether you know anything of the whereabouts of Dr. Small on the night of the robbery at Peter Schroeder’s.”

Small had detected Walter’s agitation, and, taking alarm, had edged his way around so as to stand full in Walter’s sight, and there, with keen, magnetic eye on the weak orbs of the young man, he was able to assume his old position, and sway the fellow absolutely.

“On the night of the robbery”—Walter’s voice was weak, but he seemed to be reading his answer out of Small’s eyes—“on the night of the robbery Dr. Small came home before—” here the witness stopped and shook and shivered again. For Bud, detecting the effect of Small’s gaze, had pushed his great hulk in front of Small, and had fastened his eyes on Walter with a look that said, “Tell the truth or go to penitentiary.”

“I can’t, I can’t. O God! What shall I do?” the witness exclaimed, answering the look of Bud. For it seemed to him that Bud had spoken. To the people and the court this agitation was inexplicable. Squire Hawkins’ wig got awry, his glass eye turned in toward his nose, and he had great difficulty in keeping his teeth from falling out. The excitement became painfully intense. Ralph was on his feet, looking at the witness, and feeling that somehow Bud and Dr. Small—his good angel and his demon—were playing an awful game, of which he was the stake. The crowd swayed to and fro, but remained utterly silent, waiting to hear the least whisper from the witness, who stood trembling a moment with his hands over his face, and then fainted.

The fainting of a person in a crowd is a signal for everybody else to make fools of themselves. There was a rush toward the fainting man, there was a cry for water. Everybody asked everybody else to open the window, and everybody wished everybody else to stand back and give him air. But nobody opened the window, and nobody stood back. The only perfectly cool man in the room was Small. With a quiet air of professional authority he pushed forward and felt the patient’s pulse, remarking to the court that he thought it was a sudden attack of fever with delirium. When Walter revived, Dr. Small would have removed him, but Ralph insisted that his testimony should be heard. Under pretense of watching his patient, Small kept close to him. And Walter began the same old story about Dr. Small’s having arrived at the office before eleven o’clock, when Bud came up behind the doctor and fastened his eyes on the witness with the same significant look, and Walter, with visions of the penitentiary before him halted, stammered, and seemed about to faint again.

===Chinese===
「请你告诉法庭，」布朗森说，「彼得·施罗德家失盗那天夜里，斯莫尔大夫的行踪，你可知道些什么。」

斯莫尔早瞧出沃尔特的心慌，一惊之下，侧着身子蹭挪过去，站到沃尔特的正视线里，在那儿用一双尖利的、磁石般的眼睛盯住这年轻人的软眼珠，稳稳占回了他的老位置，把这小子攥了个死死的。

「失盗那天夜里」——沃尔特的嗓音有气无力，可他像是正从斯莫尔的眼睛里照着念他的答话——「失盗那天夜里，斯莫尔大夫是……在……之前回的家——」说到这里，证人住了口，又抖又颤起来。因为巴德瞧出了斯莫尔目光的效验，把他那大块头往斯莫尔面前一杵，把眼睛钉住沃尔特，那一眼说的正是：「讲真话，不然进感化院。」

「俺不能，俺不能。哦上帝！俺可怎么办哪？」证人喊出来，回答的是巴德那一眼。因为在他听来，巴德分明开了口。在众人和国堂看来，这番激动没头没脑。霍金斯治安官的假发歪了，玻璃眼睛朝鼻子斗了进去，他费了好大的劲才没让假牙掉出来。激动紧得叫人发疼。拉尔夫站起身，望着证人，只觉得巴德和斯莫尔大夫——他的好天使和他的恶魔——不知怎么正在玩一场怕人的赌局，赌注就是他。人群前仰后合地晃，却鸦雀无声，单等着证人的一丝气音。他双手捂脸，哆嗦着站了一小会儿，随即晕倒了。

人群里有人晕倒，便是其余所有人出洋相的号令。众人一窝蜂朝晕倒的涌过去，喊水的喊水。人人央求别人开窗，人人巴望别人退后给他透风。可谁也不开窗，谁也不退后。屋里唯一彻底冷静的人是斯莫尔。他带着一副行家威严的安闲气派挤上前，搭病人的脉，向法庭报告说，他看这是一阵急发热，带谵妄的。沃尔特缓过来后，斯莫尔大夫本要把他弄走，拉尔夫却坚持他的证词非听不可。斯莫尔托着看护病人的名义，寸步不离他。沃尔特便又从头讲起那套老话——斯莫尔大夫十一点之前就回了诊室——这时巴德绕到大夫背后，把眼睛钉住证人，还是那副意味深长的一眼，沃尔特眼前晃着感化院的影子，卡住了，结巴起来，眼看又要晕。

===Original===
“If the court please,” said Bronson, “this witness is evidently intimidated by that stout young man,” pointing to Bud. “I have seen him twice interrupt witness’s testimony by casting threatening looks at him. I trust the court will have him removed from the courtroom.”

After a few moments’ consultation, during which Squire Hawkins held his wig in place with one hand and alternately adjusted his eye and his spectacles with the other, the magistrates, who were utterly bewildered by the turn things were taking, decided that it could do no harm, and that it was best to try the experiment of removing Bud. Perhaps Johnson would then be able to get through with his testimony. The constable therefore asked Bud if he would please leave the room. Bud cast one last look at the witness and walked out like a captive bear.

Ralph stood watching the receding form of Bud. The emergency had made him as cool as Small ever was. Bud stopped at the door, where he was completely out of sight of the witness, concealed by the excited spectators, who stood on the benches to see what was going on in front.

“The witness will please proceed,” said Bronson.

===Chinese===
「请法庭容禀，」布朗森说，指着巴德，「这位证人分明是叫那个壮小伙子吓着了。我亲眼看见他两回拿凶狠的眼色打断证人的证词。我信法庭会把他逐出法庭。」

两位法官商量了一小会儿——这中间霍金斯治安官一只手按住假发，另一只手轮换着扶他的玻璃眼睛和眼镜——他们叫事情的这个转折弄得晕头转向，末了断定这样不妨事，把巴德请出去试试顶好。兴许约翰逊这就能把证词讲完了。差役于是请问巴德肯不肯赏光离席。巴德朝证人投去最后一眼，像一头被擒的熊，走了出去。

拉尔夫站着，目送巴德远去的背影。这场急变把他激得同斯莫尔一样冷静。巴德在门口站住，那里证人完全瞧不见他，叫那些站在长凳上张望前头的兴奋看客遮了个严实。

「请证人继续。」布朗森说。

===Original===
“If the court please”—it was Ralph who spoke—“I believe I have as much at stake in this trial as anyone. That witness is evidently intimidated. But not by Mr. Means. I ask that Dr. Small be removed out of sight of the witness.”

“A most extraordinary request, truly.” This was what Small’s bland countenance said; he did not open his lips.

“It’s no more than fair,” said Squire Hawkins, adjusting his wig, “that the witness be relieved of everything that anybody might think affects his veracity in this matter.”

Dr. Small, giving Walter one friendly, appealing look, moved back by the door, and stood alongside Bud, as meek, quiet, and disinterested as any man in the house.

“The witness will now proceed with his testimony.” This time it was Squire Hawkins who spoke. Bronson had been attacked with a suspicion that this witness was not just what he wanted, and had relapsed into silence.

===Chinese===
「请法庭容禀」——说话的是拉尔夫——「我信这场审判里，我押上的赌注不比谁少。那位证人分明是受了吓。可吓他的不是米恩斯先生。我请求把斯莫尔大夫挪出证人的视线。」

「真是位了不起的请求啊。」这是斯莫尔那张和气脸面说的话；他嘴唇没开。

「这也不过是公道，」霍金斯治安官扶着假发说，「凡是众人可能疑心搅扰证人在这件事上说实话的东西，都该替他撤掉。」

斯莫尔大夫朝沃尔特投去一道友好的、恳求的一眼，退到门边，同巴德并肩站定，那份温良、安静、不偏不倚，不输屋里任何人。

「证人现在请继续作证。」这回说话的是霍金斯治安官。布朗森早犯了一层疑心：这个证人恐怕不是他想要的那号人，便缩回去不作声了。

===Original===
Walter’s struggle was by no means ended by the disappearance of Small and Bud. There came the recollection of his mother’s stern face—a face which had never been a motive toward the right, but only a goad to deception. What would she say if he should confess? Just as he had recovered himself, and was about to repeat the old lie which had twice died upon his lips at the sight of Bud’s look, he caught sight of another face, which made him tremble again. It was the lofty and terrible countenance of Mr. Soden. One might have thought, from the expression it wore, that the seven last vials were in his hands, the seven apocalyptic trumpets waiting for his lips, and the seven thunders sitting upon his eyebrows. The moment that Walter saw him he smelled the brimstone on his own garments, he felt himself upon the crumbling brink of the precipice, with perdition below him. Now I am sure that “Brother Sodoms” were not made wholly in vain. There are plenty of mean-spirited men like Walter Johnson, whose feeble consciences need all the support they can get from the fear of perdition, and who are incapable of any other conception of it than a coarse and materialistic one. Let us set it down to the credit of Brother Sodom, with his stiff stock, his thunderous face, and his awful walk, that his influence over Walter was on the side of truth.

“Please proceed,” said Squire Hawkins to Walter. The Squire’s wig lay on one side, he had forgotten to adjust his eye, and he leaned forward, tremulous with interest.

“Well, then,” said Walter, looking not at the court nor at Bronson nor at the prisoner, but furtively at Mr. Soden—“well, then, if I must”—and Mr. Soden’s awful face seemed to answer that he surely must—“well, then, I hope you won’t send me to prison”—this to Squire Hawkins, whose face reassured him—“but, oh! I don’t see how I can!” But one look at Mr. Soden assured him that he could and that he must, and so, with an agony painful to the spectators, he told the story in driblets. How, while yet in Lewisburg, he had been made a member of a gang of which Small was chief; how they concealed from him the names of all the band except six, of whom the Joneses and Small were three.

===Chinese===
沃尔特的挣扎，并没有随斯莫尔和巴德的隐去而到头。他记起他母亲那张严酷的脸——那张脸从来不是引他向善的动因，只是逼他作假的鞭子。他要是招了，她可怎么说？他刚缓过一口气，正要把那句老谎再搬一遍——那句话两回死在他嘴唇上，都因为瞧见了巴德的眼色——他又撞见另一张脸，叫他重又发起抖来。那是索登先生那张又崇高又可怕的尊容。瞧那脸上的神情，人家准当末后的七碗就捧在他手里，启示录的七支号角单等他嘴唇一碰，七声雷霆正骑在他的眉毛上。沃尔特一瞧见他，就闻见自己衣裳上的硫磺味，觉得自己正站在崩塌的悬崖边上，脚下便是沉沦。如今我算信了，「所多玛弟兄」们造出来并非全然白费。世上多的是沃尔特·约翰逊这样心志孱弱的人，他们那点薄弱的良心，全得靠对沉沦的恐惧撑腰，而他们所能设想的沉沦，又只是一种又粗又唯物的模样。那么，让我们把这一笔记在所多玛弟兄的功劳簿上——记上他那浆硬的领巾、他的雷霆脸、他那可怕的步态——他加给沃尔特的影响，是站在真话这一边的。

「请继续。」霍金斯治安官对沃尔特说。治安官的假发搭在一边，他忘了扶眼睛，整个人朝前倾着，激动得直颤。

「那么，」沃尔特说，眼睛不看法庭，不看布朗森，也不看被告，只偷偷觑着索登先生——「那么，要是非讲不可」——索登先生那张可怕的脸上像是答道：非讲不可——「那么，俺求你们别送俺进监狱」——这一句是对霍金斯治安官说的，那张脸叫他放了心——「可是，唉！俺瞧不出俺怎么讲得出口！」可再瞧一眼索登先生，就叫他拿准了他讲得出，也非讲不可；于是，带着一场叫看客们心疼的苦斗，他把那故事一滴一滴挤了出来。讲他还在刘易斯堡的时候，怎么被收罗进一伙帮派，斯莫尔是头领；讲他们怎么向他隐瞒了全伙的名姓，只除了六个，琼斯家哥儿俩和斯莫尔便是那六个里的三个。

===Original===
Here there was a scuffle at the door. The court demanded silence.

“Dr. Small’s trying to git out, plague take him,” said Bud, who stood with his back planted against the door. “I’d like the court to send and git his trunk afore he has a chance to burn up all the papers that’s in it.”

“Constable, you will arrest Dr. Small, Peter Jones, and William Jones. Send two deputies to bring Small’s trunk into court,” said Squire Underwood.

The prosecuting attorney was silent.

Walter then told of the robbery at Schroeder’s, told where he and Small had whittled the fence while the Joneses entered the house, and confirmed Ralph’s story by telling how they had seen Ralph in a fence-corner, and how they had met the basket-maker on the hill.

===Chinese===
这时门口一阵扭打。法庭喝令肃静。

「斯莫尔大夫想溜，这瘟神，」巴德说，他正拿脊梁抵着门。「俺想请法庭赶紧派人去取他的箱子，赶在他把里头的文书全烧掉之前。」

「差役，你把斯莫尔大夫、彼得·琼斯、威廉·琼斯逮捕起来。派两名帮手，把斯莫尔的箱子提到庭上来。」安德伍德治安官说。

起诉检察官没了声。

沃尔特接着讲施罗德家那桩盗窃，讲他和斯莫尔在哪儿削的栅栏、琼斯家哥儿俩从哪儿进的屋；又讲了他们怎么在栅栏拐角瞧见拉尔夫、怎么在山冈上碰见编篮子的，把拉尔夫那番话坐实了。

===Original===
“*To* be sure,” said the old man, who had not ventured to hold up his head, after he was arrested, until Walter began his testimony.

Walter felt inclined to stop, but he could not do it, for there stood Mr. Soden, looking to him like a messenger from the skies, or the bottomless pit, sent to extort the last word from his guilty soul. He felt that he was making a clean breast of it—at the risk of perdition, with the penitentiary thrown in, if he faltered. And so he told the whole thing as though it had been the day of doom, and by the time he was through, Small’s trunk was in court.

Here a new hubbub took place at the door. It was none other than the crazy pauper, Tom Bifield, who personated General Andrew Jackson in the poorhouse. He had caught some inkling of the trial, and had escaped in Bill Jones’s absence. His red plume was flying, and in his tattered and filthy garb he was indeed a picturesque figure.

===Chinese===
「那可不。」老头子说。他被押之后一直不敢抬头，直到沃尔特开了口。

沃尔特很想就此打住，可打不住，因为索登先生立在那儿，在他眼里就像天上——或者无底坑——打发来的使者，专要向他那有罪的灵魂逼出末一句话。他觉得自个儿这是竹筒倒豆子——一步踏错，便是沉沦，外加搭上一座感化院。于是他把整个事情讲了出来，仿佛今日便是末日审判；到他讲完的当口，斯莫尔的箱子已经提到法庭上了。

这时门口又起了一场骚乱。来的不是别人，正是那个疯乞丐汤姆·比菲尔德（Tom Bifield）——济贫院里扮演安德鲁·杰克逊将军的那位。他风闻了这场审判，趁比尔·琼斯不在，逃了出来。他的红翎子飘着，一身破烂腌臜的行头，倒真是个入画的身影。

===Original===
“Squar,” said he, elbowing his way through the crowd, “I kin tell you somethin’. I’m Gineral Andrew Jackson. Lost my head at Bueny Visty. This head growed on. It a’n’t good fer much. One side’s tater. But t’other’s sound as a nut. Now, I kind give you information.”

Bronson, with the quick perceptions of a politician, had begun to see which way future winds would probably blow. “If the court please,” he said, “this man is not wholly sane, but we might get valuable information out of him. I suggest that his testimony be taken for what it is worth.”

“No, you don’t swar me,” broke in the lunatic. “Not if I knows myself. You see, when a feller’s got one side of his head tater, he’s mighty onsartain like. You don’t swar me, fer I can’t tell what minute the tater side’ll begin to talk. I’m talkin’ out of the lef’ side now, and I’m all right. But you don’t swar me. But ef you’ll send some of your constables out to the barn at the pore-house and look under the haymow in the northeast corner, you’ll find some things maybe as has been a-missin’ fer some time. And that a’n’t out of the tater side, nuther.”

===Chinese===
「治安官，」他拿胳膊肘开路挤过人群，「俺能告诉你们点事儿。俺是安德鲁·杰克逊将军。俺这颗脑袋在布埃纳维斯塔丢过。这颗是后长上的。不顶大用。一边是土豆做的。可另一边响当当一个坚果仁。这会儿，俺能给你们报个信儿。」

布朗森有政客的快手快眼，已经瞧出往后的风要往哪边吹了。「请法庭容禀，」他说，「这个人神志不算全清，可我们兴许能从他嘴里掏出要紧的信儿。我提议，他的证词，值几分算几分地采用。」

「不，你们别想叫俺赌咒，」疯子插进来。「俺自个儿明白自个儿，就不干。你瞧，一个人的脑袋一边是土豆，他就靠不住得很。你们别叫俺赌咒，因为俺说不准土豆那半边哪一刻开口。俺这会儿是用左边讲话，俺没事儿。可你们别叫俺赌咒。不过，你们要是派几个差役上济贫院的草料棚，瞧瞧东北角干草垛底下，兴许能找着几样丢了有些日子的东西。这一句可不是从土豆那半边说的。」

===Original===
Meantime Bud did not rest. Hearing the nature of the testimony given by Hank Banta before he entered, he attacked Hank and vowed he’d send him to prison if he didn’t make a clean breast. Hank was a thorough coward, and, now that his friends were prisoners, was ready enough to tell the truth if he could be protected from prosecution. Seeing the disposition of the prosecuting attorney, Bud got from him a promise that he would do what he could to protect Hank. That worthy then took the stand, confessed his lie, and even told the inducement which Mr. Pete Jones had offered him to perjure himself.

“*To* be sure,” said Pearson.

Squire Hawkins, turning his right eye upon him, while the left looked at the ceiling, said: “Be careful, Mr. Pearson, or I shall have to punish you for contempt.”

“Why, Squar, I didn’t know ’twas any sin to hev a healthy contemp’ fer sech a thief as Jones!”

The Squire looked at Mr. Pearson severely, and the latter, feeling that he had committed some offense without knowing it, subsided into silence.

Bronson now had a keen sense of the direction of the gale.

===Chinese===
这边巴德也没闲着。他进门之前听说了汉克·班塔那番证词的底细，便扑住汉克，赌咒说他不竹筒倒豆子就送他进监狱。汉克是个十足的怂包，如今他的朋友们都下了狱，只要能免予追究，他巴不得讲真话。巴德瞧出起诉检察官的心思已经转向，便从他嘴里讨来一句应承：尽力护着汉克。于是那位可敬的先生上了证人席，认了自个儿的谎，连皮特·琼斯先生收买他发伪誓的那份好处，也一五一十讲了。

「那可不。」皮尔逊说。

霍金斯治安官把右眼转过来对着他，左眼望着天花板，说：「当心点儿，皮尔逊先生，不然我只好办你一个藐视法庭。」

「咦，治安官，俺不晓得，对琼斯这样的贼抱一副结结实实的瞧不起，还算个罪过！」

治安官严厉地瞧了皮尔逊先生一眼，皮尔逊先生觉出自个儿不知犯了什么过错，缩头不言语了。

布朗森这会儿把这场风的风向嗅了个真切。

===Original===
“If the court please,” said he, “I have tried to do my duty in this case. It was my duty to prosecute Mr. Hartsook, however much I might feel assured that he was innocent, and that he would be able to prove his innocence. I now enter a *nolle* in his case and that of John Pearson, and I ask that this court adjourn until tomorrow, in order to give me time to examine the evidence in the case of the other parties under arrest. I am proud to think that my efforts have been the means of sifting the matter to the bottom, of freeing Mr. Hartsook from suspicion, and of detecting the real criminals.”

“Ugh!” said Mr. Pearson, who conceived a great dislike to Bronson.

“The court,” said Squire Hawkins, “congratulates Mr. Hartsook on his triumphant acquittal. He is discharged from the bar of this court, and from the bar of public sentiment, without a suspicion of guilt. Constable, discharge Ralph Hartsook and John Pearson.”

===Chinese===
「请法庭容禀，」他说，「本案我已尽力尽了职分。起诉哈茨克先生本是职分所在，不管我心里多么拿得准他是清白的、拿得准他证明得了自己的清白。现在，对他的案子和对约翰·皮尔逊的案子，我具结*中止追诉*（nolle）；并请本庭休庭到明天，容我腾出工夫，查验其余在押诸案的证据。想到我出的力竟成了把这件事兜底筛清的手段——洗清了哈茨克先生的嫌疑，查获了真正的罪犯——我深以为傲。」

「呸！」皮尔逊先生说，他对布朗森起了一股子大大的嫌恶。

「本庭，」霍金斯治安官说，「祝贺哈茨克先生得胜开释。他从本庭的栏杆里开释，也从公众舆论的栏杆里开释，不带一丝罪嫌。差役，把拉尔夫·哈茨克和约翰·皮尔逊放了。」

===Original===
Old Jack Means, who had always had a warm side for the master, now proposed three cheers for Mr. Hartsook, and they were given with a will by the people who would have hanged him an hour before.

Mrs. Means gave it as her opinion that “Jack Means allers wuz a fool!”

“This court,” said Dr. Underwood, “has one other duty to perform before adjourning for the day. Recall Hannah Thomson.”

“I jist started her on ahead to git supper and milk the cows,” said Mrs. Means. “A’n’t a-goin’ to have her loafin’ here all day.”

“Constable, recall her. This court can not adjourn until she returns!”

===Chinese===
老杰克·米恩斯本来就一向向着这位先生，这时便提议为哈茨克先生三呼万岁，一小时前还想吊死他的人们，痛痛快快呼了三声。

米恩斯太太发表她的高见：「杰克·米恩斯打娘胎里出来就是个傻子！」

「本庭，」安德伍德大夫说，「今天散庭之前，还有一桩职分要尽。把汉娜·汤姆森召回来。」

「俺刚打发她家去烧晚饭、挤牛奶了，」米恩斯太太说。「可不能叫她在这儿晃荡一整天。」

「差役，去把她召回来。她不回来，本庭不能散庭！」

===Original===
Hannah had gone but a little way, and was soon in the presence of the court, trembling for fear of some new calamity.

“Hannah Thomson”—it was Squire Underwood who spoke—“Hannah Thomson, this court wishes to ask you one or two questions.”

“Yes, sir,” but her voice died to a whisper.

“How old did you say you were?”

“Eighteen, sir, last October.”

“Can you prove your age?”

===Chinese===
汉娜没走多远，不多时就到了法庭跟前，只怕又有什么新的祸事，浑身发着抖。

「汉娜·汤姆森」——说话的是安德伍德治安官——「汉娜·汤姆森，本庭要问你一两句话。」

「是，先生。」可她的声音细成了一丝气音。

「你方才说你多大来着？」

「十八，先生，上年十月满的。」

「你的年岁，证明得了吗？」

===Original===
“Yes, sir—by my mother.”

“For how long are you bound to Mr. Means?”

“Till I’m twenty-one.”

“This court feels in duty bound to inform you that, according to the laws of Indiana, a woman is of age at eighteen, and as no indenture could be made binding after you had reached your majority, you are the victim of a deception. You are free, and if it can be proven that you have been defrauded by a willful deception, a suit for damages will lie.”

“Ugh!” said Mrs. Means. “You’re a purty court, a’n’t you, Dr. Underwood?”

“Be careful, Mrs. Means, or I shall have to fine you for contempt of court.”

===Chinese===
「证明得了，先生——俺娘能证明。」

「你抵给米恩斯先生，抵到什么时候？」

「抵到俺二十一。」

「本庭有职责告知你：按照印第安纳的法律，女子十八岁即为成年；你既已到了成年，任何卖身契都再绑不住你——你是叫一场欺哄坑了。你自由了；倘若能证明你是叫人存心欺哄着坑了，还可以提起一场索赔的官司。」

「哼！」米恩斯太太说。「好一个体面法庭，可不是嘛，安德伍德大夫？」

「放检点些，米恩斯太太，不然我只好罚你一个藐视法庭。」

===Original===
But the people, who were in the cheering humor, cheered Hannah and the justices, and then cheered Ralph again. Granny Sanders shook hands with him, and allers knowed he’d come out right. It allers ’peared like as if Dr. Small warn’t jist the sort to tie to, you know. And old John Pearson went home, after drinking two or three glasses of Welch’s whisky, keeping time to an imaginary triumphal march, and feeling prouder than he had ever felt since he fit the Britishers under Scott at Lundy’s Lane. He told his wife that the master had jist knocked the hind-sights offen that air young lawyer from Lewisburg.

Walter was held to bail that he might appear as a witness, and Ralph might have sent his aunt a Roland for an Oliver. But he only sent a note to his uncle, asking him to go Walter’s bail. If he had been resentful, he could not have wished for a more complete revenge than the day had brought.

===Chinese===
可众人正欢呼在兴头上，为汉娜欢呼，为两位法官欢呼，接着又为拉尔夫欢呼。桑德斯婆婆同他握手，说她从来就知道他有好结果；又说斯莫尔大夫那号人，你瞧着吧，她向来就没当是能靠得住的。老约翰·皮尔逊喝了两三杯韦尔奇的威士忌回家去，脚下踩着一支想象中的得胜进行曲的拍子，那份得意，打从他在伦迪巷跟着斯科特打英国佬那阵儿起还不曾有过。他告诉老婆子，说那位先生把刘易斯堡来的那个年轻律师的后准星，一拳敲了个精光。

沃尔特交了保，以证人身份到案；拉尔夫本可以给他婶母送一份旗鼓相当的回敬。可他只给他叔叔捎了个便条，请他去替沃尔特作保。他心里要真存着怨，也再想不出比这一天送上门来的更齐全的报应了。

## XXIX The Trial / 二十九 审判

===Original===
The “prosecuting attorney” (for so the State’s attorney is called in Indiana) had been sent for the night before. Ralph refused all legal help. It was not wise to reject counsel, but all his blood was up, and he declared that he would not be cleared by legal quibbles. If his innocence were not made evident to everybody, he would rather not be acquitted on a preliminary examination. He would go over to the circuit court and have the matter sifted to the bottom. But he would have been pleased had his uncle offered his counsel, though he would have declined it. He would have felt better to have had a letter from home somewhat different from the one he received from his Aunt Matilda by the hand of the prosecuting attorney. It was not very encouraging or very sympathetic, though it was very characteristic.

===Chinese===
那「起诉检察官」（印第安纳管州检察官就叫这个名目）是头天晚上请来的。拉尔夫拒绝一切法律援助。回绝辩护本来算不得聪明，可他一腔血全涌了上来，声言他不愿意靠法律上的遁词开脱。倘若他的清白不能叫人人看得明明白白，他宁可不在预审里脱罪，要转到巡回法院去，把这件事兜底筛一遍。可要是他叔叔肯主动出来替他辩护，他心里倒是欢喜的——尽管他仍会辞谢。要是家里来的信不是这一封——由起诉检察官亲手转来的玛蒂尔达婶母的信——而是另一副样子的，他心里也会好受些。这封信不大鼓劲，也不大体贴，倒极有它本人的风味。

===Original===
> “Dear Ralph:
> “This is what I have always been afraid of. I warned you faithfully the last time I saw you. My skirts are clear of your blood. I can not consent for your uncle to appear as your counsel or to go your bail. You know how much it would injure him in the county, and he has no right to suffer for your evil acts. O my dear nephew! for the sake of your poor, dead mother—”

===Chinese===
> 「亲爱的拉尔夫：
> 「这正是我一直害怕的事。上回见你，我忠言规劝过你。你流的血沾不到我的裙角上。我不能答应让你叔叔出庭做你的辩护人，也不能给你作保。你知道这会在县里多么毁他，他没有义务替你的恶行受罪。哦，我亲爱的侄儿！看在你可怜的、死去的母亲分上——」

===Original===
We never shall know what the rest of that letter was. Whenever Aunt Matilda got to Ralph’s poor, dead mother in her conversation Ralph ran out of the house. And now that his poor, dead mother was again made to do service in his aunt’s pious rhetoric, he landed the letter on the hot coals before him, and watched it vanish into smoke with a grim satisfaction.

Ralph was a little afraid of a mob. But Clifty was better than Flat Creek, and Squire Hawkins, with all his faults, loved justice, and had a profound respect for the majesty of the law, and a profound respect for his own majesty when sitting as a court representing the law. Whatever maneuvers he might resort to in business affairs in order to avoid a conflict with his lawless neighbors, he was courageous and inflexible on the bench. The Squire was the better part of him. With the cooperation of the constable, he had organized a posse of men who could be depended on to enforce the law against a mob.

===Chinese===
那封信的下半截，我们永远无从知道了。每逢玛蒂尔达婶母的话头说到拉尔夫可怜的、死去的母亲，拉尔夫就往屋外跑。如今他那可怜的、死去的母亲又被搬出来，在他婶母的敬虔修辞里当差，他便把信撂在面前的火炭上，看着它化成一股烟，心里带着一丝冷冷的快意。

拉尔夫有点儿怕暴民闹事。可克利夫蒂比弗拉特溪强，霍金斯治安官毛病虽多，却敬爱公道，对法律的威严有一份深深的敬畏——对他自己坐堂代表法律时的那份威严，也有一份深深的敬畏。做买卖的时候，为着不同那些无法无天的邻居起冲突，他尽可以耍些花招；一坐上堂，他却勇敢而不容情。治安官这个身份，是他这个人身上较好的一半。有差役帮忙，他组织起一支民团，个个靠得住，顶得住暴民，把法律贯彻下去。

===Original===
By the time the trial opened in the large schoolhouse in Clifty at eleven o’clock, all the surrounding country had emptied its population into Clifty, and all Flat Creek was on hand ready to testify to something. Those who knew the least appeared to know the most, and were prodigal of their significant winks and nods. Mrs. Means had always suspected him. She seed some mighty suspicious things about him from the word go. She’d allers had her doubts whether he was jist the thing, and ef her ole man had axed her, liker-n not he never’d a been hired. She’d seed things with her own livin’ eyes that beat all she ever seed in all her born days. And Pete Jones said he’d allers knowed ther warn’t no good in sech a feller. Couldn’t stay abed when he got there. And Granny Sanders said, Law’s sakes! nobody’d ever a found him out ef it hadn’t been fer her. Didn’t she go all over the neighborhood a-warnin’ people? Fer her part, she seed straight through that piece of goods. He was fond of the gals, too! Nothing was so great a crime in her eyes as to be fond of the gals.

===Chinese===
十一点钟，克利夫蒂那座大校舍里开庭的时候，四乡的人都倒进了克利夫蒂，弗拉特溪更是倾巢而来，个个预备着为点什么作证。知道得最少的，看着倒像知道得最多，挤眉弄眼，大方得很。米恩斯太太起早就疑心他了。打头一天起她就瞧出他浑身是顶顶可疑的事儿。她一直嘀咕他到底对不对路数，要是她家老头子问过她，十有八九压根儿雇不成他。她用她自己那双活眼睛见过的事儿，把她这辈子见过的一切全比下去了。皮特·琼斯也说，他一直就知道这种货色没个好——到了床上连床都躺不住。桑德斯婆婆说，我的老天！要不是亏了她，谁也别想把他瞧破。她不是挨家挨户串门儿警告人来着？依她看，她早把那匹料子看穿了。他还好迷姑娘们！在她眼里，天底下顶大的罪过，就是好迷姑娘们。

===Original===
The constable paid unwitting tribute to William the Conquerer by crying Squire Hawkins’ court open with an Oyez! or, as he said, “O yes!” and the Squire asked Squire Underwood, who came in at that minute, to sit with him. From the start, it was evident to Ralph that the prosecuting attorney had been thoroughly posted by Small, though, looking at that worthy’s face, one would have thought him the most disinterested and philosophical spectator in the courtroom.

Bronson, the prosecutor, was a young man, and this was his first case since his election. He was very ambitious to distinguish himself, very anxious to have Flat Creek influence on his side in politics; and, consequently, he was very determined to send Ralph Hartsook to State prison, justly or unjustly, by fair means or foul. To his professional eyes this was not a question of right and wrong, not a question of life or death to such a man as Ralph. It was George H. Bronson’s opportunity to distinguish himself. And so, with many knowing and confident nods and hints, and with much deference to the two squires, he opened the case, affecting great indignation at Ralph’s wickedness, and uttering Delphic hints about striped pants and shaven head, and the grating of prison-doors at Jeffersonville.

===Chinese===
差役喊霍金斯治安官的法庭开庭，无意间给征服者威廉上了一礼：他喊的是「Oyez！」——照他自己嘴里出来的音，是「O yes！（哦——是的！）」治安官请恰在这会儿进门的安德伍德治安官同他一起坐堂。一开场拉尔夫就看得明白：起诉检察官早叫斯莫尔把底喂透了，只是瞧瞧那位可敬先生的脸，谁都会当他是法庭上顶不偏心、顶有哲学家气度的看客。

检察官布朗森是个年轻人，这是他当选以来的头一桩案子。他一门心思想露脸，急巴巴要拉弗拉特溪的政治影响站到自己这边，于是铁了心要把拉尔夫·哈茨克送进州监狱——有罪也好无罪也好，光明正大也好不择手段也好。在他那双行家的眼睛里，这不是一个是非问题，对拉尔夫这样的人也不是一条人命的问题。这是乔治·H·布朗森露一手的机会。于是，冲两位治安官递了许多内行而笃定的点头和口风、行了许许多多的礼数之后，他开了案，对拉尔夫的恶行装出一腔大义愤，又神神道道地点了几句条纹囚裤和剃光头，点了几句杰斐逊维尔监狱铁门的吱嘎声。

===Original===
“And, now, if the court please, I am about to call a witness whose testimony is very important indeed. Mrs. Sarah Jane Means will please step forward and be sworn.”

This Mrs. Means did with alacrity. She had met the prosecutor, and impressed him with her dark hints. She was sworn.

“Now, Mrs. Means, have the goodness to tell us what you know of the robbery at the house of Peter Schroeder, and the part defendant had in it.”

“Well, you see, I allers suspected that air young man—”

Here Squire Underwood stopped her, and told her that she must not tell her suspicions, but facts.

“Well, it’s facts I am a-going to tell,” she sniffed indignantly. “It’s facts that I mean to tell.” Here her voice rose to a keen pitch, and she began to abuse the defendant. Again and again the court insisted that she must tell what there was suspicious about the schoolmaster. At last she got it out.

===Chinese===
「现在，请法庭容禀，我要传一位证人，他的证词实在要紧得很。有请莎拉·简·米恩斯太太上前宣誓。」

米恩斯太太应声上前，腿脚麻利。她见过检察官，用她那几记阴恻恻的暗示给了他不浅的印象。她宣了誓。

「那么，米恩斯太太，劳驾把你所知道的彼得·施罗德家那桩盗窃案，还有被告在里头充当的一份，讲给我们听。」

「这个么，你瞧，俺起早就疑心那个年轻人——」

安德伍德治安官止住了她，告诉她不许讲她的疑心，要讲事实。

「俺要讲的正是事实，」她气哼哼地嗤了一声，「俺要讲的就是事实。」说到这里，她的嗓门拔到了尖儿上，开口骂起被告来。法庭再三要她讲讲那位教书匠到底哪里可疑。她总算挤出来了。

===Original===
“Well, fer one thing, what kind of gals did he go with? Hey? Why, with my bound gal, Hanner, a-loafin’ along through the bluegrass paster at ten o’clock, and keepin’ that gal that’s got no protector but me out that a-way, and destroyin’ her character by his company, that a’n’t fit fer nobody.”

Here Bronson saw that he had caught a tartar. He said he had no more questions to ask of Mrs. Means, and that, unless the defendant wished to cross-question her, she could stand aside. Ralph said he would like to ask her one question.

“Did I ever go with your daughter Miranda?”

“No, you didn’t,” answered the witness, with a tone and a toss of the head that let the cat out, and set the courtroom in a giggle. Bronson saw that he was gaining nothing, and now resolved to follow the line which Small had indicated.

===Chinese===
「这个么，头一件，他搭的是哪一门子姑娘？嗯？跟的是俺家的抵债姑娘汉娜，十点钟还俩人在蓝草牧场上闲逛荡，叫那个除了俺没别人护着的姑娘在外头野到那份上，拿他那种连给谁作伴儿都不配的伴儿，糟蹋她的名声！」

布朗森看出自己咬上了一块硬骨头。他说对米恩斯太太他没有别的话要问了，除非被告想盘问她，她可以退过一旁。拉尔夫说想问她一句。

「我跟没跟你家闺女米兰达走过一路？」

「没，你没走过。」证人答道，那口气、那一甩脑袋，把底儿泄了个干净，惹得满法庭咯咯直笑。布朗森见讨不着便宜，便决计照斯莫尔指的那条道走了。

===Original===
Pete Jones was called, and swore point-blank that he heard Ralph go out of the house soon after he went to bed, and that he heard him return at two in the morning. This testimony was given without hesitation, and made a great impression against Ralph in the minds of the justices. Mrs. Jones, a poor, browbeaten woman, came on the stand in a frightened way, and swore to the same lies as her husband. Ralph cross-questioned her, but her part had been well learned.

There seemed now little hope for Ralph. But just at this moment who should stride into the schoolhouse but Pearson, the one-legged old soldier basket-maker? He had crept home the night before, “to see ef the ole woman didn’t want somethin’,” and hearing of Ralph’s arrest, he concluded that the time for him to make “a forrard movement” had come, and so he determined to face the foe.

“Looky here, Squar,” he said, wiping the perspiration from his brow, “looky here. I jes want to say that I kin tell as much about this case as anybody.”

“Let us hear it, then,” said Bronson, who thought he would nail Ralph now for certain.

===Chinese===
皮特·琼斯被传上来，一口咬死赌咒说，他上床不久就听见拉尔夫出屋，又听见他清早两点回来。这份证词答得干脆，不带含糊，在两位法官心里给拉尔夫记下重重的一笔。琼斯太太，一个可怜的、受惯了踩咕的女人，怯生生上了证人席，赌了和她男人一样的谎。拉尔夫盘问她，可她那一份戏背得烂熟。

拉尔夫眼下似乎没什么指望了。可恰在这个当口，大步跨进校舍来的不是别人，正是皮尔逊——那个独腿的老兵、编篮子的。头天夜里他溜回家去，「瞧瞧老婆子缺个啥不」，听说了拉尔夫被押，断定自己「向前挺进」的时辰到了，便决计迎面去会一会敌人。

「你听俺说，治安官，」他一面从脑门上抹汗，一面说，「你听着。俺只想讲一句：这个案子，俺知道的跟谁比都不多让。」

「那就讲给我们听。」布朗森说，心想这回拉尔夫是钉死了。

===Original===
So, with many allusions to the time he fit at Lundy’s Lane, and some indignant remarks about the pack of thieves that driv him off, and a passing tribute to Miss Martha Hawkins, and sundry other digressions, in which he had to be checked, the old man told how he’d drunk whisky at Welch’s store that night, and how Welch’s whisky was all-fired mean, and how it allers went straight to his head, and how he had got a leetle too much, and how he had felt kyinder gin aout by the time he got to the blacksmith’s shop, and how he had laid down to rest, and how as he s’posed the boys had crated him, and how he thought it war all-fired mean to crate a old soldier what fit the Britishers, and lost his leg by one of the blamed critters a-punchin’ his bagonet27 through it; and how when he woke up it was all-fired cold, and how he rolled off the crate and went on to*wurds* home, and how when he got up to the top of Means’s hill he met Pete Jones and Bill Jones, and a slim sort of a young man, a-ridin’; and how he know’d the Joneses by ther hosses, and some more things of that kyind about ’em; but he didn’t know the slim young man, though he thought he might tell him ef he seed him agin kase he was dressed up so slick and town-like. But blamed ef he didn’t think it hard that a passel of thieves sech as the Joneses should try to put ther mean things on to a man like the master, that was so kyind to him and to Shocky, though, fer that matter, blamed ef he didn’t think we was all selfish, akordin’ to his tell. Had seed somebody that night a-crossin’ over the bluegrass paster. Didn’t know who in thunder ’twas, but it was somebody a-makin’ straight fer Pete Jones’s. Hadn’t seed nobody else, ’ceptin’ Dr. Small, a short ways behind the Joneses.

===Chinese===
于是，老头子夹着不少他当年在伦迪巷打仗的旧话，几句把那伙把他轰走的贼崽子们骂得痛快的气话，一段顺带献给玛莎·霍金斯小姐的敬意，还有种种别的离题的话——中间叫人拦了几回——讲起来：那天夜里他在韦尔奇的铺子怎么喝了威士忌，韦尔奇的威士忌怎么歹毒得要命，怎么一进嘴就直冲脑门，他怎么多贪了一星半点儿，走到铁匠铺的时候怎么觉得身上有点儿撑不住，怎么躺下歇息，怎么估摸着是小子们把他装进了条板箱，又怎么想来想去都觉得：把一个跟英国佬打过仗、叫一个天杀的英国佬拿刺刀27捅穿了一条腿的老兵装进条板箱，实在歹毒得没天理。他还讲，他醒过来的时候怎么冷得要命，怎么从条板箱上滚下来朝家走，走到米恩斯家山冈顶上怎么碰见了皮特·琼斯和比尔·琼斯，还有一个瘦条条的年轻人，骑着马；他怎么凭着马认出了琼斯家哥儿俩，还凭着几件那一类的事认出了他们；可那个瘦条条的年轻人他不认得，不过他想，再见着没准儿能认出来，因为那家伙穿得溜光水滑，一副城里人派头。可是真真儿的，他觉得实在不像话：琼斯家这样一伙贼，竟想把他们那些歹毒事栽到一个像先生这样的人身上——先生对他、对肖基都那么好；不过话说回来，照他自个儿说来，他又真真儿地觉得俺们人人都是自私自利的。那天夜里他瞧见有个人影儿穿过蓝草牧场。鬼知道是谁，反正那人直奔皮特·琼斯家去了。旁的谁也没瞧见，只瞧见斯莫尔大夫，缀在琼斯家哥儿俩后头不远。

===Original===
Hannah was now brought on the stand. She was greatly agitated, and answered with much reluctance. Lived at Mr. Means’s. Was eighteen years of age in October. Had been bound to Mrs. Means three years ago. Had walked home with Mr. Hartsook that evening, and, happening to look out of the window toward morning, she saw someone cross the pasture. Did not know who it was. Thought it was Mr. Hartsook. Here Mr. Bronson (evidently prompted by a suggestion that came from what Small had overheard when he listened in the barn) asked her if Mr. Hartsook had ever said anything to her about the matter afterward. After some hesitation, Hannah said that he had said that he crossed the pasture. Of his own accord? No, she spoke of it first. Had Mr. Hartsook offered any explanations? No, he hadn’t. Had he ever paid her any attention afterward? No. Ralph declined to cross-question Hannah. To him she never seemed so fair as when telling the truth so sublimely.

===Chinese===
汉娜这时被带上了证人席。她激动得很，答话百般不情愿。住在米恩斯先生家。十月里满的十八岁。三年前立契抵给了米恩斯太太。那天傍晚她同哈茨克先生一道走回家，下半夜碰巧朝窗外望，看见有人穿过牧场。不认得是谁。以为是哈茨克先生。这里布朗森先生（明摆着是得了人提点，那话头出自斯莫尔在谷仓里偷听来的东西）问她，哈茨克先生事后可曾对她提起过这件事。汉娜迟疑了一下，说他提过，说他穿过牧场。他自己主动提的？不是，是她先说起的。哈茨克先生解释过什么没有？没有。他后来可曾对她献过什么殷勤？没有。拉尔夫放弃盘问汉娜。在他眼里，她从没有像此刻这样庄严地讲着真话的时候那么美过。

===Original===
Bronson now informed the court that this little trick of having the old soldier happen in, in the flick of time, wouldn’t save the prisoner at the bar from the just punishment which an outraged law visited upon such crimes as his. He regretted that his duty as a public prosecutor caused it to fall to his lot to marshal the evidence that was to blight the prospects and blast the character, and annihilate forever, so able and promising a young man, but that the law knew no difference between the educated and the uneducated, and that for his part he thought Hartsook a most dangerous foe to the peace of society. The evidence already given fastened suspicion upon him. The prisoner had not yet been able to break its force at all. The prisoner had not even dared to try to explain to a young lady the reason for his being out at night. He would now conclude by giving the last touch to the dark evidence that would sink the once fair name of Ralph Hartsook in a hundred fathoms of infamy. He would ask that Henry Banta be called.

===Chinese===
布朗森这时禀告法庭：叫那老老兵恰恰赶在这个节骨眼上撞进来，这点小把戏救不了被告，救不了他逃脱被触犯的法律为这一类罪行所加的应得之罚。他说他深感遗憾：他身为公诉检察官的职分，竟派到他头上，来摆布这班证据——去摧折这么一个能干有为的年轻人的前程，炸毁他的名声，把他永远毁掉。只是法律不问受教育的和没受教育的；而且就他而论，他认为哈茨克是社会上太平的一个顶危险的仇敌。已有的证据把嫌疑钉在了他身上。被告至今全然没能撼动这些证据的分量。被告甚至不敢试它一试——连向一位年轻小姐解释解释他夜里在外的缘由都不敢。他现在就要收尾了，给这黑色的证据补上最后一笔，把拉尔夫·哈茨克那一度清白的名声，沉进一百英寻的耻辱里。他请求传亨利·班塔。

===Original===
Hank came forward sheepishly, and was sworn. Lived about a hundred yards from the house that was robbed. He seen ole man Pearson and the master and one other feller that he didn’t know come away from there together about one o’clock. He heerd the horses kickin’, and went out to the stable to see about them. He seed two men come out of Schroeder’s back door and meet one man standing at the gate. When they got closter he knowed Pearson by his wooden leg and the master by his hat. On cross-examination he was a little confused when asked why he hadn’t told of it before, but said that he was afraid to say much, bekase the folks was a-talkin’ about hanging the master, and he didn’t want no lynchin’.

The prosecution here rested, Bronson maintaining that there was enough evidence to justify Ralph’s committal to await trial. But the court thought that as the defendant had no counsel and offered no rebutting testimony, it would be only fair to hear what the prisoner had to say in his own defense.

All this while poor Ralph was looking about the room for Bud. Bud’s actions had of late been strangely contradictory. But had he turned coward and deserted his friend? Why else did he avoid the session of the court? After asking himself such questions as these, Ralph would wonder at his own folly. What could Bud do if he were there? There was no human power that could prevent the victim of so vile a conspiracy as this, lodging in that worst of State prisons at Jeffersonville, a place too bad for criminals. But when there is no human power to help, how naturally does the human mind look for some divine intervention on the side of Right! And Ralph’s faith in Providence looked in the direction of Bud. But since no Bud came, he shut down the valves and rose to his feet, proudly, defiantly, fiercely calm.

===Chinese===
汉克缩头缩脑地上前，宣了誓。住在离失盗那家一百来码的地方。一点钟光景，他瞧见老皮尔逊、先生，还有一个他不认得的家伙，仨人一块儿从那儿走开。他听见马踢腾，就出去到马棚里照看。他瞧见两个人从施罗德家后门出来，同站在大门口的一个人碰了头。走近了些，他凭木头腿认出了皮尔逊，凭帽子认出了先生。盘问的时候，问到他先前怎么不讲，他有点儿慌乱，说他不敢多嘴，因为大伙儿正嚷嚷着要吊死先生，他不想瞧见私刑。

公诉到这里歇了手，布朗森咬定证据足够把拉尔夫收监候审。可法庭认为，被告既没有辩护人，又没有提出什么反驳的证词，那么听听犯人自己替自己说些什么，才算公道。

这半天里，可怜的拉尔夫一直在满屋子找巴德。巴德近来的行踪古怪得很，一阵一阵自相矛盾。可他难道怂了，把朋友撇了？要不然他干吗躲着法庭不开脸？问过自己这些个，拉尔夫又要笑自己糊涂。巴德就是在场，又能顶什么用？掉进这么一场下作阴谋里的人，人间再没有力量能拦住他不进杰斐逊维尔那座顶坏的州监狱——那地方连关罪犯都嫌坏。可人间没有力量搭救的时候，人心多么自然地要指望神明出来站到正义这一边搭把手啊！拉尔夫对天意的信心，就朝着巴德那个方向望着。可巴德终究没有来，他便关上汽阀，站起身——高傲地，叫板地，带着一股狠劲的平静。

===Original===
“It’s of no use for me to say anything. Peter Jones has sworn to a deliberate falsehood, and he knows it. He has made his wife perjure her poor soul that she dare not call her own.” Here Pete’s fists clenched, but Ralph in his present humor did not care for mobs. The spirit of the bulldog had complete possession of him. “It is of no use for me to tell you that Henry Banta has sworn to a lie, partly to revenge himself on me for punishments I have given him, and partly, perhaps, for money. The real thieves are in this courtroom. I could put my finger on them.”

“*To* be sure,” responded the old basket-maker. Ralph looked at Pete Jones, then at Small. The fiercely calm look attracted the attention of the people. He knew that this look would probably cost him his life before the next morning. But he did not care for life. “The testimony of Miss Hannah Thomson is every word true, I believe that of Mr. Pearson to be true. The rest is false. But I can not prove it. I know the men I have to deal with. I shall not escape with State prison. They will not spare my life. But the people of Clifty will one day find out who are the thieves.” Ralph then proceeded to tell how he had left Pete Jones’s, Mr. Jones’s bed being uncomfortable; how he had walked through the pasture; how he had seen three men on horseback: how he had noticed the sorrel with the white left forefoot and white nose; how he had seen Dr. Small; how, after his return, he had heard someone enter the house, and how he had recognized the horse the next morning. “There,” said Ralph desperately, leveling his finger at Pete, “there is a man who will yet see the inside of a penitentiary. I shall not live to see it, but the rest of you will.” Pete quailed. Ralph’s speech could not of course break the force of the testimony against him. But it had its effect, and it had effect enough to alarm Bronson, who rose and said:

===Chinese===
「我说什么都没有用。彼得·琼斯赌的是一句处心积虑的谎话，他自己知道。他逼着他婆娘把她那可怜的灵魂拿来发伪誓——那灵魂，她自己都做不得主。」说到这里，皮特的拳头攥紧了，可拉尔夫眼下这个心气，横竖不把暴民放在眼里。斗牛犬的魂灵把他整个占了。「我用不着告诉你们，亨利·班塔赌的也是谎——一半是要报我罚过他的仇，一半，兴许，是为了钱。真正的贼就在这法庭里。我拿指头就点得出来。」

「那可不。」老编篮子的应了一声。拉尔夫瞧了皮特·琼斯一眼，又瞧了斯莫尔一眼。那带着狠劲的平静目光，引得众人都注意了。他知道这一眼多半要叫他活不过明天早晨。可他不在乎活命。「汉娜·汤姆森小姐的证词一字一句都是真的，皮尔逊先生的证词我相信也是真的。其余的全是假的。可我证明不了。我晓得我打的是什么交道。我不会止步于州监狱。他们不会饶我的命。可克利夫蒂的人总有一天会弄明白，贼是谁。」接着，拉尔夫讲他怎么离开了皮特·琼斯家——琼斯先生那张床太硌人；怎么穿过了牧场；怎么瞧见了三个骑马的；怎么留意到那匹栗色马，左前脚一只白蹄，鼻子也是白的；怎么瞧见了斯莫尔大夫；回屋之后怎么听见有人进了宅子，第二天清早又怎么认出了那匹马。「瞧啊，」拉尔夫绝望地伸指头点着皮特，「那儿站着一个早晚要亲眼看看感化院里头是什么样的人。我是活不到那一天了，你们大伙儿都瞧得见。」皮特瘮了一下。拉尔夫这番话当然折不断压在他身上的证词的分量，可它有它的效验，而且效验大到叫布朗森着了慌。布朗森站起来说：

===Original===
“I should like to ask the prisoner at the bar one question.”

“Ask me a dozen,” said Hartsook, looking more like a king than a criminal.

“Well, then, Mr. Hartsook. You need not answer unless you choose; but what prompted you to take the direction you did in your walk on that evening?”

This shot brought Ralph down. To answer this question truly would attach to friendless Hannah Thomson some of the disgrace that now belonged to him.

“I decline to answer,” said Ralph.

“Of course, I do not want the prisoner to criminate himself,” said Bronson significantly.

===Chinese===
「我想问被告一个问题。」

「问一打也使得。」哈茨克说，那模样更像一位国王，不像一个罪犯。

「那么，哈茨克先生。你愿意答就答，不愿意也随你；只是，那天傍晚你散步，是什么引着你挑了那个方向的？」

这一枪把拉尔夫打落了马。照实答这一问，就要把如今落在他身上的这份耻辱，分一些到无依无靠的汉娜·汤姆森头上。

「我拒绝回答。」拉尔夫说。

「当然，我不是要被告自证其罪。」布朗森意味深长地说。

===Original===
During this last passage Bud had come in, but, to Ralph’s disappointment he remained near the door, talking to Walter Johnson, who had come with him. The magistrates put their heads together to fix the amount of bail, and, as they differed, talked for some minutes. Small now for the first time thought best to make a move in his own proper person. He could hardly have been afraid of Ralph’s acquittal. He may have been a little anxious at the manner in which he had been mentioned, and at the significant look of Ralph, and he probably meant to excite indignation enough against the schoolmaster to break the force of his speech, and secure the lynching of the prisoner, chiefly by people outside his gang. He rose and asked the court in gentlest tones to hear him. He had no personal interest in this trial, except his interest in the welfare of his old schoolmate, Mr. Hartsook. He was grieved and disappointed to find the evidence against him so damaging and he would not for the world add a feather to it, if it were not that his own name had been twice alluded to by the defendant, and by his friend, and perhaps his confederate, John Pearson. He was prepared to swear that he was not over in Flat Creek the night of the robbery later than ten o’clock, and while the statements of the two persons alluded to, whether maliciously intended or not, could not implicate him at all, he thought perhaps this lack of veracity in their statements might be of weight in determining some other points. He therefore suggested—he could only suggest, as he was not a party to the case in any way—that his student, Mr. Walter Johnson, be called to testify as to his—Dr. Small’s—exact whereabouts on the night in question. They were together in his office until two, when he went to the tavern and went to bed.

Squire Hawkins, having adjusted his teeth, his wig, and his glass eye, thanked Dr. Small for a suggestion so valuable, and thought best to put John Pearson under arrest before proceeding further. Mr. Pearson was therefore arrested, and was heard to mutter something about a “passel of thieves,” when the court warned him to be quiet.

Walter Johnson was then called. But before giving his testimony, I must crave the reader’s patience while I go back to some things which happened nearly a week before and which will serve to make it intelligible.

===Chinese===
这最后一幕过场的当口，巴德进来了，可叫拉尔夫失望的是，他只挨着门口站，同一路来的沃尔特·约翰逊说话。两位法官凑在一处定保金的数目，因为两人意见不合，商量了好几分钟。斯莫尔这时头一回觉得，该亲自出面走一着了。他倒不见得是怕拉尔夫脱罪。他许是对人家提到他名字的那个口气、对拉尔夫那意味深长的一眼，有点儿着忙；而多半是想给教书匠煽起足够的公愤，压下他那番话的分量，保准叫犯人吃私刑——动手的还多半是他那一伙以外的人。他站起来，用顶温柔的声调请法庭听他讲。他说他在这桩案子里没有一点私利，只有对老同学哈茨克先生福祉的关切。眼看对他不利的证据这么沉重，他又痛心又失望；他断断不肯往上添一根羽毛——若不是他的名字叫被告提了两回，又叫他那位朋友、或许还是同伙的约翰·皮尔逊提了两回。他可以宣誓作证：失盗那夜，十点钟以后他就不在弗拉特溪那边。方才提到的那两位的陈述，不管存心歹毒不歹毒，本来一根毫毛也牵连不到他身上；不过他想，他们陈述里这一点儿不老实，在断定别的几个关节上，分量许是不轻。因此他提议——他只能提议，因为他在本案里无论哪一头都不是当事——传他的学生沃尔特·约翰逊先生作证，证明他——斯莫尔大夫——在出事那夜的确切去向。他们俩在他诊室里一直待到两点，随后他上客栈睡了觉。

霍金斯治安官安顿好假牙、假发和玻璃眼睛，谢过斯莫尔大夫这一条宝贵的提议，觉得最好先把约翰·皮尔逊看押起来再往下进行。皮尔逊先生于是被看押了，只听他嘟囔了一句什么「一伙贼」，法庭便警告他安静。

接着传沃尔特·约翰逊。可在他作证之前，我得求读者耐着性子，容我回头交代将近一个礼拜以前的几件事，好叫这份证词明白易懂。

## V The Walk Home / 五 回家的路

===Original===
You expect me to describe that walk. You have had enough of the Jack Meanses and the Squire Hawkinses, and the Pete Joneses, and the rest. You wish me to tell you now of this truehearted girl and her lover; of how the silvery moonbeams came down in a shower—to use Whittier’s favorite metaphor—through the maple boughs, flecking the frozen ground with light and shadow. You would have me tell of the evening star, not yet gone down, which shed its benediction on them. But I shall do no such thing. For the moon was not shining, neither did the stars give their light. The tall, black trunks of the maples swayed and shook in the wind, which moaned through their leafless boughs. Novelists always make lovers walk in the moonlight. But if love is not, as the cynics believe, all moonshine, it can at least make its own light. Moonlight is never so little needed or heeded, never so much of an impertinence, as in a love-scene. It was at the bottom of the first hollow beyond the schoolhouse that Ralph overtook the timid girl walking swiftly through the dark. He did not ask permission to walk with her. Love does not go by words, and there are times when conventionality is impossible. There are people who understand one another at once. When one soul meets another, it is not by password, nor by hailing sign, nor by mysterious grip that they recognize. The subtlest freemasonry in the world is this freemasonry of the spirit.

===Chinese===
你等着我来描写那一段路。杰克·米恩斯们、霍金斯治安官们、皮特·琼斯们和其余诸公，你已经听够了。这会儿你要我讲讲这个真心的姑娘和她的恋人：讲那银白的月光怎样像一阵细雨——借惠蒂埃心爱的比喻——透过枫树枝倾泻下来，把冰冻的地面洒得光影斑驳。你要我讲那颗还没落下去的长庚星，怎样把祝福洒在他们身上。可我偏不这么写。因为月亮并没有照耀，星宿也不发光。枫树高大的黑躯干在风里摇晃，风在光秃秃的枝桠间呜咽。小说家总让恋人在月光下散步。可爱情即使不像愤世者信的那样全是月光泡影，它至少也能自己发光。在恋爱的场面里，月光从没有这么无关紧要、这么不知趣过。就在校舍过去第一道沟的沟底，拉尔夫追上了那个在黑暗里疾走的怯生生的姑娘。他没有请求陪她走。爱不走言语的路数，况且有些时候，客套根本不可能。有一种人，彼此一见就懂。一个灵魂遇见另一个灵魂，认出对方，不凭口令，不凭招呼的暗号，也不凭神秘的握法。天底下最精微的共济会，就是这心灵的共济会。

===Original===
Ralph and Hannah knew and trusted. Ralph had admired and wondered at the quiet drudge. But it was when, in the unaccustomed sunshine of praise, she spread her wings a little, that he loved her. He had seen her awake.

You, Miss Amelia, wish me to repeat all their love-talk. I am afraid you’d find it dull. Love can pipe through any kind of a reed. Ralph talked love to Hannah when he spoke of the weather, of the crops, of the spelling-school. Weather, crops, and spelling-school—these were what his words would say if reported. But below all these commonplaces there vibrated something else. One can make love a great deal better when one doesn’t speak of love. Words are so poor! Tones and modulations are better. It is an old story that Whitefield could make an audience weep by his way of pronouncing the word Mesopotamia. A lover can sound the whole gamut of his affection in saying Good morning. The solemnest engagements ever made have been without the intervention of speech.

===Chinese===
拉尔夫和汉娜彼此相知、彼此信任。拉尔夫早已对这个默默苦干的姑娘心怀赞叹与称奇。可是直到她在不习惯的赞美的阳光里稍稍舒展开翅膀，他才爱上了她。他看见她醒过来了。

你，阿米莉亚小姐，盼我把他们的情话一句句学给你听。只怕你会觉得乏味。爱情吹得起任何一种芦管。拉尔夫跟汉娜谈天气、谈收成、谈拼字学校的时候，谈的全是爱情。天气、收成、拼字学校——他的话要是原样记录下来，就是这么些。可这些平常话底下，震颤着别的东西。不说爱情的时候，倒最能谈情。言语就是这么贫乏！声调和抑扬要好得多。怀特菲尔德能凭他念「美索不达米亚」这个词的念法叫满场听众落泪，这是老话了。一个恋人道一声早安，就能把他全部爱情的音阶奏个遍。最庄严的婚约，从来不用言语从中撮合。

===Original===
And you, my Gradgrind friend, you think me sentimental. Two young fools they were, walking so slowly though the night was sharp, dallying under the trees, and dreaming of a heaven they could not have realized if all their wishes had been granted. Of course they were fools! Either they were fools to be so happy, or else some other people are fools not to be. After all, dear Gradgrind, let them be. There’s no harm in it. They’ll get trouble enough before morning. Let them enjoy the evening. I am not sure but these lovers whom we write down fools are the only wise people after all. Is it not wise to be happy? Let them alone.

For the first time in three years, for the first time since she had crossed the threshold of “Old Jack Means” and come under the domination of Mrs. Old Jack Means, Hannah talked cheerfully, almost gayly. It was something to have a companion to talk to. It was something to be the victor even in a spelling-match, and to be applauded even by Flat Creek. And so, chatting earnestly about the most uninteresting themes, Ralph courteously helped Hannah over the fence, and they took the usual shortcut through the “bluegrass pasture.” There came up a little shower, hardly more than a sprinkle, but then it was so nice to have a shower just as they reached the box-elder tree by the spring! It was so thoughtful in Ralph to suggest that the shade of a box-elder is dense, and that Hannah might take cold! And it was so easy for Hannah to yield to the suggestion! Just as though she had not milked the cows in the open lot in the worst storms of the last three years! And just as though the house were not within a stone’s-throw! Doubtless it was not prudent to stop here. But let us deal gently with them. Who would not stay in an earthly paradise ten minutes longer, even though it did make purgatory the hotter afterward? And so Hannah stayed.

===Chinese===
还有你，我的葛擂梗朋友，你觉得我多愁善感。两个小傻子，夜色那么清冷还走得那么慢，在树底下磨蹭，梦想一个哪怕一切愿望都遂了也实现不了的天堂。他们当然是傻子！要么他们傻在这么快活，要么另一些人傻在不肯这么快活。说到底，亲爱的葛擂梗，由他们去吧。这又没什么害处。不等天亮，他们的苦头有得吃呢。且让他们享用这个夜晚吧。我简直疑心，这些被我们记作傻子的恋人，到头来才是唯一的聪明人。快活难道不聪明么？别去管他们。

三年来头一回，打从跨进「老杰克·米恩斯」家的门槛、落到老杰克·米恩斯太太的管辖之下以来头一回，汉娜说起话来眉飞色舞，简直是快活了。有个伴儿可以说说话，总是一桩事。哪怕是拼字赛里当一回赢家、哪怕是让弗拉特溪的人鼓一回掌，也总是一桩事。于是，拉尔夫一边就着最没趣味的话题恳切地闲谈，一边客客气气地扶汉娜过栅栏，他们照例抄近路，穿过「蓝草牧场」。飘来一小阵雨，跟毛毛雨差不多少，可他们刚走到泉边那棵梣叶槭树底下，雨就来了，这有多好！拉尔夫多么体贴，提醒说梣叶槭树荫浓，汉娜会着凉的！而汉娜听从这提醒，又是多么顺理成章！仿佛这三年里顶大的风暴她没有在露天的圈里挤过奶似的！仿佛房子不就在扔一块石头就到的跟前似的！在这儿停下，当然欠审慎。可咱们待他们厚道些吧。谁不肯在人间的乐园里多待十分钟，哪怕事后炼狱因此烧得更旺？于是汉娜留下了。

===Original===
“Tell me your circumstances,” said Ralph, at last. “I am sure I can help you in something.”

“No, no! you cannot,” and Hannah’s face was clouded. “No one can help me. Only time and God. I must go, Mr. Hartsook.” And they walked on to the front gate in silence and in some constraint. But still in happiness.

As they came to the gate, Dr. Small pushed past them in his cool, deliberate way, and mounted his horse. Ralph bade Hannah good night, having entirely forgotten the errand which had been his excuse to himself for coming out of his way. He hastened to his new home, the house of Mr. Pete Jones, the same who believed in the inseparableness of “lickin’ and larnin’.”

===Chinese===
「把你的境况讲给我听听吧，」拉尔夫终于说。「我敢说，总有我能帮上一点的地方。」

「不，不！你帮不上，」汉娜的脸罩上了阴云。「谁也帮不上我。只有时间和上帝。我得进去了，哈特索克先生。」于是他们默默走到前门，彼此都有些拘束。但依然是快活的。

他们走到院门时，斯莫尔大夫用他那副冷冰冰、慢条斯理的做派从他们身边挤过去，上了马。拉尔夫向汉娜道了晚安——他原本拿「有事」当借口，才说服自己绕这一段路的，这会儿忘了个干净。他赶紧奔他的新住处、皮特·琼斯先生的家去了。这位琼斯先生，正是那位笃信「不打不学」、二者不可分的人。

===Original===
“You’re a purty gal, a’n’t you? You’re a purty gal, a’n’t you? *You* air! Yes, you *air*!!” and Mrs. Means seemed so impressed with Hannah’s prettiness that she choked on it, and could get no further. “A purty gal! you! Yes! you air a mighty purty gal!” and the old woman’s voice rose till it could have been heard half a mile. “To be a-santerin’ along the big road after ten o’clock with the master! Who knows whether he’s a fit man fer anybody to go with? Arter all I’ve been and gone and done fer you! That’s the way you pay me! Disgrace me! Yes, I say disgrace me! You’re a mean, deceitful thing. Stuck up bekase you spelt the master down. Ketch *me* lettin’ you got to spellin’-school to-morry night! Ketch **me**! Yes, ketch **me**, I say!”

===Chinese===
「你可是个俊丫头，是不是？你可是个俊丫头，是不是？*你*就是！对，你*就是*！！」米恩斯太太似乎让汉娜的俊美震住了，噎在这上头，说不出下文。「好个俊丫头！你！对！你可真是个了不得的俊丫头！」老太婆的嗓门越拔越高，半英里外都听得见。「十点多钟了，还跟教书先生在大路上晃荡！谁知道他是不是个正经人？俺为你操的心、受的累，全白搭了！你就这么报答俺！丢俺的脸！对，俺就说是丢俺的脸！你是个卑鄙、下作、当面一套背后一套的东西。拼字拼过了先生，尾巴就翘到天上去了。看*俺*明儿晚上还放你去拼字学校！看**俺**！对，看**俺**，俺说！」

===Original===
“Looky here, marm,” said Bud, “it seems to me you’re a-makin’ a blamed furss about nothin’. Don’t yell so’s they’ll hear you three or four mile. You’ll have everybody ’tween here and Clifty waked up.” For Mrs. Means had become so excited over the idea of being caught allowing Hannah to go to spelling-school that she had raised her last “Ketch me!” to a perfect whoop.

“That’s the way I’m treated,” whimpered the old woman, who knew how to take the “injured innocence” dodge as well as anybody. “That’s the way I’m treated. You allers take sides with that air hussy agin your own flesh and blood. You don’t keer how much trouble I have. Not you. Not a dog-on’d bit. I may be disgraced by that air ongrateful critter, and you set right here in my own house and sass me about it. A purty fellow you air! An’ me a-delvin’ and a-drudgin’ fer you all my born days. A purty son, a’n’t you?”

Bud did not say another word. He sat in the chimney-corner and whistled “Dandy Jim from Caroline.” His diversion had produced the effect he sought: for while his tenderhearted mother poured her broadside into his ironclad feelings, Hannah had slipped up the stairs to her garret bedroom, and when Mrs. Means turned from the callous Bud to finish her assault upon the sensitive girl, she could only gnash her teeth in disappointment.

===Chinese===
「你听着，娘，」巴德说，「照俺看，你这是没事瞎咋呼。别嚎了，嚎得三四英里外都听得见。你要把这儿到克利夫蒂沿路的人都吵醒了。」原来米恩斯太太一想到自己竟准了汉娜去拼字学校、兴许被人抓住话柄，激动得把最后那声「看俺！」拔成了十足的一声尖啸。

「俺就是这么被对待的，」老太婆抽抽搭搭地说。论装「受了委屈的无辜者」这一手，她的本事不输任何人。「俺就是这么被对待的。你们一个个都向着那个小贱蹄子，倒不向着俺这亲骨血。俺受多大的累，你们一点儿不放在心上。不，你们不。一丝一毫也不。俺兴许要叫那个没良心的畜生丢尽脸，你倒坐在俺自个儿的屋里，为这个跟俺犟嘴。你可真是个好样儿的！俺起早贪黑、当牛做马，为你们刨了一辈子食。你可真是个好儿子，是不是？」

巴德一个字也没再说。他坐在壁炉角里，吹起了《卡罗来纳的时髦吉姆》。他这一手转移，正收到了他要的效果：趁他那心肠慈悲的母亲把满腔炮火往他铁打的感情上倾泻，汉娜早已溜上楼梯，回她的阁楼卧室去了。等米恩斯太太从没心没肺的巴德那儿转过身来，要接着收拾那个敏感的姑娘时，只能气得干咬牙。

===Original===
Stung by the insults to which she could not grow insensible, Hannah lay awake until the memory of that walk through the darkness came into her soul like a benediction. The harsh voice of the scold died out, and the gentle and courteous voice of Hartsook filled her soul. She recalled piece by piece the whole conversation—all the commonplace remarks about the weather; all the insignificant remarks about the crops; all the unimportant words about the spelling-school. Not for the sake of the remarks. Not for the sake of the weather. Not for the sake of the crops. Not for the sake of the spelling-school. But for the sake of the undertone. And then she traveled back over the three years of her bondage and forward over the three years to come, and fed her heart on the dim hope of rebuilding in some form the home that had been so happy. And she prayed, with more faith than ever before, for deliverance. For love brings faith. Somewhere on in the sleepless night she stood at the window. The moon was shining now, and there was the path through the pasture, and there was the fence, and there was the box-elder.

She sat there a long time. Then she saw someone come over the fence and walk to the tree, and then on toward Pete Jones’s. Who could it be? She thought she recognized the figure. But she was chilled and shivering, and she crept back again into bed, and dreamed not of the uncertain days to come, but of the blessed days that were past—of a father and a mother and a brother in a happy home. But somehow the schoolmaster was there too.

===Chinese===
那些骂她的话像针一样扎人，她又无法麻木不仁，汉娜睁着眼躺在那儿，直到那趟穿过黑暗的散步像祝福一样进到她灵魂里。骂人的粗嗓门消散了，哈特索克那温存有礼的声音充满了她的心。她一段一段回想起整场谈话——所有关于天气的平常话；所有关于收成的不要紧话；所有关于拼字学校的不紧要字句。不是为了那些话。不是为了天气。不是为了收成。不是为了拼字学校。是为了那底下的低音。然后她回望过去三年的苦役，又前望将来要来的三年，拿一个渺茫的希望喂养自己的心：盼着有朝一日，以某种样子，把那个曾经那么幸福的家重新立起来。于是她祷告，比以往任何时候都更有信心地，求上帝搭救。因为爱情带来信心。不眠之夜的某一刻，她站在了窗前。月亮这会儿出来了，那条穿过牧场的路在那儿，那道栅栏在那儿，那棵梣叶槭也在那儿。

她在窗前坐了很久。随后她看见一个人翻过栅栏，走到树跟前，又朝皮特·琼斯家的方向去了。会是谁呢？她觉得认出了那个身影。可她身上发冷，抖个不停，便又爬回床上。她梦见的不是未卜的来日，而是过去那些有福的日子——一位父亲、一位母亲、一个哥哥，一个快活的家。只是不知怎么，那位教书先生也在梦里。
